# Session Log — 2026-08

Append-only. Newest entry at the bottom. One entry per session, written by
the chronicler BEFORE the session's final commit so the entry rides in it.
Format: `## <date> — <issue/trigger> — <outcome>`, then what was
attempted/shipped (commit hash), ratchet movement, decisions, surprises,
friction, next.

## 2026-08-01 — #344 — parser/xmltree: UTF-16 BOM-marked documents decode, with `decodeBOM`'s `Peek` error latched instead of dropped; the replan of #226 changed the three characters that twice failed review and nothing else in the transcoder, and the arbiter accepted on round 1

- Shipped on `wip/issue-344` as three commits on top of `61b0ad4` — `2cdae8e`
  (`parser: decode UTF-16 BOM-prefixed XML, latching decodeBOM's Peek error
  instead of dropping it (#344)`, 5 files, +822/−7), `af52bcd`
  (`conformance: bank schema +5 for UTF-16 BOM decoding (#344)`, 1 file,
  +5/−5, the arbiter's own ratchet write), and `fd70ec8` (`parser: repoint
  #344's GAP(xml) markers at #361 (#344)`, 2 files, +2/−2, the landing
  obligation the arbiter attached). Branch total 6 files, +827/−12:
  `parser/xmltree/encoding.go` (new, 320 lines),
  `parser/xmltree/reader.go`, `parser/xmltree/reader_test.go`,
  `parser/xmltree/doc.go`, `parser/document_test.go`,
  `conformance/testdata/expectations/schema.txt`. **Arbiter ACCEPT on the
  first round, no repair round consumed.**
- **Ratchet: schema lane +5 — 4307→4312 pass, 0 regressed, 0 vanished, all
  other lanes unchanged.** The arbiter's figures, copied not recomputed. The
  five upward flips, all `fail -> pass`:
  `assertion/d4_3_15ii29/schema/d4_3_15ii29s`,
  `assertion/d4_3_15ii30/schema/d4_3_15ii30s`,
  `constraintsOnAttribute/s3_2_3ii02/schema/s3_2_3ii02s`,
  `.../s3_2_3ii04/schema/s3_2_3ii04s`, `.../s3_2_3v04/schema/s3_2_3v04s`.
  `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` rewrote exactly five lines and touched only
  `expectations/schema.txt`.
- **The movement beat the issue's own forecast, and the extra flip is
  explained rather than tolerated.** #344's body forecast +4 — the four
  fixtures #226's diagnostic run had measured on the retired branch. A fifth,
  `assertion/d4_3_15ii30`, flipped too: an unforecast fixture of the same
  class, an `ibmData` schema document whose bytes begin `ff fe`, i.e. exactly
  the documents this diff teaches the reader to decode. The arbiter traced it
  before banking. **An under-count with every flip attributable to the diff is
  a different thing from an unexplained flip**, and only the second is a
  ratchet hazard.
- **What actually changed relative to the retired work is three characters
  and their consequences.** #226 was parked after two arbiter rejections that
  were the same finding at the same site both times — the dropped `_` in
  `prefix, _ := br.Peek(bomLen)` inside `decodeBOM`. `decodeBOM` now captures
  that error, and when it is non-nil and not `io.EOF` wraps the body in a new
  unexported `latchedErrReader`: it serves whatever bytes `Peek` had already
  buffered, then reports the latched error once the buffer drains, **without
  ever touching the underlying source again**. The mechanism note is in the
  code and is the load-bearing part —
  `bufio.Reader.Peek` takes its error from `readErr`, which does
  `err := b.err; b.err = nil`, so the peek is the *last* place that error is
  visible.
- **The regression this closes is a diagnostics regression against `main`, not
  a false-accept.** A source failing on its very first read — a broken disk, a
  reset connection — made `Token()` return bare `io.EOF`, which `reader.go`
  documents as a well-formed empty document, so the user got
  `[xml-wf] document has no root element` where the real cause was
  `[xml-wf] boom: original cause`. Two new regression tests pin it:
  `TestSourceFailureSurfacesItsCause` (xmltree, two arms — fails before a mark
  can be read, and fails after a mark is ruled out) and
  `TestReadDocumentSourceFailureSurfacesItsCause` (parser, end to end,
  asserting `errors.Is(err, cause)`, the `RuleXMLWellFormed` charge, the
  document URI on the `Loc`, and explicitly that the message does **not**
  contain "no root element").
- **Spec**: XML 1.0 §4.3.3 (`docs/specs/md/xml.md`) + non-normative Appendix
  F.1 for the mark byte sequences. The grounding was #226's, reused unchanged
  and not re-derived: a mark alone is sufficient evidence of an entity's
  encoding, a declaration that IS present must agree, and a mark/declaration
  conflict is a fatal well-formedness error → `xsderr.RuleXMLWellFormed`.
- **Two advisories left open when #226 parked were cleared in this landing.**
  (1) `srcChunk`'s doc comment claimed the constant "is even, so a full read
  never splits a code unit" — false, since the actual read length is
  `cap(buf) - base` and varies with the carry; it now states the real
  invariant, that a split code unit is preserved by `decode`'s carry of the
  leftover bytes and by no property of the chunk size. (2) The five
  map-ranging test tables that each needed a
  `// Deterministic iteration is irrelevant here` disclaimer became
  `[]struct{...}` slices, so the disclaimer is gone rather than justified —
  needing the same disclaimer five times was the signal (`80ef0c3` carried 6
  occurrences; HEAD carries 1, the pre-existing one on `main`).
- **Exported surface diff is empty.** `encoding.go` declares no exported
  identifier at all; `latchedErrReader` and its `Read` are unexported; the
  only new top-level identifiers in the branch are `Test*` functions and
  unexported test readers.
- **The arbiter did not take the carried-forward code on trust, and said so
  with experiments rather than assertions.** Three checks, all independent of
  mason's report: a **mutation check** — reverting the latch reproduced the
  exact round-2 symptom of #226, so the new tests are non-vacuous; an
  **adversarial probe of its own** — five prefix shapes against a source that
  panics if touched twice, confirming `latchedErrReader` never retries; and a
  **provenance diff** confirming the transcoder logic carried forward from
  `80ef0c3` is byte-identical except the four intended changes (the `Peek`
  latch, `latchedErrReader`, the `srcChunk` comment, the `#361` marker
  repoint).
- Surprises — **mason found a real bug in the carried-forward text that two
  rounds of review on #226 had not caught, and it was a genuine bug, not a
  garble introduced by the port.** `checkDeclaration`'s message read
  `"encoding declaration %q disagrees with the %s byte-order mark"` with
  `%s` = `bomEncoding`, whose `String()` returns *"a UTF-16 big-endian
  byte-order mark"* — so the literal carried-forward text would have rendered
  as "disagrees with the **a UTF-16 big-endian byte-order mark** byte-order
  mark". The message now reads "disagrees with the entity's actual encoding:
  %s", and `String()`'s doc comment says why it names the evidence rather than
  the mark. The general shape: **carrying code forward from a retired branch
  means re-reading it, not re-applying it** — a branch that was rejected twice
  on one site can still be wrong somewhere nobody looked, because review
  attention pooled where the rejection was.
- Friction — **effectively none for this session, and the one process note is
  the absence of a recurrence.** Mason's trailer on `2cdae8e` read
  `Ratchet: schema +4 (unbanked; arbiter applies)` — a prediction, marked as
  unbanked, naming the agent who can measure it. That is the third consecutive
  session without the `Ratchet: unchanged` failure recorded against #231/#232,
  and the first where the *unbanked* qualifier appears in the trailer itself.
  The forecast was low by one, but low-and-explained is the harmless
  direction. Worth carrying to the next `/retro` as evidence on whether the
  trailer fix enumerated in #232's entry is still needed.
- Next:
  1. **[for cartographer's post-land pass — arbiter advisory, NOT charged as
     repair-round work] The §4.3.3 disagreement message is now duplicated
     verbatim in two files.** `parser/xmltree/reader.go:154` (the
     `checkDeclaration` direction the Go decoder cannot see) and
     `parser/xmltree/encoding.go:79` (the `charsetReader` direction) carry the
     identical format string `encoding declaration %q disagrees with the
     entity's actual encoding: %s`, and **no test pins the full text** of
     either. Two copies of a string that just needed fixing once, with nothing
     asserting they agree. Not blocking, not fixed here.
  2. **[same batch, arbiter advisory]
     `TestUTF16StreamsAcrossFillBoundaries`'s "bulk reads" arm no longer
     crosses a fill boundary.** Its document is 4020 UTF-16 source bytes
     (2 BOM + 2×2009 code units) against `srcChunk` 4096, so a bulk reader
     fills once and the arm tests nothing its name claims. The "one byte per
     read" arm still exercises the carry, so the *test* is not vacuous — but
     half of it is decorative, and the arm will silently stay decorative if
     `srcChunk` ever grows. Fix is one number in the test, deliberately not
     made here.
  3. **[same batch, smallest of the three] A sixth
     `// Deterministic iteration is irrelevant here` disclaimer survives** at
     `parser/xmltree/reader_test.go:507` (`TestUnboundPrefixIsError`),
     pre-existing on `main` and outside this diff's scope. The five inside the
     diff were converted to slices; this one was left alone because reaching
     outside the diff to fix it is not this issue's remit. Harvestable as a
     one-line cleanup if a nearby issue touches that file.
  4. **Done, not pending: the `GAP(xml)` repoint.** Both markers
     (`encoding.go:64`, `doc.go:34`) said "Tracked by #344"; since this
     landing closes #344 they would have pointed at a closed issue, which
     STYLE P3 forbids and which #226 did before it was caught. The
     cartographer filed **#361** for the residual gap — BOM-less UTF-16
     declared only by `encoding=`, and legacy single-byte `EncName`s, both
     intentionally undecoded and fail-open, neither a bug — and `fd70ec8`
     repointed both markers at it. Recorded so the next `/backlog` does not
     re-file it.
- Thread record (do not duplicate) — <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/344>
  (replan of <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/226>, whose grounding

## 2026-08-01 — #331 — conformance: a seeded-but-unmapped lexical type stops being declined at a `backend.Mapping` miss and routes through `value.ValidateLexical`; the 48 non-list integer-family fixtures #224 deliberately left unclaimed are now decided, all 48 `New@pass`, arbiter ACCEPT on round 1

- Shipped on `wip/issue-331` as two commits on top of `2c70354` — `2e00511`
  (`conformance: route seeded-but-unmapped lexical types through
  value.ValidateLexical (#331)`, 2 files, +266/−74) and `053d652`
  (`conformance: bank the datatypes ratchet for the integer-family lexical
  claim (#331)`, 1 file, +48/−0, the arbiter's own machine-written ratchet
  write). Branch total 3 files, +314/−74: `conformance/datatypes.go`,
  `conformance/datatypes_test.go`,
  `conformance/testdata/expectations/datatypes.txt`. **Arbiter ACCEPT on the
  first round, no repair round consumed** — `FINDINGS: [none blocking]`, one
  workflow note (this LOG entry) and one non-blocking observation.
- **Ratchet: datatypes lane +48 — 1081 → 1129 lines, all 48 `New@pass`, 0
  Improved, 0 Regressed, 0 Vanished; instance lane unchanged at 26426, so no
  case moved between lanes.** The arbiter's figures, copied not recomputed.
  `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` produced `48 insertions(+), 0 deletions` and touched only
  `expectations/datatypes.txt`; no expectation was hand-edited. The 48 are
  `byte001–008`, `long001–008`, `short001–008`, and
  `unsignedByte/unsignedInt/unsignedLong/unsignedShort001–006` under
  `MS-DataTypes2006-07-15/…/instance/`.
- **The change is one routing predicate and one regex, and the routing is the
  point.** `execLexicalCase` resolved the tested type in the seeded symbol
  table and then demanded a **direct** `backend.Mapping` for that exact QName
  (`m, mapped := backend.Mapping(qn); if !mapped { return Fail() }`). The
  strict backend maps the 20 primitives only, so every derived builtin missed
  and the case recorded an honest decline. The escape hatch already existed
  one branch down — `fixesTimezone` → `decideLexicalByFacets` →
  `value.ValidateLexical`, live since #140 for `xs:dateTimeStamp` alone — and
  now fires on the general condition too: `if !mapped || fixesTimezone(st)`.
  `value.governingMapping` walks the base chain to the nearest mapped ancestor
  (`xs:decimal` for the whole integer family, the widest-space rule of
  `st-restrict-facets` §3.16.6.4) and the type's **own** effective facets
  decide the literal. `Fail()` is now reachable only for a type genuinely
  ungoverned all the way up its chain (`!mapped && !strictGoverns`), or for a
  context-dependent primitive the backend does not map.
- **Spec**: `cvc-datatype-valid` §4.1.4 is a conjunction, and that is the whole
  argument — clause 2.1 (`dv_atomic`) licenses membership in the **{primitive
  type definition}**'s lexical space, while clause 1 (`dv_pattern`) and clause
  3 (`dv_vfacets`) require the tested type's own pattern and value-based
  facets. `parseOK` against the governing mapping satisfies only clause 2.1, so
  it provably **false-accepts** `"128"` or `"1.5"` as an `xs:byte`. For this
  family the deciding facets are the fixed pattern `[\-+]?[0-9]+`
  (`cvc-pattern-valid` §4.3.4.4), `fractionDigits=0`
  (`cvc-fractionDigits-valid` §4.3.12.3) and the per-type bounds
  (`cvc-min/maxInclusive-valid` §4.3.10.3/§4.3.7.3), with the pattern gate
  running **before** the value facets — so `"5.0"` is a pattern rejection, not
  a fractionDigits one. `cos-applicable-facets` §4.1.5, §3.4.13–§3.4.25 for
  the family's derivation. No change to package `value`, no new backend
  mapping.
- **`fixesTimezone` stayed as an independent OR arm because the oracle proved
  the two conditions are independent, not because nobody tried to fold them.**
  The issue body itself asked for the new predicate to *subsume* `fixesTimezone`
  "if it can". It cannot: `explicitTimezone` appears only on the eight temporal
  primitives' `cos-applicable-facets` §4.1.5 rows, **all** of which are directly
  strict-mapped, while `xs:decimal`'s row has no `explicitTimezone` entry at
  all, so the integer family can never reach that arm. `xs:dateTimeStamp`
  satisfying both conditions is a coincidence, and the routing comment now says
  so in the code so a later reader does not "simplify" one away. The arbiter
  did not take this on trust: reverting the `!mapped ||` arm leaves
  `TestDatatypesLexicalDateTimeStampTimezone` passing while
  `TestDatatypesLexicalIntegerFamily` fails, so neither arm subsumes the other
  behaviourally either.
- **A second selector was retired, not left as a subset (STYLE D3).** #224's
  dedicated `integerListCase` matched exactly seven list-variety fixtures
  (`byte009`, `long009`, `short009`, `unsignedByte/Int/Long/Short007`) that the
  widened `datatypesCase` alternation now also matches, and `selectsDatatypes`
  ORs its disjuncts, so the claim set is identical. The arbiter checked both
  that it is a strict subset **and** that dispatch is unaffected — `datatypesCase`
  is referenced only from `selectsDatatypes`, never from `newDatatypesExec`'s
  dispatch chain, so the seven still read as the user-defined `myList`, still
  miss `sym`, and still take `execListCase`. Confirmed empirically by their
  expectation lines being untouched: the ratchet diff is 48 insertions, **0
  modifications**.
- **The selector is pinned from both sides, and the tests were shown to be
  able to fail.** `TestDatatypesSelectorClaimsOnlyCohort` flips `byte008` and
  `unsignedShort006` from NOT-claimed to claimed — #224 planted those two
  negative rows precisely so a later widening "has to argue with a test rather
  than just edit a regex" — and gains three fresh negative rows
  (`Facets/int/test111092.xml`'s multi-element shape, plus `int001.xml` and
  `integer001.xml`). New `TestDatatypesLexicalIntegerFamily` drives 14 fixtures
  through the real executor in both polarities and pins the routing premises
  (`xs:byte` seeded, unmapped, governed, not `fixesTimezone`) and the rejection
  rule IDs. Mutation check, in a detached worktree: reverting the routing to
  `main`'s form makes all 14 report as disagreements; reverting only the OR arm
  panics on a zero `Mapping` in `parseOK`, independently confirming the two
  edits are coupled and load-bearing.
- **Exported surface diff is empty** — `go doc ./conformance` byte-identical
  before and after; package `conformance` is a test harness with no published
  surface, and the only new top-level identifier in the diff is
  `TestDatatypesLexicalIntegerFamily`.
- Surprises:
  1. **The issue anticipated "a mix of `New@pass` and `New@fail`" and got 48
     out of 48 pass — the stronger outcome, and explained rather than
     celebrated.** Every out-of-range fixture is suite-declared **invalid**
     (`.i`) and the facet path rejects it for the *right* rule, so agreement is
     what all-pass means here. The arbiter spot-checked literals beyond the 14
     the new test pins — `byte003` `"0"` `.v`,
     `unsignedByte/unsignedInt/unsignedLong002` `"-1"` `.i` (minInclusive 0),
     `long006` `"-9223372036854775809 "` `.i` (below minInclusive after
     whiteSpace collapse), `short001`/`unsignedShort001` `""` `.i` (fixed
     pattern). **No goxsd8-vs-suite disagreement anywhere in the 48, so no #149
     dismissal is owed** — recorded explicitly so a later reader does not go
     looking for the missing dismissal note.
  2. **The issue body carried two wrong section numbers and the grounding
     caught both before a line was written.** `cvc-fractionDigits-valid` is
     §4.3.12.3, not §4.3.12.4 (which is the COSS "Constraints on
     fractionDigits Schema Components", not the Validation Rule);
     `cvc-minInclusive-valid` is §4.3.10.3, not §4.3.9.3 (which is
     *minExclusive*'s rule — this repo's numbering runs maxInclusive §4.3.7,
     maxExclusive §4.3.8, minExclusive §4.3.9, minInclusive §4.3.10). Mason
     used the corrected numbers; the arbiter re-verified every cited section
     against the local spec headings rather than against the grounding. This is
     the **second consecutive session** in which the oracle's grounding
     corrected a rule-ID error that originated upstream of it (#232's entry
     records the inverse case, where the grounding itself carried the wrong
     clause number) — worth carrying to the next `/retro` as evidence about
     where rule IDs get garbled, since #331's came from the *issue body*, i.e.
     the cartographer's entry point.
- Friction — **none charged against the change, and the one process note is a
  role boundary working as designed.** The arbiter's verdict listed
  `[workflow, pre-land] docs/LOG/2026-08.md — branch carries no LOG entry`.
  That is correct *and* expected: mason must never write `docs/LOG` (the whole
  point of #195), so the absence is the chronicler's cue, not a mason defect,
  and the arbiter classified it that way — "must be appended before the PR, not
  a defect in the change under judgment". Recorded so a future `/retro` reading
  a run of verdicts does not mistake this recurring line for recurring friction.
  Separately: mason's `Ratchet:` trailer on `2e00511` predicted "datatypes lane
  1081 -> 1129 cases, 48 new decisions, all New@pass, 0 regressions and 0
  vanished (non-ratchet run; the arbiter banks the actual move)" and the
  arbiter's `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` run confirmed it **exactly**, qualifier and all.
  That is now three consecutive sessions (#235 exact, #344 low-by-one and
  explained, #331 exact) since the #231/#232 pattern of false `Ratchet:
  unchanged` trailers. The structural blind spot itself is unchanged — a
  read-only conformance PASS still cannot distinguish +0 from +N — but the
  evidence is accumulating that the trailer is being written honestly, and the
  next `/retro` should decide whether the fix enumerated in #232's entry is
  still worth filing or should be dismissed on the record.
- Next:
  1. **[to file] `int001–008` and `integer001–012` are the same shape and the
     same one-line widening, deliberately left unclaimed.** Twenty more
     `msData/datatypes/` lexical fixtures with the identical `comp_foo`/
     `simpleTest` form that would ride the exact route this issue built, needing
     only two more alternatives in `datatypesCase`. Mason scoped them out
     because #331 enumerated exactly 48 and the arbiter upheld that: "silently
     widening past that would have made my ratchet number un-attributable to the
     issue." Documented in the file's prose (`conformance/datatypes.go`, the
     cohort comment) rather than as a `GAP(` marker, matching that file's
     convention — it carries no `GAP(` markers at all. Worth an issue on the
     cartographer's post-land pass; **note the ratchet consequence**: unlike the
     48, these have no forecast, so the mix of `New@pass`/`New@fail` the issue
     body expected here may actually materialise there.
  2. **[arbiter observation, non-blocking, NOT charged as repair work]
     `conformance/datatypes.go:796`'s `!mapped && !strictGoverns(backend, st)`
     is logically equivalent to `!strictGoverns(backend, st)` alone.** `sym` is
     keyed by `t.Name()`, so `st.Name() == qn`, and `strictGoverns` tests `st`
     itself on its first loop iteration — `mapped` therefore implies
     `strictGoverns`. The extra conjunct is a short-circuit, not duplicated
     state, so it is **not** a D3 violation; the arbiter recorded it only so a
     later reader does not mistake it for two independent facts. Harvestable as
     a one-line simplification if a nearby issue touches that function; not
     worth an issue of its own.
  3. **Done, not pending: the in-code deferral pointer from #224 is retired.**
     `conformance/datatypes.go`'s old prose — "Routing a seeded-but-unmapped
     lexical type through `value.ValidateLexical` … is what would make them
     decidable — the natural follow-up, not part of #224" — was replaced by the
     implemented reality, and what remains deferred now names #331. Recorded so
     the next `/backlog` does not re-file it.
- Thread record (do not duplicate) —
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/331>: the oracle GROUNDING at
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/331#issuecomment-5148754116>
  (the `cvc-datatype-valid` conjunction quoted verbatim, the
  `fixesTimezone`-independence finding, and the two corrected section numbers)
  and the arbiter's ACCEPT verdict at
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/331#issuecomment-5148826138>
  (gate transcript, the `integerListCase`-retirement check, the mutation

  This entry is WORKFLOW's "Land" step, written on the WIP branch before the PR

## 2026-08-01 — #337 — parser: `symbols.simpleTypes` gains an owning producer, so a chameleon-included document's own unqualified `base=` resolves under the DECLARING document; #228's `complexSource` was generalized rather than twinned, and the arbiter accepted on round 1

- Shipped on `wip/issue-337` as one mason commit — `c707cfb`
  (`parser: give symbols.simpleTypes an owning producer (#337)`, 2 files,
  +136/−26: `parser/produce.go`, `parser/parse_test.go`). **Arbiter ACCEPT on
  round 1, no repair round consumed.**
- **Ratchet: unchanged** — the arbiter's figures, copied not recomputed.
  `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` ran and wrote nothing; the expectations files are
  byte-identical before and after (md5-verified by the arbiter). Lanes:
  schema 4312/15432, datatypes 1107/1129, instance 0/26426, all at the
  pre-#337 baseline. **No W3C fixture happens to combine chameleon inclusion
  with an unqualified simple-type `base=`**, so this is a false-reject fix
  guarded by its own unit test, not a lane mover — the issue expected exactly
  this.
- **Spec**: §F.1 task (b) (chameleon coercion); `src-resolve` §3.17.6.2
  clauses 1.1 and 4.1.1; §3.16.2.1 (simpleType `{base type definition}`);
  §3.4.2.3.1 (the complexType twin); §4.2.3 `c-incl-incl`/`c-incl-ns-capture`;
  §4.2.5 (override ordering).
- **The defect.** `symbols.simpleTypes` held a bare `*Element` with no owning
  producer, so an on-demand `buildSimpleType` triggered from a foreign or
  referring document resolved that type's unqualified `base=` under the
  **asking** producer. The asker's `unqualifiedRefNS`/`declares` are always
  false for a foreign declaring element, so a chameleon-included document's own
  unqualified `base=` took the absent namespace instead of §F.1's coerced
  target namespace — a false reject, and exactly the class #228 had already
  fixed one index over for `symbols.complexTypes`.
- **The grounding's load-bearing finding is that resolution authority does not
  travel with the request.** The oracle established that §F.1 coercion updates
  unqualified QName references (`xs:*/@base` included) in the DECLARING
  document, coercing to that document's own target namespace — a property of
  the document holding the attribute, not of whoever asks for the build.
  §4.2.3's `c-incl-incl`/`c-incl-ns-capture` explain why a foreign producer can
  legitimately trigger the build (assembly-wide visibility) but transfer no
  resolution authority; `src-resolve` clause 4.1.1 scopes its absent-namespace
  default to "the schema document containing the QName". And §3.16.2.1's
  simpleType `{base type definition}` mapping is **identical in mechanism** to
  complexType's §3.4.2.3.1, so the #228 pattern applies mechanically rather
  than by analogy.
- **The design decision: one `typeSource`, not two parallel source structs.**
  `complexSource{elem, owner}` was generalized into `typeSource{elem, owner}`
  — same shape, one encoding (STYLE T4) — and `symbols.simpleTypes` became
  `map[xsd.QName]typeSource`. `prescan` populates it with
  `typeSource{elem: decl, owner: p}`; both on-demand build call sites route
  through `src.owner.buildSimpleType` (`resolveBaseType`, `produce.go:494`;
  `resolveBase`, `produce.go:589`). The memo stays assembly-wide, so component
  identity is untouched.
- **The stale doc comment was deleted, not amended.** `symbols` had claimed
  "simpleTypes needs no owner because a `<simpleType>` body holds no local
  declaration whose namespace could differ". True as far as it went and wrong
  in effect: `base=` is itself an unqualified reference subject to §F.1, which
  the comment overlooked. The replacement states the declaring-document rule
  directly, citing §3.16.2.1, clause 4.1.1 and §F.1.
- **The `<override>` edge case had to survive the fix, and did.** §4.2.5's
  override ordering means an element substituted in by `<override>` from
  another document is NOT subject to that document's own §F.1 coercion. The
  existing `declares`/`unqualifiedRefNS` logic already encodes this; routing
  the build through the owner had to preserve it rather than paper over it.
- **The arbiter reproduced the defect rather than trusting the test.** It
  dropped `TestParseSimpleTypeBaseBuiltUnderItsOwnProducer` onto unmodified
  `origin/main` in a scratch worktree and confirmed it fails on the
  "referrer produced before declarer" order with exactly
  `[src-resolve] base type Root does not resolve to any simple type in scope
  (src-resolve clause 1.1)`, and passes on the control order — the test is
  load-bearing, not vacuous. It also verified **call-site completeness** (only
  three readers of `symbols.simpleTypes`; both on-demand ones converted,
  `prescan`/`run` correctly left on `p`) and the **identity premise by
  construction** (`parse.go` shares one `*symbols` pointer across all
  producers). Exported-surface diff empty, confirmed by the arbiter's own
  `go doc -all` byte-diff across all 11 packages (T5/T8). Full gate green.
- The new test (`parser/parse_test.go`, modeled on #228's
  `TestParseExtensionBaseBuiltUnderItsOwnProducer`) forces **both** document
  processing orders and asserts base-chain resolution plus component pointer
  identity, so a future regression cannot hide behind whichever order happens
  to run first.
- Friction — **none charged against the change.** One-commit branch, one
  round, no gate churn. The only process note is the follow-up in the next
  bullet, which the arbiter explicitly said belongs in an issue rather than
  buried in the #337 thread.
- Next:
  1. **[to file — cartographer's post-land pass] `symbols.attributeGroups` has
     the identical no-owner shape.** `collectReferencedGroup`
     (`parser/produce_complex.go:1328`) resolves a possibly-foreign
     `<attributeGroup>` definition under the asking producer: same false-reject
     class, one index over, and the third instance of the pattern after #228
     and #337. Deliberately out of scope here (the issue's own Notes said so)
     and flagged by the arbiter as needing a **standalone issue**, not a
     thread comment.
  2. **Known-unreachable today, so deliberately not fixed: `<list itemType>`
     and `<union memberTypes>` carry the same unqualified-QName exposure per
     §F.1's XSLT stylesheet.** There is no path to trigger it yet —
     `restrictionOf` rejects a `<simpleType>` without a `<restriction>` child —
     but whenever those constructs land they need the same owner routing.
     Recorded here so the landing of list/union does not have to rediscover it.
- Thread record (do not duplicate) —
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/337>: the oracle's grounding (§F.1
  task (b) quoted, the clause-4.1.1 scoping argument, the §3.16.2.1/§3.4.2.3.1
  mechanism identity, and the §4.2.5 override carve-out) and the arbiter's
  ACCEPT verdict (the `origin/main` reproduction transcript, the call-site
  completeness check, the exported-surface diff, and the md5-verified flat
  ratchet). Prior art referenced and not re-derived: #228 (`complexSource`,
  the fix this one generalizes) and #195 (no `docs/LOG` in mason commits —
  `c707cfb` is clean on that concern). This entry is WORKFLOW's "Land" step,
  written on the WIP branch before the PR is opened so it rides in the squash.

## 2026-08-01 — #236 — xsd: `au-props-correct` clause 3's `{value}`-identity conjunct lands for both attribute-declaration variants and the two `loc-testSubP` `GAP(xsd)` markers close, over a new `xsd.ValueSpace` seam implemented in `value`; the warden split clause 2 out at pre-flight, and the +4 schema flips are all `loc-testSubP`, none of them clause 3

- Shipped on `wip/issue-236` as three commits on top of `4f6df32` — `0625fd3`
  (`xsd: au-props-correct clause 3 + close loc-testSubP 4.2/5.2.2 GAP markers
  (#236)`, 15 files, +1419/−76), `8f8df86` (`xsd: repair round — warden diff
  findings on #236`, 5 files, +86/−16), and `74be0a1` (`conformance: ratchet up
  from #236`, the arbiter's own ratchet write). Branch total 17 files,
  +1498/−85. New files: `xsd/valuespace.go`, `xsd/valueconstraintvalid.go`
  (+ test), `value/valuespace.go` (+ test). **Arbiter ACCEPT after one repair
  round.**
- **Ratchet: schema lane +4 — 4312→4316 pass, 0 regressed, 0 vanished, all
  other lanes unchanged.** The arbiter's figures, copied not recomputed. The
  four upward flips, all `fail -> pass`:
  `MS-Attribute2006-07-15/attZ008_e` (a restriction re-fixes `@att`
  `"not_fixed"`→`"fixed"`; `loc-testSubP` cl.5.2.2 now decides and rejects) and
  `MS-Particles2006-07-15/particlesIf007/008/009` (a restriction re-fixes an
  element declaration's `fixed=`; cl.4.2 now decides and rejects).
- **Attribution correction from the arbiter, worth more than the number: all
  four flips are `loc-testSubP` (the two closed `GAP(xsd)` markers).
  `au-props-correct` clause 3 / Phase E contributed exactly ZERO flips.** No
  W3C suite case exercises an attribute *use* whose own fixed `{value
  constraint}` disagrees with its declaration's. That matches the issue's own
  acceptance text, so it is not a defect — but **Phase E rests entirely on its
  unit tests**, and a later session must not read the +4 as suite coverage of
  clause 3.
- **Spec**: `au-props-correct` cl.3 (§3.5.6); `loc-testSubP` cl.4.2 and
  cl.5.2/5.2.2 (§3.4.6.4); `key-evc` (§3.5.4); `derivation-ok-restriction` cl.3
  (§3.4.6.3); Datatypes §2.2.1/§2.2.2 (identity vs. equality). Deferred, not
  touched: `cos-valid-simple-default` (§3.2.6.2).
- **The grounding's load-bearing finding: "identical" and "equal or identical"
  in Part 1 are VALUE-space relations, never lexical.** `{value}`'s type is an
  ·actual value·, not the sibling `{lexical form}`; §3.2's gloss says "it is
  *values* that are checked, not strings"; Part 2 §2.2.2 says sameness checks
  "test for either equality or identity, not for identity alone". `"1"` vs
  `"01"` under `xs:integer` must compare same — a lexical compare is exactly
  the fail-open the two `GAP(xsd)` markers described. The oracle also fixed the
  dependency direction: `xsd` is a pure leaf that must not import `value`
  (PRINCIPLES 1), so the comparison belongs at the layer above.
- **The warden's pre-flight rewrote the surface three ways.** (a) The sketch's
  3-outcome `(v ActualValue, ok, err)` return and opaque `ActualValue` token
  were dropped — `xsd` never inspects such a token, it only hands it back, so
  it had no independent purpose (T1/T5). Collapsed to a two-method
  `ValueSpace` (`Identical`, `EqualOrIdentical`), each taking BOTH sides'
  `*SimpleType` + `ValueConstraint`. (b) `EqualOrIdentical` alone cannot
  express clause 3 — the spec says "identical", not "equal or identical", and
  one union predicate would silently accept `+0` vs `-0`. (c) The proposed
  mutable `SetValueSpace` setter became a non-nilable
  `FinalizeWith(vs ValueSpace) (*Schema, error)` sibling entry point — a setter
  lets one builder finalize twice with different verdicts, and `nil` would mean
  both "never set" and "explicitly disabled" (T1/D4).
- **The widest-space / cross-type hazard is resolved inside `value`, never in
  `xsd`** — the warden's never-false-reject condition. In `loc-testSubP`
  5.2.2/4.2 the two constraints belong to different types; parsing each under
  its own governing mapping independently would make cross-type comparison
  return `decided=true, same=false` and reject a valid schema. `value`'s
  `sharedMapping` resolves both sides to a governing type node and returns
  undecided whenever the two differ (pointer identity — a false negative on
  comparability is always safe, a false positive is impossible).
- **The scope split is the process decision of this session.** The warden ruled
  `au-props-correct` clause 2 (`cos-valid-simple-default`) OUT of #236:
  clause 2 is a new rejection surface touching every `default=`/`fixed=` in the
  suite, whereas clause 3 + the GAP closures only narrow already-`fixed`/`fixed`
  comparisons. Bundling them would have made any unexpected lane movement
  unattributable — against the one rule that outranks everything. The arbiter
  verified the split actually held: no clause-2 code path exists, the deferral
  is prose only.
- **Repair round — three warden findings, all documentation-or-duplication, no
  redesign.** (a) `value.Identical`'s godoc scoped its `Eq` fallback to
  "enumeration purposes", but `identical()` now uses that same fallback for
  strict identity on a path ending in schema rejection; a future value type
  whose identity is *wider* than its equality and implements only `Eq` would
  false-reject. The doc now states the obligation generally (a type whose
  identity differs from its equality MUST implement `Identical`). Not a live
  bug — today's `builtin/strict` cohort all implement `Identical` — but the
  extension contract had a hole. (b) Anonymous inline complex types rendered an
  empty QName in Phase E error messages (`complex type  gives attribute …`);
  fixed via `complexTypeOwner` and pinned by
  `TestPhaseEOwnerNamesAnonymousComplexType`. (c) `governingType` duplicated
  `governingMapping`'s base-chain walk — extracted to one `governingNode`
  (T4).
- **The `Identical`-else-`Eq` fallback was explicitly ruled sound, not a
  collapse.** `identical()` dispatches on capability presence and never ORs the
  two relations; only `equalOrIdentical` does. `decimalVal`/`stringVal`/
  `hexBinaryVal`/`base64BinaryVal`/`anyURIVal` implement `Eq` only, so an
  `Identical`-only reading would have left clause 3 undecided for nearly the
  whole numeric/string cohort. Pinned by
  `TestIdentityIsNotTheEqualOrIdenticalUnion`.
- **Phase E walks one site MORE than Phase A, deliberately.** It mirrors Phase
  A's descent (types, elements, model groups, including inline anonymous
  complex types under elements) and adds `s.attributeGroups`, which Phase A
  skips. Documented as an intentional extension with its consequence: an
  unresolvable `<attribute ref>` inside a group is skipped rather than charged
  `src-resolve`, which is fail-open.
- **The arbiter checked the warden's findings in the tree rather than on
  report**, mutation-tested five code paths (each mutation fails at least one
  test; no test passes with its change reverted), and byte-diffed `go doc -all`
  against a worktree at `origin/main`: exactly three new exports —
  `xsd.ValueSpace`, `(*xsd.SchemaBuilder).FinalizeWith`, `value.NewValueSpace`
  — each with a live non-test consumer (`parser/produce.go:96`,
  `parser/parse.go:554`). Full STYLE pass clean.
- Friction — **none charged against the change**; one round, no gate churn. Two
  observations worth carrying:
  1. The `GAP(xsd)` markers were **narrowed, not deleted** (P3), and now
     enumerate the exact residual. The debt-sweep token stays greppable.
  2. Phase E's zero lane coverage (above) means the clause-3 path has no
     conformance backstop at all. Not an action item — the suite simply has no
     such case — but if Phase E ever regresses, only the unit tests will say so.
- Next:
  1. **[to file — cartographer's post-land pass] `cos-valid-simple-default` /
     `au-props-correct` clause 2 (and the `a-props-correct` clause 2 half).**
     Deferred by the warden's pre-flight scope-split ruling, not by lack of
     grounding — the oracle sourced the rule text and the warden already
     designed its landing (`checkSimpleDefault` shared across both call sites,
     T4, shipping with the `ValidDefault` method that consumes it). Needs a
     **standalone issue**.
  2. **[to file — cartographer's post-land pass] QName/NOTATION value-space
     comparison**, deferred under the new `GAP(value):` marker in
     `value/valuespace.go`. Their lexical mapping resolves a prefix against the
     namespace bindings in scope AT THE LITERAL (§3.3.18/§3.3.19, PRINCIPLES
     19), and `xsd.ValueConstraint` carries no such context — it is a
     `{variety}` and a `{lexical form}` and nothing else. Closing it is a
     producer-side change: the value constraint must retain its writing
     document's in-scope bindings. Refusing is fail-open today. The marker
     names no owner issue, so this line is the only tracking record until one
     is filed.
- Thread record (do not duplicate) —
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/236>: the oracle's grounding (the
  four rule texts verbatim with file:line, the three-part value-space argument,
  and the float/dateTime identity-vs-equality traps), the warden's pre-flight
  (ten findings, including the SPLIT ruling as finding 8) and diff review
  (three blocking changes plus the non-blocking ruling on the `Eq` fallback),
  and the arbiter's ACCEPT (the flip-by-flip ratchet explanation, the
  attribution correction, the mutation transcript, and the `go doc -all`
  surface diff). Prior art referenced and not re-derived: #70 (which deferred
  both clauses here), #173 (the resolve phase Phase E mirrors), #235 (the
  producer that makes Use-level value constraints observable at all), #265
  (still open, named by the narrowed `GAP(xsd)` text). This entry is WORKFLOW's
  "Land" step, written on the WIP branch before the PR is opened so it rides in
  the squash.

## 2026-08-01 — #309 — conformance: a missing `testdata/xsdtests` stops being a 6ms `t.Skipf` and becomes a hard failure naming the init command, taken BEFORE the ratchet branch so `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` over an empty suite can never report "no movement"; the `GOXSD_SUITE_OPTIONAL=1` opt-out covers read-only runs only, and the arbiter accepted on round 1

- Shipped on `wip/issue-309` as one commit on top of `134290e` — `6d522b2`
  (`conformance: fail TestConformance when the W3C submodule is absent
  (#309)`), 4 files, +99/−11: `conformance/runner.go`,
  `conformance/conformance_test.go`, `conformance/doc.go`,
  `conformance/runner_test.go`. No new files. **Arbiter ACCEPT on the first
  round, no repair round consumed.**
- **Ratchet: unchanged** — and the arbiter confirmed that rather than taking it
  on report. The diff touches only the four `conformance/*.go` files above: no
  lane executor, no selector, no expectations file. With the submodule
  initialized at `7bc3365` the read-only run passes **41858 cases across 6
  lanes**, and a full `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` run passes with **all 7 expectation
  files md5-identical before and after** — a genuine "unchanged" over a
  non-empty suite, which is precisely the thing this issue existed to make
  distinguishable from an unearned one.
- **Spec: n/a — process/tooling, no XSD rule.** The grounding comment says so
  explicitly; no rule ID was cited and none should have been.
- Gate: build/test/vet/lint clean.
- **The defect, stated exactly**: a container without the submodule ran
  `TestConformance` to `ok` in ~6ms via `t.Skipf`, and because the old check sat
  BEFORE nothing in particular, a `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` invocation over zero cases
  produced the same "no movement" text a real unchanged run produces. The fix
  reorders one line — `ratcheting` is now read first — so the suite-presence
  decision can see which run mode it is ending.
- **The policy is three-way, and the arbiter proved all four env combinations**
  (none / `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` / `GOXSD_SUITE_OPTIONAL=1` / both) against a
  submodule-absent tree, matching the exit codes and messages the issue
  required. Default: hard fail. `GOXSD_SUITE_OPTIONAL=1` alone: skip.
  `GOXSD_SUITE_OPTIONAL=1` + `GOXSD_RATCHET=1`: still a hard fail. **The opt-out
  demonstrably never covers a ratchet run** — that is the property the one rule
  that outranks everything depends on, and it is now executable rather than
  asserted.
- **The opt-out exists because agent containers legitimately run `go test
  ./...` without the submodule** — refusing to have one would have made the
  whole suite un-runnable in the ordinary session environment. It is deliberate
  and must be typed, so silence is never the default; that asymmetry (opt-in to
  quiet, never opt-in to loud) is the whole design.
- **Structural decision: the check became pure.** `checkSuitePresent(index
  string) error` in `runner.go` decides presence and names the remedy; the test
  file's `endUnusableSuiteRun(t, err, ratcheting)` decides what to DO about it.
  The pure half is unit-testable without a submodule, which is why
  `TestCheckSuitePresent` holds in any checkout. Mutation-tested by the arbiter:
  it kills both a `return nil` mutant and a message mutant.
- **One fact, one encoding (STYLE D3): `suiteModulePath = "testdata/xsdtests"`
  is now the fact, and `suiteRoot = "../" + suiteModulePath` is derived.** The
  old single `suiteRoot` constant was the reason the old skip message printed
  `git submodule update --init ../testdata/xsdtests` — a command that does not
  run from the module root. The message now quotes the path exactly as it is
  typed.
- **The 15 sibling `t.Skipf` sites (13 in `datatypes_test.go`, 2 in
  `schema_test.go`) were deliberately LEFT as unconditional skips**, and the
  reasoning is recorded in `conformance/doc.go` rather than in a commit message
  alone. Each drives a named cohort to prove one executor decides it for the
  right reason; skipping them loses unit coverage but cannot dress an empty run
  up as suite-wide conformance. `TestConformance` was the only test whose
  silence was mistakable for a score. Scoping this out kept the diff to the one
  behaviour under judgement.
- **Exported surface: empty diff.** All new identifiers (`suiteModulePath`,
  `suiteOptionalEnv`, `checkSuitePresent`, `endUnusableSuiteRun`) are
  unexported; the package is test-only. `go doc -all ./conformance` differs from
  `main` by exactly one new doc line.
- Friction — **none charged against the change**; one round, no gate churn.
  Worth carrying: the submodule-present verification is the one path mason could
  not run itself (the implementing container had no suite), so the whole
  "unchanged over 41858 cases" half of the evidence came from the arbiter's
  environment. That division worked here, but it means an issue of this shape is
  structurally un-self-verifiable by its implementer.
- Next — three non-blocking arbiter findings, recorded here for the
  cartographer's post-land pass to harvest; **none were filed by this session**:
  1. **The 15 sibling skip messages still interpolate `suiteRoot`**, so they
     print `git submodule update --init ../testdata/xsdtests` — not runnable
     from the module root. Pre-existing on `main`, but this diff introduced the
     correct `suiteModulePath` constant without applying it at those sites: two
     encodings of one message, one of them wrong (STYLE D3 again).
  2. **`conformance_test.go`'s call to `endUnusableSuiteRun(...)` has no
     `return` after it.** Correct today only because every branch of the helper
     reaches `t.Fatalf`/`t.Skipf`; a later edit to `t.Errorf` would silently fall
     through into `parseSuite`. Correctness resting on a callee's control flow
     rather than the caller's.
  3. **`endUnusableSuiteRun`'s policy branching is not unit-tested** — only the
     pure `checkSuitePresent` gate is. The arbiter verified the fatal/skip
     matrix by hand across the four env combos; nothing in the tree re-checks it
     on the next change.
- Thread record (do not duplicate) —
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/309>: the GROUNDING comment
  (<https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/309#issuecomment-5151370971>,
  n/a spec — process/tooling) and the arbiter's ACCEPT verdict
  (<https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/309#issuecomment-5151401032>, the
  four-combo env matrix, the submodule-present run at `7bc3365`, the md5
  identity of all 7 expectation files, the exported-surface diff, and the three
  non-blocking findings above). This entry is WORKFLOW's "Land" step, written on
  the WIP branch before the PR is opened so it rides in the squash.

## 2026-08-01 — #238 — conformance: a multi-document `schemaTest` stops being silently collapsed to its LAST `<schemaDocument>` by `encoding/xml`, and the harness declines the set it cannot compose instead of deciding an arbitrary member of it; the fix exposed 69 wrong-reason schema-lane passes, corrected downward by the arbiter under the issue's own authorization

- Shipped on `wip/issue-238` as three commits on top of `28b37fa` — `1408819`
  (`conformance: fix schemaTest multi-document loss + decline-not-guess extra
  docs (#238)`, mason, 5 files, +334/−20), `9a345f2` (`conformance: arbiter
  downward correction of 69 wrong-reason schema passes (#238)`, the arbiter's own
  machine-written ratchet write, 1 file, 69 lines flipped `pass`→`fail`), and
  `ae1a500` (`conformance: correct #238 comment claims about extra-doc
  reachability mechanism`, the round-1 repair, comment-only, +15/−6). Branch
  total 6 files, +412/−89: `conformance/runner.go`, `conformance/runner_test.go`,
  `conformance/schema.go`, `conformance/schema_closure.go`,
  `conformance/schema_closure_test.go`,
  `conformance/testdata/expectations/schema.txt`. No new files. **REJECT on
  round 1, ACCEPT on round 2 — one repair round consumed of the two allowed.**
- **Ratchet: schema lane 4316/15432 → 4247/15432 (−69), an authorized downward
  correction.** datatypes unchanged at 1107/1129; instance, xpath, json and ber
  untouched. **Zero upward flips, zero added `pass`, zero removed `fail`** —
  the flipped-ID set is identical to the verified set, the file stays canonically
  sorted at 15432 unique IDs, and no other lane file was written.
- **Spec: n/a — harness correctness.** The normative text here is the suite's own
  catalog schema, `testdata/xsdtests/common/xsts.xsd`, not an XSD rule; no rule
  ID was cited and none should have been.
- Gate: build/test/vet/lint clean, conformance green, re-run independently by the
  arbiter in round 2.
- **The defect, stated exactly**: `validityTest.SchemaDoc` was a scalar `docRef`,
  and `encoding/xml` **overwrites** a non-slice field on each repeated matching
  child. A `schemaTest` declaring several `<schemaDocument>` children therefore
  kept only the **last** one, and the harness rooted `parser.Parse` at whichever
  document happened to be listed last — deciding the case against a document the
  suite had not put under test. The field is now `SchemaDocs []docRef`; `makeCase`
  splits it into `caseSpec.doc` (the first, the parse root) plus a new
  `caseSpec.extraDocs` (the rest, resolved, in document order); a `schemaTest`
  naming no `schemaDocument` at all now errors as the malformed catalog entry it
  is rather than indexing into an empty slice.
- **The grounding is what made this a set problem rather than a picking problem.**
  `xsts.xsd` (lines 401–426) documents multi-document semantics in its own
  `xsd:documentation`: "a single schema is constructed from the set", "the test
  should be run as if the schema documents given were loaded one by one, in
  order", modelled as one synthetic `<xsd:import>` per listed document. That is a
  **load-every-listed-document** model. There is no "first is principal, rest are
  dependencies" convention — the `role` attribute (`principal|imported|…`) that
  would have expressed one exists but is documented as "a late introduction and
  is not widely used", and `role="overridden"` appears exactly **once** in the
  whole local tree. So no heuristic over the list is defensible; only loading all
  of it is.
- **`parser.Parse` takes one root, so the harness DECLINES what it cannot
  compose.** `execSchemaCase` decides a multi-document case only when every extra
  document is provably reachable in the closure `closureScan` already walks from
  the first (via a new `(*closureScan).visited`, a namespace-agnostic lookup over
  the existing loaded index). Otherwise the case declines — a recorded, honest
  gap — rather than emitting a verdict on a schema other than the declared one.
  Same decline-not-guess discipline `schemaShapeDecidable` already applies to
  `defaultAttributes`; the alternative (merge several roots) is a mechanism this
  harness does not have and this issue was not going to invent.
- **The 69 are the real story, and the arbiter proved the cohort is exactly
  closed rather than taking the count on report.** The schema lane holds **93**
  multi-document schemaTests; **exactly 69** were recorded `pass`; the regression
  set after the fix is **byte-identical** to those 69, both directions; **no
  single-document case moves at all.** For each of the 69 the arbiter replayed
  the closure walk from the document the *old* code actually rooted at (the last
  `<schemaDocument>`) and found it reaches **none** of the other declared
  documents — every one of those passes was a verdict on a strict subset of the
  declared set that coincidentally agreed with the suite's declared validity.
  `wildO002` decided `wildO002a.xsd`, a lone `<attribute>`, while the principal
  `wildO002.xsd` holds the anonymous complexType with `<anyAttribute>` actually
  under test; `addB004` decided a `<notation name="aa"/>` while the case asserts
  on `<notation public="aa"/>`. Post-fix split: **0/93 decide, 87/93 decline at
  closure decidability on the first document, 6/93 at the new extra-document
  reachability check** (within the 69 specifically, 67 and 2).
- **Why a downward write was legitimate, and how it was constrained.** `Ratchet`
  refuses to write while regressions exist (`expectations.go:167`), so
  `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` alone cannot express this move. The authority is #238's own
  Acceptance clause — "expected unchanged or **downward-corrected by the
  arbiter** (arbiter-only) … do not edit any expectations file by hand" — and
  #238 is human-filed by the repo owner, which is the gate CLAUDE.md's one rule
  requires. Mechanically: the 69 verified IDs were dropped by a filter driven by
  the computed ID list, then re-recorded at observed status **through
  `WriteExpectations` under `GOXSD_RATCHET=1`**, never hand-edited, then audited
  line by line. Retiring a false accept is correcting a mis-recorded expectation,
  not lowering one — and it landed as its own commit on top of mason's rather
  than being amended in, so the repair round could build on it without revisiting
  it.
- **The issue body was wrong about `<xs:redefine>`, and mason said so instead of
  implementing around it.** The issue assumed `redefine` composition had landed
  with #183; only `<xs:override>` did (`parser/parse.go:107`), and
  `schemaShapeDecidable` declines any top-level `<redefine>` outright
  (`conformance/schema.go:310`). So of the four cases the issue named, three
  (`s3_4_2_4ii01s`, `ii03s`, `ii05s`) were `fail` before and remain `fail` —
  unchanged, not flipped — and only `d4_3_15ii29s` moves, pass→decline, and for
  a reason unrelated to the multi-document fix (an inline anonymous complexType
  in its own first document). The issue's headline motivation was thus mostly
  wrong while its underlying defect was entirely real.
- **The rejection was narrow and entirely about comments describing the rule
  wrongly in the file that defines it.** Two sites in `conformance/schema.go`
  named `<redefine>` as a directive by which an extra document becomes reachable
  and omitted `<override>`; `closureScan.decidable` follows exactly
  `include`/`override`/`import`, and a `<redefine>` can never supply reachability
  because its presence declines the carrying document before any directive is
  walked. The third finding was an unsupported frequency claim — "most such cases
  are self-arranging" — which measurement showed to be false (0 of 93 decide).
  The repair named the three real directives, said why `<redefine>` cannot serve,
  and replaced the guess with the measured state; nothing else moved.
- Surprises — **a comment can be rejected for being false and then survive for
  being specific.** Round 2 explicitly kept the replacement sentence ("No
  multi-document schemaTest in the suite meets the condition today … most decline
  at closure decidability") after re-measuring it (87/93 — "most" is accurate),
  on the reasoning that round 1 rejected the old claim for being **false, not for
  being specific**, and that the sentence answers the question the code otherwise
  provokes: *if nothing decides, why does `extraDocsInClosure` exist?* Its
  staleness risk is self-correcting — it is hedged with "today", and anything
  widening the decidable subset flips these very cases through the ratchet, which
  puts the arbiter in the loop by construction.
- Friction — **none charged against the session**; grounding → implement → judge
  → repair → re-judge ran in one clean pass, and the one rejection was
  comment-only. Two things worth carrying, neither blocking:
  1. **Erroring on a `schemaTest` with zero `<schemaDocument>` makes one
     malformed catalog entry fatal to the whole run.** Harmless today (discovery
     still yields all 41858 cases) and required to guard the `SchemaDocs[0]`
     index, but worth remembering at the next suite bump.
  2. `gofmt -l` flags `xsderr/catalog.go` in both rounds. Pre-existing on
     `origin/main`, untouched here, `golangci-lint` clean — not this branch's
     defect, but it keeps showing up in verdicts.
- Next:
  1. **[for the next `/retro`, not filed] the issue's stale `<xs:redefine>`
     premise is a class, not an incident.** #238 assumed redefine composition
     landed with #183 when only `<override>` did, and that assumption survived
     into a `ready` issue body and out the other side. Worth sweeping the open
     and recently-closed issues for the same assumption before another one is
     implemented against it. This is the second consecutive session (cf. #235,
     #236) where the issue body, not the grounding, was the stale artifact.
  2. **[to file] no suite case exercises `extraDocsInClosure`'s decide path.**
     0 of 93 multi-document schemaTests are decided today, so the reachability
     check is covered by unit tests only. The cohort becomes live the moment the
     closure's decidable subset widens — which is exactly when a silent bug there
     would be most expensive.
  3. **[to file, from #309's ledger and still unfiled] the three non-blocking
     findings recorded in this month's #309 entry** (the 15 sibling skip messages
     interpolating `suiteRoot`, the missing `return` after `endUnusableSuiteRun`,
     and its untested policy branching) remain unowned. Flagged here so the leak
     is visible to the follow-up audit rather than only in the entry that created
     it.
- Thread record (do not duplicate) — all on
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/238>. Oracle GROUNDING (the verbatim
  `xsts.xsd:401–426` "loaded one by one, in order" note, the synthetic-import
  model, the `role` attribute's disuse, and the per-case `<schemaDocument>` data
  for the four named cases):
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/238#issuecomment-5151459468>; the full
  grounding also sits at `.agent/grounding-issue-238.md` on the branch, which is
  **gitignored scratch and deliberately not in the landed diff** — the comment is
  the durable copy. Arbiter round 1 (**reject** at `1408819` — the closed-cohort
  proof of the 69, the five file-level spot checks, the ratchet authorization and
  write procedure, the mutation checks on all three new tests, and the three
  comment findings):
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/238#issuecomment-5151532127>. Arbiter
  round 2 (**accept** at `ae1a500` — comment-only diff confirmed line by line,
  the corrected text re-verified against `closureScan.decidable` and
  `schemaShapeDecidable`, the 93-case re-measurement, and the re-derivation of
  4316 → 4247 straight from the expectations files):
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/238#issuecomment-5151549272>. This
  entry is WORKFLOW's "Land" step, written on the WIP branch before the PR is
  opened so it rides in the squash; the squash message carries
  `Ratchet: schema lane 4316/15432 → 4247/15432 (−69, arbiter downward
  correction of wrong-reason passes)`.

## 2026-08-01 — #368 — parser: a referenced `<attributeGroup>` folds under its DECLARING producer, closing the third and last instance of the no-owner pattern after #228 and #337; mason deliberately diverged from the oracle's recommended option, and the arbiter independently verified the divergence was correct before accepting on round 1

- Shipped on `wip/issue-368` as one mason commit — `b6b818e`
  (`parser: fold a referenced <attributeGroup> under its declaring producer
  (#368)`, 4 files, +232/−16: `parser/produce.go`, `parser/produce_complex.go`,
  `parser/parse_test.go`, and the new
  `parser/produce_attributegroup_internal_test.go`). **Arbiter ACCEPT on
  round 1, no repair round consumed.**
- **Ratchet: unchanged** — the arbiter's figures, copied not recomputed.
  `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` ran and wrote nothing; all seven expectations files are
  md5-identical before and after. Lanes: schema 4247/15432,
  datatypes 1107/1129, instance 0/26426, every one identical to `main`.
  **No W3C fixture combines chameleon inclusion with this exact exposure**, so
  this is a false-reject-only fix guarded by its own tests, not a lane mover —
  the same expected result as #337's own "Ratchet: unchanged", and the issue
  predicted it.
- **Spec**: §3.4.2.4 clause `c-add2` and §3.6.2.1 (the `{attribute uses}` fold
  and its transitive closure); `src-resolve` §3.17.6.2 clause 4.1.1; §F.1
  task (b); §3.2.2.2 `dcl.att.local`; §3.10.2.2 (`<anyAttribute>`); §4.2.3
  `c-incl-incl`; §4.2.5 (override ordering).
- **The defect, third of three.** `symbols.attributeGroups` held a bare
  `*Element` with no owning producer, so `collectReferencedGroup`
  (`parser/produce_complex.go`) folded a referenced `<attributeGroup ref="G">`'s
  content under the **asking** producer instead of G's **declaring** document's
  producer. Local `<attribute>` declarations therefore took the referrer's
  `attributeFormDefault`/target namespace, and unqualified `type=`/`ref=`
  QNames took the referrer's §F.1 coercion — a false reject for any
  chameleon-included or cross-document attribute group, in both document
  orders. Identical class to `symbols.complexTypes` (#228) and
  `symbols.simpleTypes` (#337), one index over, and the instance #337's own
  "Next" flagged for a standalone issue.
- **The oracle recommended option (b); mason shipped option (a), with the
  divergence argued rather than assumed.** The grounding established that
  `c-add2`/§3.6.2.1 fold the referenced group's **already-built**
  `{attribute uses}` component property, and on that reading recommended
  reusing the top-level `AttributeGroupDefinition` built once under its own
  producer (option (b)) over re-walking raw source elements at each reference
  site (option (a)). Mason took (a) — routing the existing `visited`-threaded
  recursive walk through `src.owner.collectAttributeContent(src.elem, visited,
  uses, wildcards)` — on two grounds. **(1) Cycles.** attributeGroup reference
  cycles are spec-legal under §3.6.2.1 (the property is a transitive closure,
  never an error), so a completed-component memo cannot use the existing
  `built`/`builtComplex` on-stack-is-an-error tri-state idiom: here on-stack is
  legal, not a defect. **(2) Diamonds.** Two referrers sharing one nested group
  would double-splice that group's uses under naive component reuse, colliding
  with `ag-props-correct`'s no-duplicate-expanded-name constraint — whereas the
  existing shared `visited` map already contributes a shared group exactly once.
- **The struct was reused, not twinned, for the third time running.**
  `symbols.attributeGroups` moved to the same `typeSource{elem, owner}` that
  #337 generalized out of #228's `complexSource`. One encoding, no parallel
  source struct minted (STYLE T4) — the pattern that made this the cheapest of
  the three fixes to land.
- **The arbiter found the decisive evidence the grounding did not have, and it
  settles (a) vs (b) on its own.** Tracing `parser/parse.go:541–551`, it
  established that `compile()` runs **every** document's `prescan()` before any
  `run()` — so in a referrer-first document order the referenced group's
  top-level component **does not exist yet** at the moment the fold is needed.
  Option (b) would therefore have required an on-demand-build memo, which is
  exactly what the legal-cycle argument says the existing idiom cannot safely
  provide. The divergence was correct, not a shortcut, and the arbiter said so
  after reconstructing it rather than taking mason's word.
- **The fix also routes `produceWildcard` correctly, a bonus beyond the fold.**
  A referenced group's `<anyAttribute>` now resolves its namespace constraint
  under the declaring producer per §3.10.2.2 — same defect class, same
  one-line-of-routing cure, not separately claimed by the issue.
- **The tests were proven load-bearing by mutation, twice over.** The arbiter
  ran both new test files against unmodified `main` (both fail) and then
  reverted **only** the one-line routing change inside the full diff (fails
  identically) — so neither test can pass vacuously nor be carried by the rest
  of the change. It then wrote **four adversarial fixtures of its own**:
  cross-document diamond, cross-document cycle with mixed owners, a genuine
  duplicate-name collision inside a diamond (still correctly rejected — the
  fix does not launder real `ag-props-correct` violations), and
  component-vs-fold agreement under a cycle. All pass. Exported surface
  identical (`go doc -all` byte-diff empty, T5/T8), and the §4.2.5
  `<override>` carve-out preserved byte-identical in shape.
- **One test is package-internal on purpose.**
  `TestAttributeGroupComponentAndInlineFoldAgree` lives in the new
  `parser/produce_attributegroup_internal_test.go` because `xsd.Schema` exposes
  no `AttributeGroup(QName)` accessor, and adding one purely to let an external
  test reach the component would export API with no consumer (STYLE T8/T5).
  `TestParseAttributeGroupFoldedUnderItsOwnProducer` (`parser/parse_test.go`)
  stays external and forces **both** document orders, matching the #228/#337
  house style so no regression can hide behind whichever order runs first.
- Surprises — **the oracle's reading of the spec was right and its
  implementation recommendation was still wrong.** `c-add2` does describe a
  fold of an already-built component; the recommendation followed from the spec
  text but not from this codebase's construction order, which the grounding had
  no reason to inspect. Worth remembering that a grounding's *what* can be
  sound while its *how* is a guess about code the oracle cannot read.
- Friction — **none charged against the session.** Ground → implement → judge
  ran in one clean pass, single commit, single round, no gate churn. Having
  #228 and #337 already landed meant the shape of the fix was known before the
  first line was written; the only real work was the (a)-vs-(b) argument.
- Non-blocking arbiter note, recorded here so a future D4 sweep does not
  misattribute it: `collectAttributeContent`'s `visited` set is a **pre-existing
  "seen set" pattern, unchanged by this landing**, and it computes the
  §3.6.2.1-mandated transitive closure correctly. No defect, no follow-up
  filed — a heads-up only, on the arbiter's own framing.
- Next:
  1. **The no-owner pattern is now closed at all three indices** (#228
     complexTypes, #337 simpleTypes, #368 attributeGroups). Nothing further to
     file on it. #337's second "Next" still stands untouched and unfiled by
     design: `<list itemType>` and `<union memberTypes>` carry the same
     unqualified-QName exposure per §F.1 but remain unreachable today, and need
     the same owner routing whenever those constructs land.
  2. **Still unowned from earlier this month, and repeated here so the leak
     stays visible to the follow-up audit rather than only in the entries that
     created it**: #238's two follow-ups (the stale-`<xs:redefine>`-premise
     sweep for the next `/retro`; no suite case exercising
     `extraDocsInClosure`'s decide path) and #309's three non-blocking findings.
- Thread record (do not duplicate) —
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/368>. Oracle GROUNDING (the
  `c-add2`/§3.6.2.1 fold reading and the option (a)/(b) framing this session
  ultimately decided against):
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/368#issuecomment-5151680205>. The
  arbiter's round-1 ACCEPT carries the `parse.go:541–551` prescan-before-run
  finding, the four adversarial fixtures, the two mutation checks, the
  §3.10.2.2 wildcard confirmation, the empty exported-surface diff, and the
  md5-verified flat ratchet. Prior art referenced and not re-derived: #228
  (`complexSource`) and #337 (`typeSource`, and the "Next" that filed this
  issue). This entry is WORKFLOW's "Land" step, written on the WIP branch
  before the PR is opened so it rides in the squash; the squash message
  carries `Ratchet: unchanged`.

## 2026-08-01 — backlog — band 1 reordered so harness integrity leads, on the strength of #238's measured 69 wrong-reason passes; the `schema` lane is NET DOWN 37 across eleven landings; one issue filed (#379), the GAP ledger closed at 28/28 owned, and libuser is stale but unrunnable under this toolset

- Doc-only reconciliation, **no commit of my own** — `docs/PLAN.md` (+265 lines,
  appended as a new dated entry at the end of the M4 section; **no old paragraph
  rewritten**, per the append-only convention) and this LOG entry are the only
  file edits. No Go code changed, no gate run, no `GOXSD_RATCHET=1`. Branch:
  `claude/dazzling-cerf-i5gj0k`, at `origin/main` tip `ee1226b`; the
  orchestrating session lands both files as the single `meta: backlog 2026-08-01`
  commit via PR per WORKFLOW.md's "Other triggers". **Second `/backlog` in as
  many days**, run after eleven landings.
- **Ratchet: unchanged** (doc-only trigger). Figures below are *read off*
  `conformance/testdata/expectations/*.txt` at `ee1226b` and cross-checked
  against every `Ratchet:` commit trailer in the window — **no live conformance
  run was performed**, which is the arbiter's job and not this trigger's.

  | lane | pass / total | since 2026-07-31 |
  |---|---|---|
  | `schema` | 4247 / 15432 | 4284 → 4247, **net −37** |
  | `datatypes` | 1107 / 1129 | 1059/1081 → 1107/1129, **+48** |
  | `instance` | 0 / 26426 | unchanged |
  | `xpath` / `json` / `ber` | empty by design | unchanged |

  The `schema` arithmetic, recorded in PLAN so nobody re-derives it: 4284 →#230
  **+12**→ 4296 →#231 **+7**→ 4303 →#232 **+2**→ 4305 →#235 **+2**→ 4307 →#344
  **+5**→ 4312 →#236 **+4**→ 4316 →#238 **−69**→ **4247**. Eight landings earned
  **+32 gross**; #238's authorized downward correction of 69 wrong-reason passes
  erased it and more. #309, #337 and #368 were verified flat by the arbiter,
  #309 over a *non-empty* suite — which is the distinction #309 existed to
  create.
- **Band 1 was reordered, and that reordering is this pass's main output.** New
  working queue: **#277 → #276 (+#377 riding) → #264 → #265 → #301 → #281 →
  #365 → #346 → #324.** The driver is #238. The 2026-07-31 band ranked #277 and
  #276 **dead last**, with the note that they "move no lane by design" while
  being "the only items here whose absence can lock a false accept into the
  ratchet". Eight days later #238 turned that hypothetical into a **measured 69
  wrong-reason passes in the same package**. The cost of a wrong-reason pass
  compounds — the never-regress wall makes it expensive to unwind *later*, not
  now — so harness integrity moves to the front while the lane is still young
  enough to pay for it. **#365 is newly promoted**: the only issue in the whole
  queue whose acceptance criterion is literally a lane number, and the cheapest
  movement available. **Eight entries / eight issues — the 8–10 band met for the
  working queue for the first time since 2026-07-21**, though only because the
  list is doing the band's work by hand.
- **Issues: one filed, none closed, none relabelled.** **#379** — the one
  unowned `GAP(` marker left in the tree (`parser/doc.go:118`): two
  distinct-but-transform-equivalent `<xs:override>` elements get different
  `docKey`s, so the same component is produced twice and `sch-props-correct`
  cl.2 over-rejects. Filed `ready`, `kind/gap`, `area/parser`, M4, and
  explicitly framed as **the sibling #287 refuses to conflate itself with** —
  cross-ref comments posted both ways so the two cannot be settled in
  contradictory directions.
- **Milestones: the working queue is now milestone-consistent.** M4 assigned to
  #79, #265, #276, #277, #281, #301, #346 (plus the new #379) — **29 of 81 open
  issues carry M4, up from 21 of 80**. The backlog behind band 1 was
  deliberately left alone: what a milestone means for a backlog issue versus a
  working-queue issue is **#347's question**, not a separate one. This overtakes
  the 2026-07-25 "no open issue carries a milestone" note, which was wrong in
  the other direction too — one milestone was in use all along, assigned by
  nothing but filing date, and partial coverage reads as a claim.
- **Comments posted, all correcting drift the sweep found rather than adding
  scope**: **#345** (line drift, and it has **silently shrunk from 3 sites to
  2** — the third now belongs to #372), **#279** (line drift; its "do before
  #183" preference has **expired**), **#267** (line drift; the marker cites the
  **wrong owner**), **#287** (the #379 cross-ref above), **#347**
  (sixth-overrun evidence, below).
- **Queue state: 81 open — 69 `ready`, 11 `blocked`, 1 deliberately unlabelled
  (#291).** The sequence is **9 → 16 → 26 → 34 → 35 → 65 → 69**, the sixth
  consecutive overrun, and this pass produced the strongest form of #347's
  finding yet: **eleven issues closed in one day and `ready` still grew by
  four.** The harvest rate is not a symptom of a slow loop. All three shrink
  mechanisms were exhausted again — nothing stale enough to close, nothing
  duplicated (the two near-misses, #345/#372 and #267/#265, are genuinely
  different sites and were disambiguated by comment), no `ready` issue with an
  open dependency — and one issue was *added* because the anti-leak convention
  required it. **#347 was not resolved**: CLAUDE.md reserves changes to
  `.claude/agents/cartographer.md` for a human-filed issue, and both of #347's
  options change it. Until then **PLAN band 1 IS the working queue** and the
  other 61 `ready` issues are the backlog behind it.
- **New evidence for #347, posted to its thread: a third label state already
  exists in practice and is undocumented.** **#291** carries neither `ready` nor
  `blocked`, deliberately ("on the steward's radar for the next `/retro`, not in
  the develop loop's queue"). That is #347's own option (a), invented ad hoc by
  one filer, with no label and no way for `/backlog` to tell it from a filing
  mistake. Whatever #347 decides should account for #291 rather than leave it a
  one-off.
- **GAP debt: 28 sites, up from 23 — and after this pass all 28 are owned.** The
  growth is entirely **#236 (+3) and #344 (+2) disclosing their own fail-open as
  they landed**, which is STYLE 9 working as designed. The **full owner → site
  map is now recorded in PLAN.md**, so the next sweep starts from a ledger
  instead of a `grep`. **#265 alone owns seven sites**, the largest
  single-issue concentration there is.
- **Branch namespace (report-only, nothing touched): three stale `wip/*` refs,
  no `parked/*`, all three flagged for human deletion.**
  **`wip/issue-368` @ `79c4700` is new and is the interesting one** — #368
  *landed* (squashed as `3e97b77`), so GitHub should have auto-deleted the ref;
  it survived because **the post-land PLAN commit was pushed to the branch after
  the merge**. `git diff origin/wip/issue-368 HEAD` is **empty** — nothing owed,
  nothing at risk. Mechanism now recorded in PLAN: a future `/backlog` that sees
  a `wip/issue-<N>` for a *closed* issue should diff it before assuming work was
  lost. **`wip/issue-195`** unchanged (replan = #304). **`wip/issue-226` is now
  fully discharged** — its replacement #344 landed with `schema +5`, banking and
  superseding the unbanked `+4` the 2026-07-31 pass recorded. The #195 → #304
  and #226 → #344 dispositions are now **both proven**: a park that gets a
  replacement issue lands; a park without one is invisible.
- **Personas: cliuser correctly skipped, libuser genuinely stale and not run.**
  cliuser's skip is mechanical, not a judgement call —
  `git diff 738fea2..HEAD -- cmd/ README.md` is **empty**, so #251, #16 and the
  `-version` reservation carry their criteria unchanged. libuser is the opposite:
  the PLAN persona addendum landed in `738fea2` and therefore **predates all
  eleven of the 2026-08-01 landings**, and **four exported identifiers have
  appeared since** — `xsd.ValueSpace`, `value.NewValueSpace`,
  `xsd.UnionNamespaceConstraint` (promoted from unexported), and the one that
  matters, **`(*xsd.SchemaBuilder).FinalizeWith(ValueSpace) (*Schema, error)`,
  a second finalize entry point on the primary builder**. #252's finding was
  made against a surface that did not have it.
- Surprises:
  1. **The lane went backwards across the busiest day this repo has had**, and
     correctly so. Eleven landings, +32 gross on `schema`, net −37. A day can be
     unambiguously productive and still end with a smaller number, and the
     `Ratchet:` trailers are the only reason that reads as a finding rather than
     a regression.
  2. **The previous band's own hedge was the thing that got measured.** #277/#276
     were ranked last *with a written warning attached*, and the warning came
     true in eight days in the same package. The reordering this pass is less a
     new judgement than the old one finally being priced.
  3. **A merged branch resurrected itself.** `wip/issue-368` exists only because
     the post-land PLAN commit raced GitHub's auto-delete — the first time the
     namespace survey has found a ref that looks like lost work and provably
     isn't.
  4. **#345 shrank without anyone editing it.** Three sites became two because
     #372 took one; nothing in the issue body recorded that. The GAP ledger now
     in PLAN exists partly so this class of silent shrinkage is visible next
     sweep.
- Friction:
  1. **The persona half of `/backlog` is still structurally unrunnable under the
     cartographer** — no subagent-spawn tool, so libuser cannot run under the
     isolation its value depends on (godoc + README only, never source), and a
     persona pass faked by a session that has read the source is worth less than
     none. This is the **third** consecutive flagging (2026-07-25, 2026-07-31,
     today) and the second time the trigger has needed two sessions' toolsets to
     complete once. Carried to the next retro, not filed — the same disposition
     as 2026-07-31, and this line is its ledger entry.
  2. **Three of five issue comments this pass were pure line drift** (#345,
     #279, #267). Marker line numbers in issue bodies rot on every landing that
     touches the file, and the sweep spends real time re-anchoring them. Worth a
     retro look at whether markers should be cited by owner + rule ID rather
     than `file:line`.
  3. **The lane figures had to be reconstructed from commit trailers** because
     this trigger cannot run the ratchet. That worked only because every one of
     the eight landings carried an honest `Ratchet:` line; one missing trailer
     and the arithmetic above is unverifiable.
- Next:
  1. Develop loop takes the reordered band 1: **#277** first, then **#276**
     (with **#377** riding — take both or neither), **#264 → #265**, **#301**,
     **#281**, **#365**, **#346**, **#324**. **#365 inherits no measurement**,
     so its `Ratchet:` figure must come from a diagnostic run — the #354 hazard,

  2. **#379 should be sequenced with #287** — the two `<xs:override>`-identity
     slices, deliberately kept apart.
  3. **libuser wants a broader-toolset session, with the specific question
     named**: does `Finalize` vs `FinalizeWith` read as a coherent choice from
     godoc alone?
  4. **#347 still wants a human or a retro decision** before the next backlog,

  5. **Still unowned and repeated here so the leak stays visible to the

     sweep; no suite case exercising `extraDocsInClosure`'s decide path — note
