## 2026-08-01 — #277 — conformance: `validity="indeterminate"` stops being folded into expects-invalid and becomes its own outcome, declined at the lane seam without ever calling an executor; 16 cases lose the ability to be won by any rejection, and the 2 that were passing for the wrong reason were surrendered by an authorized downward ratchet correction on the #238 precedent, arbiter ACCEPT on round 1

- Shipped on `wip/issue-277` as two commits on top of `42c2f7f` — `0e7881c`
  (`conformance: score validity="indeterminate" as its own outcome, declined
  (#277)`, mason, 8 files, +358/−102: `conformance/runner.go`,
  `conformance/runner_test.go`, `conformance/datatypes.go`,
  `conformance/datatypes_test.go`, `conformance/schema.go`,
  `conformance/schema_test.go`, `conformance/schema_closure_test.go`,
  `conformance/doc.go`) and `1d7937b` (`conformance: arbiter downward
  correction of 2 indeterminate passes (#277)`, the arbiter's own
  machine-written ratchet write, 1 file, 2 lines flipped `pass`→`fail`).
  Branch total 9 files, +360/−104. No new files. **Arbiter ACCEPT on round 1,
  no repair round consumed.**
- **Ratchet: schema lane 4247/15432 → 4245/15432 (−2, an authorized downward
  correction); datatypes 1107/1129 unchanged; instance, xpath, json and ber
  byte-identical by hash.** The arbiter's figures, copied not recomputed. The
  two surrendered IDs are exactly the two the issue's own acceptance criterion
  4 names — `MS-Element2006-07-15/elemZ031/schema/elemZ031` and
  `MS-Schema2006-07-15/schZ015/schema/schZ015`. **Zero upward flips, zero
  added `pass`, zero vanished**; the file stays canonically sorted at 15432
  unique IDs.
- **Indeterminate cases discovered per lane, recorded so a later submodule bump
  that changes the count is visible (the issue's acceptance criterion 5):
  schema 12, instance 4, datatypes 0 — 16 total**, all in `msMeta`:
  `Schema_w3c.xml` 9, `Additional_w3c.xml` 4, `Particles_w3c.xml` 2,
  `Element_w3c.xml` 1. That matches the issue's measured scope exactly. **Of
  the 16, 14 were already recorded `fail`**; only the two named above were
  `pass` before this change.
- **Spec: n/a as a rule implementation — but the grounding is the whole
  argument, and it is normative.** The oracle was asked whether XSD 1.1
  Structures gives any basis for treating the suite's `indeterminate` as
  equivalent to `invalid`. **It does not.** `[validity]` is a genuine
  three-valued PSVI outcome — `valid` / `invalid` / `notKnown`, §3.2.5.1
  (`sic-a-outcome`) and §3.3.5.1 (`sic-e-outcome`) — and §5.2
  (`validation_outcome`) states outright that "schema validity is not a binary
  predicate" and that "there is no requirement that input which is not
  schema-valid be rejected by an application." The suite's own catalog DTD
  (`testdata/xsdtests/wgMeta/ancillary/xsts.dtd`, lines 184–194) makes
  `indeterminate` a **fifth, disjoint** category alongside
  `valid|invalid|notKnown|runtime-schema-error` — the marker the working group
  used for cases it left undecided. So declining is a harness-scoring
  convention chosen because the spec supplies no equivalence, not a spec
  requirement; the code says so in those words.
- **The defect, stated exactly**: `resolveExpected` reduced every
  `<expected validity="...">` to `Validity == "valid"`, so `indeterminate`
  landed in the same bucket as `invalid`. A case the W3C working group could
  not agree has one right answer therefore counted as a **PASS whenever this
  processor rejected the document — for any reason, right or wrong**. That is
  the ratchet-integrity soft spot the issue is named for: 16 cases winnable by
  accident, and two of them already won.
- **The fix is a genuine three-valued type, and the zero value is the safe
  one.** `caseSpec.expectValid bool` was replaced by an unexported
  `expectation` (a struct over an unexported `outcomeKind`, constructed only
  via `expectValid` / `expectInvalid` / `expectIndeterminate`, with derived
  accessors `wantsValid()` / `isIndeterminate()`) — one axis, one encoding, no
  `indeterminate bool` sitting beside a now-meaningless `expectValid bool`
  (STYLE D3). **The zero value is indeterminate**, so a `caseSpec` built
  without an explicit classification declines rather than silently scoring as
  expects-invalid: the failure mode this issue exists to close cannot be
  reintroduced by a forgotten field. `classifyValidity` maps the suite string
  through it, composed with — not replacing — the pre-existing `@version`
  precedence rule, which still decides before validity is read.
- **The decline sits at the single `runLane` dispatch seam, and that placement
  is the point.** An indeterminate case goes straight to `Fail()`, the standing
  recorded-gap status, **without `l.exec` ever being called**. Not "the
  executor answers and we ignore it" — the executor is never asked. **No
  executor, present or future, can earn a pass on a case with no agreed right
  answer.** The 11 executor call sites in `datatypes.go` and `schema.go` were
  converted to read `c.expect.wantsValid()`; none of them learned anything new.
- **The policy is now documented where it was previously an implicit `==
  "valid"` comparison.** `conformance/doc.go` and the affected doc comments
  state the indeterminate rule explicitly. The old behaviour was not written
  down anywhere — it was an inference from one string compare, which is most
  of why it survived.
- **The escalated question of this session was the downward move, and it was
  resolved by precedent rather than by improvisation.** Acceptance criterion 4
  requires surrendering exactly two previously-`pass` cases that were passing
  for the wrong reason (accidental rejection of an indeterminate case, not a
  rule-correct rejection). `Ratchet()` **cannot express** that move — it aborts
  on any Regressed case by design, which is the one rule that outranks
  everything working correctly. The arbiter reused the **exact procedure #238
  established** ("authorized wrong-reason-pass correction", the −69 the
  2026-08-01 backlog already logged): drop the two named IDs from the committed
  expectations, then let `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` **re-derive** them at observed
  status. **`schema.txt` was never hand-edited.**
- **The preconditions for that procedure were verified before it was applied,
  not assumed from the precedent.** (1) #277 is human-filed by the repo owner —
  the gate CLAUDE.md's one rule requires. (2) Criterion 4 names the exact two
  IDs, so the authorized set is enumerated in the issue rather than computed by
  the agent doing the writing. (3) The correction **retires a false accept**
  rather than lowering a real capability. And before writing, the arbiter
  computed the full **four-way Delta on all six lanes** — not just Regressed —
  confirming `improved=0, new=0, vanished=0` everywhere and `regressed=2` in
  the schema lane only, **byte-identical to the authorized set**. A downward
  write whose scope is measured first is a different act from one whose scope
  is discovered afterwards.
- **The tests were shown to be load-bearing, including the one that asserts an
  absence.** Three are new: `TestResolveExpectedClassifiesDeclaredValidity`
  (10 rows — all three validity spellings plus the mixed-declaration cases that
  pin `@version` precedence *composing* with the new classification rather than
  being replaced by it); `TestRunLaneDeclinesIndeterminateWithoutExecuting`,
  which asserts **the executor is never invoked**, not merely that the result
  comes out `Fail` — the weaker assertion would pass against an implementation
  that runs the executor and discards its answer, which is exactly the design
  this issue rejects; and `TestMakeCaseCarriesIndeterminateThrough`. All three
  were mutation-tested by the arbiter against unmodified `main`.
- **Exported surface diff empty** — `go doc -all` byte-identical before and
  after. The whole three-valued type, its constructors and its accessors are
  unexported; `conformance` is a test harness with no published surface.
- **The design review found the type idiomatic rather than novel**: it mirrors
  the existing `Status` idiom in the same package (constructors only, derived
  accessor, no redundant flag), which is why a three-valued replacement for a
  bool cost 8 files of mechanical conversion and no new concept.
- **No warden pre-flight was required and none was skipped.** The issue's
  `## Surface` section reads "none", and the landed diff bears that out — zero
  exported identifiers added or changed.
- Surprises — **the smaller half of the 16 was the interesting half.** The
  issue framed 16 cases as winnable by any rejection; only 2 were actually
  being won. The other 14 were already `fail`, i.e. this processor was *also*
  failing to reject them, so the soft spot was mostly latent rather than
  cashed. That makes the correction cheap **today** and is precisely the
  argument the 2026-08-01 backlog made for promoting #277 to the front of band
  1: the cost of a wrong-reason pass compounds, because the never-regress wall
  makes it expensive to unwind later rather than now. Landing this at 4247
  cost 2; landing it after the schema lane matures would have cost whatever
  fraction of the 16 had gone green in the meantime.
- Friction — **none charged against the change** (one round, one mason commit,
  no gate churn), but one repo-wide drift surfaced and is deliberately **not**
  fixed here:
  1. **`conformance/runner.go:115` cites `STYLE T7`, and `docs/STYLE.md` only
     runs T1–T6.** The rule the comment means ("illegal states
     unrepresentable") is actually **T1**. This is **not mason's error**: the
     arbiter found **10 further pre-existing sites** already citing `STYLE T7`
     (often as `T1/T7`) or `STYLE D6` — `xsd/typedefinition.go` (×2),
     `xsd/schema.go` (×2), `xsd/simpletype.go`, `xsd/namespaceconstraint.go`,
     `xsd/closedsets.go`, `builtin/strict/datetime.go`,
     `builtin/strict/duration.go`, `builtin/strict/precisiondecimal.go`.
     STYLE.md's numbering (D1–D5, T1–T6) never grew to cover IDs the code and
     this LOG already use routinely. **Reaching outside this diff to renumber
     eleven sites, or to add rules to STYLE.md, is steward/cartographer scope,
     not this branch's** — a conformance-scoring issue must not quietly become
     a style-doc edit. Flagged here for the post-land pass to decide.
- Next:
  1. **[to file — post-land pass, from the friction above] the `STYLE T7` /
     `STYLE D6` citation drift.** Either STYLE.md grows the rules the code
     already cites, or the eleven sites are renumbered onto T1–T6/D1–D5. The
     decision is a doc-ownership question (does the code's usage document a
     real rule STYLE.md is missing, or is it eleven typos?), which is why it
     wants an issue and a steward opinion rather than a drive-by fix.
  2. **The indeterminate counts are now a tracked invariant, not a one-time
     measurement.** schema 12 / instance 4 / datatypes 0. A `testdata/xsdtests`
     submodule bump that changes any of them changes the set of cases this
     harness declines, and per the issue's criterion 5 that should be noticed
     rather than absorbed — the figures above are the baseline to diff against.
  3. **Still unowned from earlier this month, repeated so the leak stays
     visible to the follow-up audit rather than only in the entries that
     created it**: #238's two follow-ups (the stale-`<xs:redefine>`-premise
     sweep for the next `/retro`; no suite case exercising
     `extraDocsInClosure`'s decide path) and #309's three non-blocking
     findings. **`wip/issue-195`, `wip/issue-226` and `wip/issue-368` await
     human deletion** — all three were re-confirmed this session to map to
     already-CLOSED issues, i.e. none of them is resumable work, which is why
     this session started a new issue rather than adopting one.
- Thread record (do not duplicate) —
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/277>. Oracle GROUNDING (the
  three-valued `[validity]` finding with §3.2.5.1/§3.3.5.1 and §5.2 quoted
  verbatim, and the `xsts.dtd:184–194` disjoint-fifth-category reading):
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/277#issuecomment-5151921970>. The
  full grounding also sits at `.agent/grounding-issue-277.md` on the branch,
  which is **gitignored scratch and deliberately not in the landed diff** — the
  comment is the durable copy, exactly as #238 handled its own. The arbiter's
  round-1 ACCEPT is on the same thread and carries the gate transcript, the
  six-lane four-way Delta measured before the write, the three preconditions
  for the downward correction, the mutation transcript for all three new tests,
  the empty `go doc -all` surface diff, and the `STYLE T7` drift finding; **its
  comment permalink is not recorded here** because it was not captured during
  the session, and inventing one would be worse than omitting it. Prior art
  referenced and not re-derived: **#238** (the authorized wrong-reason-pass
  correction procedure this session reused unchanged) and the 2026-08-01
  backlog entry / PR #380 (which promoted #277 to the head of band 1 on the
  strength of #238's measured 69). This entry is WORKFLOW's "Land" step,
  written on the WIP branch before the PR is opened so it rides in the squash;
  the two branch commits squash into one whose message carries
  `Ratchet: schema 4247/15432 → 4245/15432 (−2, authorized downward correction
  of 2 wrong-reason indeterminate passes)`.

## 2026-08-01 — #240 — parser: the `ref=` form of `<unique>`/`<key>`/`<keyref>` is produced, binding to the SAME component the `name=` form registered rather than minting a second one; resolution happens at mapping time over a new assembly-wide pre-scan index so `src-resolve` stays positioned, and the arbiter rejected round 1 for a walk that indexed `<annotation>` prose as if it were schema

- Shipped on `wip/issue-240` as three commits on top of `8aca017` — `e3beadc`
  (`parser: produce the ref= form of <unique>/<key>/<keyref> (#240)`, 8 files,
  +530/−81), `d9811d5` (`parser: repair round 1 — exclude <annotation> from
  identity-constraint prescan (#240)`, 2 files, +91/−3), and `340d037`
  (`conformance: ratchet schema lane +3 for identity-constraint ref= (#240)`,
  1 file, +3/−3, the arbiter's own machine-written ratchet write). Branch total
  9 files, +621/−84: `parser/produce_xpath.go`, `parser/produce.go`,
  `parser/produce_complex.go`, `conformance/schema.go`,
  `parser/produce_xpath_test.go`, `parser/parse_test.go`,
  `parser/override_test.go`, `conformance/schema_test.go`,
  `conformance/testdata/expectations/schema.txt`. No new files. **REJECT on
  round 1, ACCEPT on round 2 — one repair round consumed of the two allowed.**
- **Ratchet: schema lane +3 — 4245/15432 → 4248/15432 pass, 0 regressed, 0
  vanished, all other lanes unchanged.** The arbiter's figures, copied not
  recomputed. The three upward flips, all `fail -> pass`:
  `IdentityConstraint/s2_2_4v01/schema/s2_2_4v01s`, `.../s2_2_4v02s`,
  `.../s2_2_4v03s` — the W3C suite's `ref=`-with-annotations cases.
  `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` rewrote exactly three lines and touched only
  `expectations/schema.txt`; no expectation was hand-edited and nothing moved
  downward.
- **Spec**: `src-identity-constraint` §3.11.3 clauses 1 (`ref` and `name`
  mutually exclusive), 4 (the `ref=` form permits only `id` and `<annotation>`
  as siblings) and 5 (the category — `unique`/`key`/`keyref` — must match the
  resolved target); `src-resolve` §3.17.6.2 clause 1.7 (QName resolution
  indexes `{identity-constraint definitions}`); §3.11.2's XML Mapping Summary;
  §3.17.2 ("anywhere within the [[children]]"); §3.1.3 (forward and
  cross-document references); §F.2 clause 1 (`<override>` children belong to
  the overridden document).
- **The grounding's load-bearing finding: the `ref=` form produces NO new
  component.** §3.11.2 is taken literally — "the corresponding schema component
  is the identity-constraint definition resolved to by the actual value of the
  `ref` attribute" — so the reference binds to the very same
  `xsd.IdentityConstraint` object the `name=` form already registered, and
  `AddIdentityConstraint` must **not** be called for it. Everything downstream
  follows from that: no new exported sum type (the issue's "Surface: none
  expected" ruled one out), no second component to keep in sync, and a
  build-once memo (`symbols.builtIC`) to guarantee the borrowed component is
  the registered one rather than a copy that happens to compare equal.
- **The design decision: resolve at produce/mapping time, not at finalize.**
  The issue body suggested splitting clause 5 out to a finalize-phase check.
  Mason diverged and flagged the divergence for the arbiter. §3.1.3 permits
  forward and cross-document references, so a new assembly-wide pre-scan index
  (`prescanIdentityConstraints` + `symbols.identityConstraints` in
  `parser/produce.go`) gathers every NAMED `<unique>`/`<key>`/`<keyref>` before
  any resolution runs — mirroring the existing `symbols.simpleTypes` /
  `symbols.complexTypes` forward-reference pattern rather than inventing a new
  mechanism. The payoff is diagnostics: clauses 4 and 5 and the `src-resolve`
  failure are all **positioned at the referring element**, which a finalize
  check structurally cannot be (`xsd/resolve.go` charges `xsderr.Loc{}`).
- **The pre-scan withholds the composition directives' subtrees.** An
  `<override>`'s children are top-level declarations of the OVERRIDDEN document
  (§F.2 clause 1) and are indexed by that document's producer instead — the
  same carve-out #337 and #368 had to preserve, here honoured at index-build
  time rather than at resolution time.
- **`produceIdentityConstraint` split into two paths** (`parser/produce_xpath.go`):
  the `name=` construction path, unchanged, and a new `ref=` resolution path
  carrying clauses 4/5 and `src-resolve` 1.7. The conformance gate's
  `identityConstraintsDecidable` decline helper is **gone entirely**, both call
  sites with it — the construct is now fully producible, so a decline helper
  for it would be dead code claiming a gap that no longer exists (P3). No
  exported identifier was added or changed anywhere in the branch.
- **Arbiter round 1 — REJECT, and the finding is a latent landmine rather than
  a live failure.** `prescanIdentityConstraints` recursed into every element
  child before applying any vocabulary filter, so it descended into
  `<annotation>`. XSD-namespace markup inside `<appinfo>`/`<documentation>` is
  legal (mixed content, `processContents="lax"`) and §3 states that neither the
  correspondences nor the XML Representation Constraints apply to elements in
  the schema namespace occurring as descendants of `<appinfo>` or
  `<documentation>` (`xmlschema11-1.md:727`) — such a `<key name="…">` is
  **prose, mapped to no component**. Indexing it made the pre-scan a strict
  **superset** of `{identity-constraint definitions}`, which is exactly the
  property `src-resolve` clause 1.7 indexes into. The arbiter reproduced three
  distinct defects, all order-dependent because the `builtIC` memo masks them
  whenever the reference follows the real definition: a **false reject** (a
  valid schema charged `src-identity-constraint` clause 2 against a truncated
  illustration), a **silent wrong-component bind** (a `ref=` borrowing a prose
  copy's selector/fields), and a **false accept** (a `ref=` to a name existing
  only inside `<documentation>` resolving cleanly instead of failing
  `src-resolve`). **No suite fixture exercises any of the three today** — which
  is why the round-1 gate was green and why this had to be caught by reading.
- **Repair round 1 was bounded to what the reject specified, and nothing else.**
  An `<annotation>`-skip guard ahead of the recursive descent (neither
  descending nor indexing); the doc comment corrected, since it had claimed the
  walk was confined to what this producer maps and that was now false; and two
  regression tests —
  `TestProduceIdentityConstraintRefIgnoresAnnotationMarkup` and
  `TestProduceIdentityConstraintRefToAnnotationOnlyNameUnresolvable` — with the
  `ref=` deliberately placed **first** in each fixture so the memo cannot mask
  the index.
- **The arbiter verified the repair by breaking it, not by reading it.** In a
  detached worktree it deleted the guard and confirmed all three round-1
  failures reproduce, then confirmed they pass with the guard restored — so
  neither new test can pass vacuously. Diff hygiene confirmed: the repair
  touched only the two files the reject flagged.
- Surprises — **the round-1 defect lived in the part of the change that looked
  least like schema logic.** The pre-scan is bookkeeping: walk the tree, collect
  names. But an index over "the whole document tree" is a claim about which
  elements are schema, and §3's appinfo/documentation exclusion is precisely
  the sentence that makes that claim non-trivial. The general shape worth
  carrying: **a walk that gathers by element name is a vocabulary decision in
  disguise, and the schema namespace is not sufficient evidence of schema-hood.**
- Friction — **none charged against the session's process**; the one repair
  round was a genuine defect found by review doing its job, not churn. Ground →
  implement → judge → repair → judge ran without gate flapping, and mason's
  `Ratchet:` trailers were honest in both directions: `e3beadc` predicted
  "schema lane 4245 -> 4248 pass (+3 improved, 0 regressed)" and the arbiter's
  `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` run confirmed it **exactly**; `d9811d5` correctly said
  `Ratchet: unchanged` and stated why (annotation-nesting has no suite
  coverage). That is now four consecutive sessions of accurate trailers since
  the #231/#232 pattern (#235 exact, #344 low-by-one and explained, #331 exact,
  #240 exact) — the next `/retro` should decide whether the fix enumerated in
  #232's entry is still worth filing or should be dismissed on the record.
- Next:
  1. **[to file — post-land pass; arbiter advisory, explicitly NOT charged as
     repair-round work] the annotation guard's doc comment overclaims.** It
     credits itself with excluding foreign-namespace elements as well as
     `<annotation>`, but the namespace check runs **after** the recursive
     descent — so a foreign-namespace host element containing XSD-namespace
     `<key>` markup **outside** an `<annotation>` would still be walked and
     indexed. The arbiter ruled it non-blocking because reaching it requires a
     schema already invalid under the schema-for-schemas element content model,
     a pre-existing and much broader gap unrelated to this issue. The fix is
     small and self-contained — hoist the namespace check above the recursion —
     and wants its own issue, **scoped separately** from any broader
     content-model enforcement work so the two do not get bundled.
  2. **Still unowned from earlier this month, repeated so the leak stays
     visible to the follow-up audit rather than only in the entries that
     created it**: #238's two follow-ups (the stale-`<xs:redefine>`-premise
     sweep for the next `/retro`; no suite case exercising
     `extraDocsInClosure`'s decide path), #309's three non-blocking findings,
     and #277's `STYLE T7`/`STYLE D6` citation-drift decision.
- Thread record (do not duplicate) —
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/240>. The oracle's GROUNDING was
  posted verbatim as a comment on that thread (clauses 1/4/5, the `src-resolve`
  clause 1.7 indexing rule, the no-new-component finding, and the §3.1.3
  argument for pre-scan-and-resolve over finalize deferral); the full text also
  sits at `.agent/grounding-issue-240.md` on the branch, which is **gitignored
  scratch and deliberately not in the landed diff** — the comment is the durable
  copy, exactly as #238 and #277 handled theirs. The arbiter's round-1 REJECT
  (the three reproduced annotation defects and the bounded repair spec) and
  round-2 ACCEPT (the guard-deletion mutation check, the diff-hygiene
  confirmation, the advisory above, and the banked +3) are on the same thread;
  **their comment permalinks are not recorded here** because they were not
  captured during the session, and inventing them would be worse than omitting
  them. Prior art referenced and not re-derived: #337 and #368 (the
  `symbols.*` forward-reference and `<override>` carve-out patterns this
  pre-scan mirrors). This entry is WORKFLOW's "Land" step, written on the WIP
  branch before the PR is opened so it rides in the squash; the three branch
  commits squash into one whose message carries
  `Ratchet: schema +3 (IdentityConstraint/s2_2_4v01..03 fail→pass)`.

## 2026-08-01 — #276 — conformance: the closure scan stops admitting an unresolvable `<xs:include>` while `<xs:import>` declined the same shape; the fix is NOT the symmetric decline it looked like — the arbiter rejected that for surrendering four genuine `src-include` cl. 2.4 passes, and the landed form declines only when an unresolved directive COINCIDES with a real parse failure, +9

- Shipped on `wip/issue-276` as three commits on top of `5cace46` — `83a3ccf`
  (`conformance: decline an <include>/<override> whose schemaLocation does not
  resolve (#276)`, 3 files, +102/−44), `80a5b4f` (`conformance: repair round 1
  — decline only when the unresolved directive coincides with a real parse
  failure (#276)`, 3 files, +210/−101), and `7e24921` (`conformance: ratchet
  schema lane 4248 -> 4257 (+9) (#276)`, 1 file, +9/−9, the ratchet write —
  measured by the arbiter, committed by the orchestrator because the arbiter's
  tooling does not push). Branch total 4 files, +248/−81:
  `conformance/schema.go`, `conformance/schema_closure.go`,
  `conformance/schema_closure_test.go`,
  `conformance/testdata/expectations/schema.txt`. No new files. **REJECT on
  round 1, ACCEPT on round 2 — one repair round consumed of the two allowed.**
- **Ratchet: schema lane 4248/15432 → 4257/15432 (+9), zero regressions, zero
  vanished; datatypes, instance, xpath, json and ber all unchanged.** The
  arbiter's figures, copied not recomputed. The nine upward flips:
  `annotA013`, `anyURI_a007_1342`, `anyURI_a008_1343`, `schE3`, `schE4`,
  `schE9`, `schZ008`, `schZ012_b`, `schZ012_c` — **the `<import>` cases #182
  previously declined under the old asymmetric handling**, now correctly
  decided. The ratchet write rewrote exactly nine lines and touched only
  `expectations/schema.txt`; nothing moved downward and no expectation was
  hand-edited.
- **Spec**: `src-include` cl. 2.4 (§4.2.3), `src-import` (§4.2.6.2),
  `src-resolve` cl. 4 / `cl.qnr.nsdeclared` (§3.17.6.2), §4.2.5 cl. 3.1.2
  (an `<override>` IS an `<include>` for this walk), §4.2.6.1, §5.3.
- **The grounding's load-bearing finding: the spec does not support the
  asymmetry the code was defending.** The old comment argued "an unresolvable
  `<include>` is not the same hazard in kind" as an unresolvable `<import>`.
  It is: the two directives' "not an error to fail to resolve" texts are
  **textually parallel**, and `src-resolve` clause 4 licenses a same-namespace
  reference **unconditionally** (4.2.1) and a foreign-namespace one on the mere
  PRESENCE of an `<import>` element (4.2.2, whether or not that import
  resolved) — so a missing included D2 and a missing imported D2 both fail at
  `src-resolve` clauses 1–3, in the same §5.3 bucket. Both are the one hazard
  the walk exists to refuse: a **fabricated "invalid"**.
- **Round 1 took that symmetry too literally, and the reject is the whole
  lesson of the session.** `compose` was changed to DECLINE on any resolver
  error for an unresolved `<include>`/`<override>` target, mirroring
  `importDirective`. Blunt symmetry, and it **overreached**: it surrendered
  **four genuine `src-include` cl. 2.4 passes with no downstream fabricated
  rejection** — `annotA014`, `anyURI_a006_1341`, `schB8`, `schD8`. None of the
  four holds a reference into the missing document, so `parser.Parse` succeeds
  and nothing is fabricated; `schD8`'s target is literally named
  `must%20not%20resolve.xyzzy`, i.e. **the suite's own canonical positive test
  of cl. 2.4 tolerance**. The arbiter's authorized-downward-correction
  precedent (#238, #277) requires the loss to be a genuine false-accept
  correction; this was not one, so the downward move had no authority behind it.
- **The reject came with the fix, not just the objection.** The arbiter
  pinpointed that the fabricated-invalid hazard needs **both halves** — a
  directive that named no document AND a reference into it that then fails
  `src-resolve` clauses 1–3 at finalize — and that the second half is
  observable at exactly one site: `perr` from `parser.Parse` in
  `execSchemaCase`. Decline on the conjunction, never on an unresolved
  `schemaLocation` in isolation.
- **The repair round did that and nothing else.** The walk no longer judges a
  no-D2 directive at all: `compose` and `importDirective` record a new
  scan-scoped `closureScan.unresolved bool` and keep walking, uniformly for all
  three directives (`<include>`, `<override>`, `<import>`) plus the bare
  `<import>` §4.2.6.2 calls legal. The decision moved to
  `conformance/schema.go:443`, `if scan.unresolved && perr != nil`. Two shapes
  deliberately stay **outright** declines: an `<include>`/`<override>` with no
  `schemaLocation` at all (the attribute is required — that is not the spec's
  "fails to resolve" non-error), and a `ReadDocument` error on a D2 that does
  exist (that one would leave a document the parser reads ungated).
- **The silent-nil path was verified, not assumed.** `parser/parse.go:490`'s
  `fetch` returns `nil, nil` **only** for `errors.Is(err, loader.ErrNotFound)`;
  every other resolver failure — permission, transport — still propagates and
  is still caught. So pairing `unresolved` with `perr` cannot swallow a real
  I/O fault.
- **Both permutations are pinned, for all three directive types under both
  polarities.** `TestSchemaExecutorDeclinesUnresolvedDirectiveTarget` keeps the
  reference that breaks the parse and requires a decline;
  the new `TestSchemaExecutorDecidesUnbrokenUnresolvedDirective` drives the
  same shapes with **no** reference into the missing document and requires a
  real decision. The walk-level table gained a `wantUnresolved` column, clear
  on every other case, so the flag is asserted where it should be absent too.
  Mutation-checked both ways: reverting to round 1's blunt decline fails 12
  assertions, reverting to `main`'s unconditional include-admit fails 10 —
  including "unresolved `<include>` target: must be DECLINED regardless of
  `expectValid=false`", the fabricated pass itself.
- **The arbiter did not take the repair on report.** It independently verified
  the `parse.go` `ErrNotFound` claim, re-ran both mutation reversions (8
  assertions failing on each), confirmed `closureScan.unresolved` is
  **non-redundant scan-scoped state** rather than a derivable flag (STYLE D3),
  re-checked every quoted clause against the local spec markdown rather than
  against the grounding, and confirmed `docs/LOG/` was untouched by mason
  (#195).
- **Exported surface: empty diff.** The issue's `## Surface` said "none" and it
  held throughout — everything here is harness-internal to package
  `conformance`, and the only new top-level identifier in the branch is
  `TestSchemaExecutorDecidesUnbrokenUnresolvedDirective`.
- Gate: build/vet/lint/gofmt clean throughout both rounds; full `go test ./...`
  green including `TestConformance`.
- Surprises — **the change that closes a false-accept and the change that
  preserves the matching true-accept are the same edit seen from two sides, and
  round 1 only saw one side.** The issue was framed entirely as "close the
  fabricated-invalid hazard for include too", and a symmetric decline closes it
  perfectly — while quietly costing four cases the suite specifically ships to
  prove the *tolerance* half of cl. 2.4. The general shape worth carrying:
  **when a rule says a condition "is not an error", a harness that declines on
  that condition is asserting the rule rather than testing it.** The conjunction
  works because it declines on the consequence (a broken parse), not on the
  premise (an unresolved location).
- Friction — **none charged against the session's process.** The one repair
  round was review doing its job on a real over-reach, not churn, and the reject
  arrived with a specific site and a specific predicate rather than a direction
  to rethink. Mason's `Ratchet:` trailers were honest in both directions and
  both times: `83a3ccf` predicted `4248 -> 4244` and **named the four cases it
  was about to lose and why they might not be losable**, which is what gave the
  arbiter the reject its own evidence; `80a5b4f` predicted
  `4257/15432 with ZERO regressed, vanished or new` and enumerated the nine
  improvements, which the arbiter's `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` run confirmed **exactly**.
  That is five consecutive sessions of accurate trailers since the #231/#232
  pattern (#235 exact, #344 low-by-one and explained, #331 exact, #240 exact,
  #276 exact in both rounds) — the next `/retro` should now **decide** whether
  the fix enumerated in #232's entry is still worth filing or should be
  dismissed on the record, rather than carrying the observation a sixth time.
- Next:
  1. **[to file — post-land pass] `closureScan.unresolved` is scan-scoped, so
     the conjunction is coarser than the hazard it models.** The flag records
     that SOME directive in the closure yielded no D2, not WHICH one, and it is
     paired with ANY parse failure. A case whose unresolved directive is
     unrelated to whatever actually broke the parse therefore declines too.
     That is the **conservative** direction — an honest decline, never a
     fabricated verdict — and no suite case is known to exhibit it today, which
     is why it was deliberately not tightened here. Correlating the specific
     missing document with the specific `src-resolve` failure would need the
     parse error's rule and target, not a boolean; worth its own issue if a
     later lane movement ever turns out to be blocked by it.
  2. **Still unowned, repeated so the leak stays visible to the follow-up
     audit rather than only in the entries that created it**: #238's two
     follow-ups (the stale-`<xs:redefine>`-premise sweep for the next
     `/retro`; no suite case exercising `extraDocsInClosure`'s decide path —
     **note this issue moved adjacent code and still did not produce one**),
     #309's three non-blocking findings, #277's `STYLE T7`/`STYLE D6`
     citation-drift decision, and #240's foreign-namespace-host prescan
     advisory.
- Thread record (do not duplicate) —
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/276>: the oracle's GROUNDING at
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/276#issuecomment-5152268165> (the
  parallel "not an error" texts, and the `src-resolve` clause 4 argument that
  4.2.1 and 4.2.2 license references identically regardless of resolution), the
  arbiter's round-1 REJECT at
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/276#issuecomment-5152328760> (the
  four surrendered cl. 2.4 passes, the #238/#277 downward-correction precedent
  it fails, and the `perr` conjunction it specifies instead), and the round-2
  ACCEPT at
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/276#issuecomment-5152395402> (the
  independent `parse.go` verification, both mutation reversions, the D3 ruling
  on the new field, and the banked +9). Prior art referenced and not
  re-derived: **#182** (which made `<import>` decline and created the asymmetry
  this issue closes — its nine declined cases are exactly the +9), #238 and
  #277 (the authorized-downward-correction precedent the round-1 form failed to
  meet). This entry is WORKFLOW's "Land" step, written on the WIP branch before

  the #182 import cases; zero regressions)`.

## 2026-08-01 — #241 — parser: `Element.LookupPrefix`/`InScopePrefixes` stop being exported, settling the tree-API convention by disposition 1; the exported surface strictly shrinks by two with zero additions, the ratchet is unchanged BY OBSERVATION rather than by assumption, and the only real wrinkle was an external black-box test calling a method about to disappear

- Shipped on `wip/issue-241` as one commit on top of `87b156d` — `8508ce0`
  (`parser: unexport Element.LookupPrefix/InScopePrefixes — no consumer
  outside package (#241)`, 5 files, +43/−16):
  `parser/tree.go`, `parser/produce.go`, `parser/produce_xpath.go`,
  `parser/document_test.go`, and `parser/tree_internal_test.go` (new, 33
  lines). **Arbiter ACCEPT on round 1, no repair round consumed.**
- **Ratchet: unchanged — no lane moved in either direction.** The arbiter's
  figure, copied not recomputed. Worth recording *how* it was established:
  the arbiter ran `GOXSD_RATCHET=1 go test ./conformance -run TestConformance
  -count=1` (ok, 4.380s) and then checked `git status --porcelain` was
  **empty afterward**, so not one file under
  `conformance/testdata/expectations/` was rewritten. "Pure surface change,
  therefore no movement" is an inference; an empty porcelain after a ratchet
  run is an observation, and only the second is evidence.
- **Spec**: none. The oracle's first finding was that this is **not an XSD
  question at all** — no `xmlschema11-1`, `xmlschema11-2`, `xpath20`,
  `xpath-functions`, or `precisionDecimal` rule applies. The governing text is
  `docs/STYLE.md:114-119` (**T5**, "export nothing without a consumer — a real
  caller, or a documented contract it fulfills") and `docs/PLAN.md:1420-1429`
  ("v1.0 — the stability line": pre-1.0, "interfaces, package boundaries, and
  exported names move freely… the ratchet and the gate are the only
  compatibility promises"). `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:184-209` describes `parser`
  without one mention of `Element`'s tree-query API, so the exported status
  read as an artifact of delegating from `xmltree.StartElement`, not as an
  architected contract.
- **The grounding also corrected the issue's own citation.** #241's Notes
  attributed pre-1.0 mobility to PRINCIPLES.md; it is not there — PRINCIPLES
  has no hit for "pre-1.0", "API mobility", or "unexport". The text lives in
  PLAN.md. A cheap correction, but the kind that silently propagates into the
  next three issue bodies if nobody grounds it.
- **Disposition 1 over disposition 2, and the choice was decided by evidence
  rather than taste.** The issue offered two: unexport, or keep them exported
  and write the convention into `parser/doc.go` as justified by the type's
  role. T5 fails on **both** prongs for these two methods — zero callers
  outside package `parser`, and no doc comment claiming a public contract — so
  disposition 2 would have had to invent the justification it was supposed to
  record. Pre-1.0 mobility makes reversal cheap if a real consumer ever
  appears. The scope stayed exactly where the issue's Notes fenced it: only
  `LookupPrefix`/`InScopePrefixes`, never `Children`/`Name`/attribute access,
  which have a genuine out-of-package caller in `conformance/schema.go`'s
  decidability walk.
- **The one wrinkle, and it is the transferable part of this session: an
  unexport is a call-site fact, not a doc fact.** `parser/document_test.go` is
  `package parser_test` — external black-box — and lines 74-78 asserted
  `root.LookupPrefix("xs")`. That assertion **could not compile** after the
  rename, and no amount of reading `docs/` or `ARCHITECTURE.md` would have
  found it. It was caught by the orchestrator's own pre-grounding grep over
  the whole module, independently confirmed by warden's pre-flight, and
  independently re-swept by the arbiter (`--include=*.go --include=*.md`,
  specs excluded). The oracle had named the hazard in its EDGE CASES before
  any code was written: "mason must grep the whole module (not just docs) for
  any external caller, since T5's 'real caller' test is about actual call
  sites, not just intent." Three independent sweeps found the same one site —
  which is the point. **A repo whose own test suite spans internal and
  external packages will hide an unexport's breakage in the black-box half.**
- **Warden pre-flight specified the diff before mason wrote it, down to the
  identifiers**, and the implementation matched it exactly: exact lowercase
  renames (`lookupPrefix`/`inScopePrefixes`, no semantic renaming), **keep the
  delegators** rather than inlining `e.src.LookupPrefix` at the four call sites
  (inlining would scatter reach-through and detach the §3.3.18 / §3.13.2
  doc comments from any named thing), doc comments verbatim except the leading
  identifier, and two prose cross-references to lowercase in the same diff
  (`parser/tree.go:57`, `parser/produce_xpath.go:56`). Considered and rejected
  at pre-flight: folding both into one `namespaceContext()` — `produce.go`'s
  use is plain QName resolution, unrelated to the XPath-context use, so a
  merged accessor would serve two unrelated callers badly.
- **Exported-surface diff: exactly two removals, zero additions**, and it was
  established twice independently — warden by diffing `go doc -all ./parser`
  against `main`, the arbiter by re-rendering it rather than taking warden's
  word. The surface strictly shrinks, which is the direction T5 wants. **A
  bonus T3 narrowing came free**: `xmltree.Namespace` was `InScopePrefixes`'
  return type and its only appearance in `parser`'s exported surface, so
  `parser` no longer re-exports that xmltree type at all. (`xmltree.Attribute`
  still leaks via `Element.Attributes` — out of scope here, correctly left
  alone.)
- **The moved test pins wiring, not algorithm.** `parser/tree_internal_test.go`
  is `package parser` with a why-internal header stating the T5 rationale and
  the boundary it keeps: that `ReadDocument` hands each `Element` the
  `xmltree.StartElement` whose scope the delegator reads — unobservable from
  `xmltree`, and unchanged by the unexport — while
  `parser/xmltree/scope_test.go` keeps owning the scope-resolution algorithm
  itself. No direct test was added for `inScopePrefixes`, deliberately:
  `parser/produce_xpath_test.go:392-397` already drives it through the real
  production path with locally-declared + inherited + implicit `xml` bindings,
  prefix-sort order, and the default-namespace exclusion all asserted at once.
  Warden's verdict on that call is worth keeping: a direct unit test there
  would have been **the T4 duplication, not the omission**.
- **The arbiter verified the new test can fail rather than assuming it.** In a
  throwaway worktree it mutated `lookupPrefix` to return `"", false` instead of
  delegating, confirmed `TestElementLookupPrefixDelegatesToStartTag` fails with
  a message naming both the got and the want, then removed the worktree and
  re-verified the tree clean. A test added alongside an unexport is exactly the
  kind that can pass vacuously; this one was shown to observe the delegation it
  claims to pin.
- Gate: `go build ./...`, `go test ./...`, `go vet ./...`,
  `golangci-lint run` (`0 issues.`), and
  `go test ./conformance -run TestConformance -count=1` (ok, 3.681s) all green
  at `8508ce0`, judged against freshly-fetched `origin/main` (`87b156d`) with
  `git status --porcelain` empty before and after.
- Surprises — **none in the code; the one surprise is in the acceptance
  criteria.** #241's Acceptance says "Pick **one** disposition, apply it, and
  write it down where the next session will find it… Either way the outcome is
  a stated rule, so the next warden pre-flight on a `parser.Element` method has
  something to test against instead of re-litigating." Disposition 1 was
  applied in full, but the *rule* is now stated only in the issue thread and in
  this entry — nothing in `parser/doc.go` or `docs/STYLE.md` says what the
  convention for `parser.Element`'s residual method set is. In practice the
  residual set is justified (warden and the arbiter both confirmed `Name`,
  `Attributes`, `Parent`, `Children`, `BaseURI`, `Loc` are **exactly** what
  `conformance/schema.go` consumes — no over- or under-shoot), so T5 holds on
  the real-caller prong for every one of them. But "holds when re-derived" is
  what this issue existed to stop. Carried to Next rather than fixed here: the
  chronicler records, and this is a scope call for the cartographer.
- Friction — **none charged against the session's process.** Grounding →
  warden pre-flight → implement → warden diff review → arbiter accept, no
  rejections, no repair round, five files. The pre-flight bought the whole
  saving: mason implemented a diff whose every identifier, kept delegator, and
  prose cross-reference had already been named, and warden's follow-up diff
  review returned zero findings. **The measurable cost of that ordering is one
  extra review turn; the measurable benefit is that the `document_test.go`
  compile break was known before a line was written rather than discovered by a
  red build.** Also worth banking: mason's `Ratchet: unchanged` trailer was
  accurate, which is the **sixth consecutive** accurate trailer since the
  #231/#232 pattern (#235, #344 low-by-one and explained, #331, #240, #276 both
  rounds, now #241) — see Next item 2, which the previous entry already asked
  the next `/retro` to decide rather than observe a seventh time.
- Next:
  1. **[to file — post-land pass] The "stated rule" half of #241's Acceptance
     is unmet.** One sentence in `parser/doc.go` naming the convention for
     `parser.Element`'s remaining exported methods — justified by
     `conformance/schema.go`'s tree walk, and honestly stating there is no
     other external consumer today and they may be unexported before 1.0 —
     would close it. This is also **libuser's P5 from the 2026-07-25
     `/backlog`**, which widened the question to the whole method set
     (`Parent`/`Children`/`Attributes`/`BaseURI` as well) and asked for a doc
     that answers "should I depend on this?" plainly. That ask was conditional
     on disposition 2 in libuser's framing, but it survives disposition 1
     intact for the four methods that stayed. Small, real, and now the only
     thing between #241 and the convention it set out to write down.
  2. **[carried, still unowned] `/retro` must DECIDE on the trailer-accuracy
     fix enumerated in #232's entry** — file it or dismiss it on the record.
     The previous entry (#276) asked for exactly this so it would not be
     carried a sixth time; it has now been carried a seventh. A recurring
     observation with no disposition is the follow-up-ledger leak the retro
     audit exists to catch.
  3. **[carried, still unowned] The standing leak list, repeated so it stays
     visible to the follow-up audit rather than only in the entries that
     created it**: #238's two follow-ups (the stale-`<xs:redefine>`-premise
     sweep; no suite case exercising `extraDocsInClosure`'s decide path),
     #309's three non-blocking findings, #277's `STYLE T7`/`STYLE D6`
     citation-drift decision, #240's foreign-namespace-host prescan advisory,
     and #276's scan-scoped `closureScan.unresolved` coarseness note. **None of
     these has a tracking issue.** This entry adds no new unowned advisory of
     its own: the arbiter's one non-blocking note here (no direct internal test
     for `inScopePrefixes`) is **dismissed on the record**, not carried — it is
     covered transitively by `produce_xpath_test.go` and by
     `parser/xmltree/scope_test.go`, and both reviewers ruled a direct test
     would be duplication.
  4. **[post-land pass] `docs/PLAN.md` priority group 5** names "#215 + #241
     (exported-surface tightening)" at line 373 and #241 again in the
     2026-07-25 backlog narrative at line 282. #241 lands here, leaving #215 as
     the sole remaining exported-surface-tightening slice; the post-land pass
     should reflect that.
- Thread record (do not duplicate) —
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/241>: libuser's independent P5
  confirmation from the 2026-07-25 `/backlog` at
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/241#issuecomment-5078470188> (an

## 2026-08-01 — #246 — process: a commit whose index is staler than the working tree the gate verified is now mechanically refused; `.githooks/pre-commit` blocks the exact `AM` shape that took `main` red at `547b42f`, WORKFLOW step 1 activates it every session because local git config does not survive an ephemeral container, and the arbiter accepted on round 1 after re-deriving the hook's behavior in its own scratch repos

- Shipped on `wip/issue-246` as two commits on top of `aac316e` — `8b2d39b`
  (`process: refuse commits with a stale index; document merge-conflict
  re-staging (#246)`, 2 files, +119/−5) and `5a6e05c` (`docs/tooling: fix PR
  misattribution and quoting comment, pin --porcelain=v1 (#246)`, 2 files,
  +6/−4, folding in the arbiter's three non-blocking nits). Branch total 2
  files, +125/−9: `.githooks/pre-commit` (new, 74 lines) and
  `docs/WORKFLOW.md`. **Arbiter ACCEPT on round 1, no repair round
  consumed.**
- **Ratchet: unchanged — a verified no-op, zero expectation files touched,
  nothing banked.** The arbiter's figure, copied not recomputed, and
  established the way #241's was: `GOXSD_RATCHET=1 go test ./conformance -run
  TestConformance -count=1` ran green, and `git status --porcelain` was
  **empty afterward**, so not one file under
  `conformance/testdata/expectations/` was rewritten. "No Go code, therefore
  no movement" would have been an inference; an empty porcelain after a
  ratchet run is an observation. Second consecutive session where the
  distinction was honored rather than assumed.
- **Spec: n/a — and the grounding comment says so explicitly rather than
  silently omitting it.** This is repo process/tooling; no
  `xmlschema11-1`, `xmlschema11-2`, `xpath20`, `xpath-functions`, or
  `precisionDecimal` rule applies, so **oracle grounding was skipped on
  purpose** and the skip is on the record in the GROUNDING comment. Worth
  keeping the habit: a missing GROUNDING and a deliberately-declined one look
  identical in the thread unless the decline is written down.
- **The repo survey is what picked the design, and it is the transferable
  part.** No `.github/workflows/` exists (no CI build on PR heads today), no
  committed hook infra (`.git/hooks/` samples-only, no `.githooks/`,
  `core.hooksPath` unset), and every session runs in a fresh ephemeral clone.
  That last fact rules out a one-time setup instruction outright: **local git
  config does not survive the container**, so a hook is only mechanical if its
  activation rides a step that already executes unconditionally. `docs/
  WORKFLOW.md` step 1 (Survey) is that step, and `git config core.hooksPath
  .githooks` is idempotent, so it can be run every session with no state to
  track and nothing to remember.
- **What the hook actually tests, stated precisely because the precision is
  the whole guard**: `git status --porcelain=v1` emits `XY path`, X = index vs
  HEAD, Y = worktree vs index. The hook fails the commit when a path has
  **both** a staged status (X in `M A R C D`) **and** a further unstaged
  status on top of it (Y in `M D`) — exactly "staged, then edited again, never
  re-staged". Unmerged paths (`DD AU UD UA DU AA UU`) are skipped so git's own
  conflict error stays the reporter; untracked files and purely-unstaged edits
  are not the hazard and are not flagged. `docs/WORKFLOW.md` gains a matching
  **"Merge-conflict resolution"** subsection: resolve → `git add -u` (re-stage
  every touched file, not just the conflicted ones, and always after the LAST
  edit) → `git diff --cached --stat` as a **named, mandatory** step of the
  ritual → commit.
- **The doc says out loud why the gate could not have caught this**, which is
  the sentence the next session actually needs: `go build`, `go test`,
  `go vet`, `golangci-lint run`, the conformance suite, and the arbiter's
  review all read the WORKING TREE; **nothing in them reads the index**. A
  green gate therefore says nothing about what is staged. At `547b42f` a
  merge-conflict resolution staged `parser/produce_xpath.go` cleanly via the
  merge, a one-line follow-up fix (#179 had turned the free function
  `resolveQName` into a method) was made in the tree afterward and never
  re-staged, and `git commit --no-edit` committed the stale index — silently
  reverting the fix while every check honestly reported green.
- **The arbiter refused to take mason's verification on trust, and re-derived
  it.** Mason had exercised 8 scenarios in a scratch repo plus a real-repo
  reproduction of the `AM` shape; the arbiter rebuilt the matrix in its own
  scratch repos rather than reading mason's table — `M␣`/`R␣` allowed;
  `MM`/`AM`/`MD`/`RM` blocked; `??`/`␣M`/`UU`/dirty-submodule not flagged;
  C-quoted paths with spaces and non-ASCII bytes blocked with the path intact
  — then ran the end-to-end merge case (conflict resolved, follow-up fix to a
  cleanly-merged file, hook blocks, `git add -u` lets it through with the fix
  present). It also **rebuilt `547b42f` in a detached worktree** to confirm the
  doc's factual anchor rather than quoting the issue body:
  `parser/produce_xpath.go:164:14: undefined: resolveQName`. A guard is a
  claim about behavior under conditions that do not exist yet; the only
  evidence for it is someone other than its author making those conditions
  happen.
- **Two silent-but-harmless paths were identified and ruled not-a-gap, with
  the reason recorded**: `git commit -a` (git re-stages into a temp index
  before the hook runs, so the committed content *is* the working tree) and
  partial `git commit -- <path>` (git takes worktree content for the named
  paths, ignoring the index). Neither can carry stale content, so the hook's
  silence there is correct rather than a hole. `sh -n` clean, correct exit
  codes under both dash and bash, silent + rc=0 on a clean tree.
  `shellcheck` is unavailable in this container, so no static lint was
  possible and behavior was established empirically instead.
- **Mason raised two items at hand-off and the arbiter dismissed both on the
  record.** (1) *No documented `--no-verify` escape hatch* — keep it
  undocumented: anyone who needs the bypass already knows the flag, and
  printing it in the very document prescribing the discipline advertises the
  hole to precisely the reader who should not take it. (2) *Activation is
  advisory-by-construction* — inherent to local hooks in an ephemeral, CI-less
  repo, and correctly scoped out; the prose does the honest thing and **states
  the limitation** ("only in a session that ran step 1's `git config
  core.hooksPath .githooks`, so the discipline stays yours too") instead of
  overselling the guard.
- **All three arbiter nits were folded in before landing rather than carried**,
  and each was a real correction: (1) `docs/WORKFLOW.md:270` read "landed at
  `547b42f` through a squash-merged PR (#246)" — `547b42f` landed under
  **#179**'s PR; #246 is the *harvesting* issue, and the hook's own header
  already said it precisely, so the prose was made to match. (2) The hook's
  positional-parse comment claimed it worked "so paths containing spaces
  survive intact" — git **C-quotes** such paths (`MM "my file.txt"`), so the
  stated reason was wrong even though the parse was right; the real reason is
  that the two status columns are fixed-width and always the first two
  characters, so the parse does not care what git does to the path after them.
  (3) `git status --porcelain` was pinned to `--porcelain=v1`, so a future
  default-format flip cannot silently turn the hook into a no-op. Mason
  re-verified the hook and re-ran the full gate after these edits.
- **STYLE: none of it applies, and the arbiter said so rather than staying
  silent.** S1–S3/E1–E3/D1–D5/T1–T5/P1–P4/L1 govern Go; **zero `.go` files
  changed**, so the T5 exported-surface diff is trivially empty. No
  conformance or expectations files touched — the issue's `Surface: none`
  holds as written.
- Gate: `go build ./...`, `go test ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `golangci-lint run`
  (**0 issues.**) and `go test ./conformance -run TestConformance -count=1`
  (ok, 5.99s) all green at `8b2d39b`, judged against freshly-fetched
  `origin/main` (`aac316e`) with `git status --porcelain` empty and the branch
  a strict fast-forward; re-run green after the nit fixes at `5a6e05c`.
- Surprises — **one, and it is a pleasant one: the fix is smaller than the
  failure suggested.** The `547b42f` breakage looked like it wanted CI, or a
  land-time diff protocol, or a rewrite of the checkpoint ritual. It wanted 74
  lines of `sh` and a two-status-column test, because the failure mode has an
  exact mechanical signature in `git status` output and that signature is
  cheap to name. The **less** pleasant half is what the survey turned up:
  `.git/hooks/` was samples-only and `core.hooksPath` was unset, meaning this
  repo has been running with **no index-level guard of any kind** for its whole
  history, and the only reason `547b42f` is the first known instance is that
  merge-conflict resolution is rare here.
- Friction — **none charged against the session's process.** Grounding
  (declined, on the record) → implement → arbiter accept → nit fold-in →
  land, two files, no rejections, no repair round. The one process
  observation worth banking is the fold-in itself: the arbiter's three nits
  were explicitly non-blocking and could have been carried as advisory notes
  into the follow-up ledger, where — on this repo's own recent record — they
  would have sat unowned. Folding them into `5a6e05c` while the branch was
  still open cost one mason turn and one gate re-run, and **closed three
  ledger entries that would otherwise have been opened.** Also banked: both
  commits' `Ratchet: unchanged` trailers were accurate, the **seventh
  consecutive** accurate trailer since the #231/#232 pattern (#235, #344
  low-by-one and explained, #331, #240, #276 both rounds, #241, now #246) —
  which makes Next item 2 below the eighth carry of a question that has never
  been decided.
- Next:
  1. **[new, unfiled — the one advisory this session adds] A pre-merge CI build
     on the PR head is a strictly stronger guard than this hook, and remains
     unfiled.** The arbiter and mason independently reached the same
     conclusion, and #246's own Notes raised it ("may be the cheaper guard than
     a local hook — worth weighing both"). The hook is advisory by
     construction: a session that skips WORKFLOW step 1 gets no protection at
     all, and nothing can make a *local* hook mandatory in an ephemeral,
     CI-less clone. CI on the PR head has none of that dependence — it reads
     the pushed tree, which is by definition the tree that will land. This is
     correctly **out of #246's scope** (the repo has no `.github/workflows/`
     at all, so it is a new capability, not a fix), and it is stated here as a
     tracked advisory rather than silently dropped. **A tracking issue, or an
     explicit dismissal, belongs to the cartographer or the next `/retro`.**
  2. **[carried, still unowned — eighth carry] `/retro` must DECIDE on the
     trailer-accuracy fix enumerated in #232's entry** — file it or dismiss it
     on the record. #276's entry asked for a disposition so it would not be
     carried a sixth time; #241's noted it had reached a seventh; this is the
     eighth, against a now seven-session unbroken run of accurate trailers,
     which is itself evidence the retro could act on. **A recurring observation
     with no disposition is exactly the follow-up-ledger leak the retro audit
     exists to catch, and it is now the longest-lived one on the list.**
  3. **[carried, still unowned] The standing leak list, repeated so it stays
     visible to the follow-up audit rather than only in the entries that
     created it**: #241's unmet "stated rule" half (one sentence in
     `parser/doc.go` naming the convention for `parser.Element`'s residual
     exported methods, which is also libuser's P5 from the 2026-07-25
     `/backlog`), #238's two follow-ups (the stale-`<xs:redefine>`-premise
     sweep; no suite case exercising `extraDocsInClosure`'s decide path),
     #309's three non-blocking findings, #277's `STYLE T7`/`STYLE D6`
     citation-drift decision, #240's foreign-namespace-host prescan advisory,
     and #276's scan-scoped `closureScan.unresolved` coarseness note. **None of
     these has a tracking issue.** Item 1 above joins the list as its newest
     member.
  4. **[post-land pass] `.githooks/` is new infrastructure and nothing outside
     `docs/WORKFLOW.md` knows it exists.** The post-land pass should decide
     whether `CLAUDE.md`'s Commands block — which today lists the gate
     commands and nothing about hooks — should name the activation, since a
     session that reads `CLAUDE.md` and not `docs/WORKFLOW.md` step 1 is
     precisely the unprotected case item 1 describes.
- Thread record (do not duplicate) —
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/246>: the GROUNDING comment at
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/246#issuecomment-5153693276> (the
  deliberate oracle skip with its reason, the repo survey establishing no CI /
  no hook infra / no surviving local config, and the step-1 activation
  argument), and the arbiter's round-1 ACCEPT at
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/246#issuecomment-5153734634> (the
  independently re-derived scenario matrix, the `547b42f` rebuild confirming
  `undefined: resolveQName`, the two harmless-silence paths, the dismissal of
  mason's two flagged items, the empty-porcelain ratchet observation, and the
  three nits folded into `5a6e05c`). Prior art referenced and not re-derived:
  **#179** (whose merge-conflict resolution produced `547b42f`), **#244** (the
  one-line code fix, filed and closed at `cc0397b` — which is why the process
  half needed #246 as a carrier or it would have leaked), and **#195** (the
  same family: a failure invisible to every green check, catchable only by
  deliberately inspecting a different artifact than the one the tools read).
  This entry is WORKFLOW's "Land" step, written on the WIP branch after the
  verdict and before the PR is opened so it rides in the squash.

## 2026-08-02 — #264 — xsd: `cos-ct-extends` is charged over extension-derived complex types, including the `<simpleContent><extension>` simple-base case the restriction twin has no analogue for; the warden's five pre-flight changes were all followed, six parser fixtures turned out to be invalid schemas the new check correctly rejects, and the arbiter accepted on round 1 with the ratchet deliberately unmoved

- Shipped on `wip/issue-264` as one commit on top of `1eeb907` — `a5f1d9f`
  (`xsd: enforce cos-ct-extends over extension-derived complex types (#264)`,
  8 files, +1445/−75): `xsd/complexextension.go` (new, 786 lines),
  `xsd/complexextension_test.go` (new, 484 lines), `xsd/complexderivation.go`,
  `xsd/complextype.go`, `xsd/resolve.go`, `parser/produce_extension_test.go`,
  `conformance/schema.go`, `conformance/schema_closure.go`. **Arbiter ACCEPT on
  the first round, no repair round consumed.**
- **Ratchet: unchanged — schema 4257 pass / 11175 fail (15432 cases),
  datatypes 1107 pass / 22 fail (1129), instance 0 / 26426, xpath/json/ber 0
  cases.** The arbiter's figures, copied not recomputed. `GOXSD_RATCHET=1`
  wrote NOTHING — `git status` empty after the ratchet run, no expectations
  file touched by the commit, zero movement in either direction. **This is the
  planned outcome, not a shortfall**: `conformance/schema.go`'s
  `complexTypeDecidable` still declines every `<extension>` shape outright, so
  the lane has nothing for `cos-ct-extends` to judge. The issue body's original
  "Ratchet: **moves the `schema` lane**" promise was corrected on the thread
  before work started (the unblock comment of 2026-07-31), which rehomed the
  lane widening to **#336**; the arbiter verified from the diff, not from the
  report, that `complexTypeDecidable`'s **logic is untouched** and only its
  status prose was reconciled. Legal per "scores only move up", not "must move".
- **What landed, clause by clause.** Case 1: **1.1** (the `{final}` flag),
  **1.2 / `c-cte`**, **1.4 in all three branches** (simple / empty /
  complex-content, including the open-content conditions 1.4.3.2.2.3 and
  1.4.3.2.2.4), **1.5's pure-extension chain**, and **1.6 / `c-vs-ctd-e`**.
  Case 2 — the simple-type base, clauses 2.1 and 2.2 — landed as an **explicit
  branch**, which is the whole reason this is not a copy of its restriction
  twin. `cos-particle-extend` (§3.9.6.2) is charged as **its own
  `xsderr.Error`, and only on the 1.4.3.2.2.2 arm**.
- **The warden's five required pre-flight changes were all followed, and the
  arbiter verified each against the diff rather than the hand-off report** —
  two of them by mutation in a scratch worktree.
  1. **[D4/T1] No visited set.** c-cte's "properties, recursively, are
     identical" walk bottoms out **by construction**: every reference-valued
     slot (`AttributeDeclarationRef`, `ElementDeclarationRef`, `ModelGroupRef`,
     `{type definition}`) stops at `sameTypeDefinition` / expanded-name
     component identity, and the only descending recursion is
     `modelGroupsIdentical` over an inline `{particles}` tree that follows no
     reference. "Identical" for a component-valued property **is** component
     identity, not structural equality — finite by that reading and unbounded
     by any other. Grep-confirmed: no `seen`, no `visited`, no map in the file.
  2. **[T4] One new file, named for the relation, not for "component
     identity".** `sameTypeDefinition` already claims that phrase and
     §3.8.6.3's `typeTablesEquivalent`/`typeAlternativesEquivalent` is a third,
     spec-distinct notion of sameness; the predicates sit beside their sole
     consumer with the boundary against **both** neighbours written down.
  3. **[T1/E2] The simple-base arm is a branch, not a skip.**
     `checkComplexTypeRestriction` returns nil for a simple base because
     `ct-props-correct` clause 2 already charges it; cos-ct-extends has a live
     clause 2 for exactly that case, so the reflex copy would have **silently
     deleted half the constraint**. The arbiter replaced the arm with
     `return nil` in a scratch worktree — 3 of `TestCosCTExtendsClause2`'s 4
     rows fail. The check is live.
  4. **[T5] `extensionBlockingKeywords` is a named EMPTY set, never a bare
     `nil`.** Clause 1.6 is ·without limitation· = `key-val-sub-type-absolute`
     (§3.4.6.4, `xmlschema11-1.md:1339`), and the two obvious ways to get it
     backwards are both **false rejects**: leaving `restrictionBlockingKeywords`
     in the call rejects every valid case, since an extension's locally
     declared type is normally extension-derived from the base's; and a
     copy-pasted message still naming `{extension, list, union}` lies about
     the rule. The two existing walks in `complexderivation.go` were
     **parameterized** (`blocked` + rule + relation label) rather than forked
     into a fifth and sixth near-identical walk. Mutation-checked: swapping in
     `restrictionBlockingKeywords` fails `TestCosCTExtendsClause16`'s passing
     row and nothing else. The arbiter separately confirmed the refactor
     reproduces restriction's pre-refactor message **character for character**
     on both the attribute and the element half.
  5. **[scope] Clause 1.5's mixed chain is `xsd`'s problem, GAP'd — not routed
     to the parser.** Synthesizing the collapsed intermediate is a
     finalize-phase derivation over assembled components; the parser maps XML
     to components and must not synthesize a component that corresponds to no
     XML. The pure chain is decided by a **proof, not a construction** — walk
     `{base type definition}` to the ancestor whose base is ·xs:anyType·, and
     if every step is an extension the Note's reordering is the identity, the
     collapse IS T, and the residual restriction is T-to-T.
- **Three GAP(xsd) markers, all fail-open, and the excuse for 1.3 is not the
  obvious one.** Clauses **1.3** (attribute-wildcard `cos-ns-subset`) and
  **1.7** (the assertions prefix) are deferred because no producer performs
  §3.4.2.4 / §3.4.2.5 / §3.4.2.1's folds of the base's wildcard and assertions
  into the derived type's — charging either over unfolded components is a
  **false reject**, not a missing relation: `wildcardSubset` already exists and
  is named as already-usable in the marker. The cheap "B has a wildcard and T
  has none" half of 1.3 is deliberately **not** charged, because with no fold
  that half is precisely the false reject. Both name **#265** as the retiring
  issue. The third marker is **clause 1.5's mixed extension/restriction chain**.
- **Surprise, and it is the substantive one: six existing parser fixtures were
  invalid schemas.** `parser/produce_extension_test.go` had been exercising
  mappings only reachable on schemas no complete processor accepts. Three were
  **repaired to stay valid** — `TestProduceExtensionAllBaseWithEmptyExplicitContent`
  (base made `mixed="true"`, per 1.4.3.2.2.1),
  `TestProduceExtensionAllPlusAllMerges` (base `<all minOccurs="0">`, since
  `cos-particle-extend` 3.1 requires `E.{min occurs} = B.{min occurs}`, so the
  old fixture was **unsatisfiable**), and `TestProduceExtensionOfAnyType`
  (`mixed="true"`, per the oracle addendum's no-exemption ruling). Three now
  **pin the rejection** instead. This was the highest-risk item in the diff and
  the arbiter checked all three **three ways** — spec derivation, mutation, and
  suite metadata — rather than reading the hand-off: disabling
  `checkComplexTypeExtension` wholesale fails exactly those three subtests and
  nothing else in `./parser`, so they could not have passed before; and
  `msData/complexType/ctZ010a.xsd` and `ctZ010d.xsd` (mixed/element-only
  mismatch **with** a local particle) both carry
  `<expected validity="invalid"/>` in `msMeta/ComplexType_w3c.xml`, while
  `ctZ010.xsd` (`mixed="false"` over `xs:anyType`, **no** local particle) is
  valid via §3.4.2.3.3 clause 4.2.2. Genuinely spec-invalid, not a masked
  regression. `TestProduceExtensionAllPlusAllMerges` does lose one bit of
  discriminating power — `minOccurs` can no longer separate base from effective
  content — and no valid schema can restore it; the test's own doc says so.
- **The oracle's addendum settled the `xs:anyType` question the other way from
  mason's worry, and the answer is "no special case".** Unlike
  `derivation-ok-restriction` clause 2.1, cos-ct-extends has **no** anyType
  exemption anywhere, and the asymmetry is intentional: restricting *away from*
  the ur-type must always be permitted, but extending a mixed type by adding
  real element-only content is a narrowing-in-disguise. The case mason feared
  over-rejecting is resolved **one level up**, before cos-ct-extends is ever
  evaluated, by the base-agnostic §3.4.2.3.3 clause 4.2.2 — no local particle ⇒
  the base's whole `{content type}` is copied verbatim, `{variety}` included,
  overriding whatever `mixed` says. So `mixed="false"` on an attributes-only
  extension is simply inert.
- **The "src-ct (§3.4.3) remainder" half of the issue was EMPTY, and that was
  said on the thread rather than shipped as silence.** Clauses 1, 3, 4 and 5
  are already enforced in `parser/produce_complex.go` under `ruleSrcCT`; clause
  2 is **unreachable, not skipped**, because `produceSimpleContent` declines
  `<simpleContent><restriction>` outright, so a check written today would be
  dead code no test could exercise except on input the producer refuses. It
  belongs to whichever issue lifts that limitation. The issue body's paraphrase
  of clause 3 ("`<extension>` ⇒ must have an `<attributeGroup ref>` child") is
  also simply **wrong** — clauses 3 and 4 are both about `<openContent>`'s
  `<any>` child, and #230 landed them. Oracle, warden and mason reached this
  independently; the correction lives in a SCOPE NOTE comment because the body
  is not safely editable through this session's channel.
- **Zero new exported identifiers.** `go doc -all ./xsd` diffed against
  `origin/main`: the *only* delta is reflowed doc-comment prose on
  `ComplexType`. No `xsderr` catalog edit — `cos-ct-extends` and
  `cos-particle-extend` were already cataloged, `c-cte`/`c-vs-ctd-e` correctly
  stay OUT per `irregularRules`' own doc and the `c-ran`/`c-vs-ctd-r`
  precedent, and `cos-ct-extends.1.2` (an artifact of rulecat matching
  Appendix B's prose) never appears as a `Rule` value. The issue's acceptance
  bullet asking for `rulecat` additions is therefore satisfied by **not**
  making them.
- Gate: `go build ./...`, `go test ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `golangci-lint run`
  (**0 issues**) and `go test ./conformance -run TestConformance -count=1`
  (ok, 4.99s) all green at `a5f1d9f`, judged against freshly-fetched
  `origin/main` (`1eeb907`) with `git status --porcelain` empty.
- Friction — **none charged against the session's process.** Ground → design
  pre-flight → warden APPROVE WITH CHANGES (five, all met) → implement →
  arbiter accept, one commit, no rejections, no repair round. The one thing
  worth banking is what the **design-before-code pre-flight bought**: three of
  the warden's five findings (the visited set, the reflex simple-base skip, and
  `restrictionBlockingKeywords` left in the clause-1.6 call) were **defects in
  a plan, caught before a line was written**, and two of the three were
  false-reject or silently-deleted-constraint class — the kind that passes a
  green gate. Also banked: the `Ratchet: unchanged` trailer is accurate, the
  **eighth consecutive** accurate trailer since the #231/#232 pattern (#235,
  #344, #331, #240, #276, #241, #246, now #264), which is the ninth carry of
  Next item 3 below.
- **Advisory finding #2, recorded so a later reader does not misread it.**
  `checkExtensionTwoStepDerivable`'s two branches both `return nil`, so
  `pureExtensionChain`'s verdict is **discarded**. That is an inevitable
  consequence of the warden's ruling — pure chain ⇒ the clause holds by the
  proof; mixed chain ⇒ fail-open GAP — not a deviation from it, and the
  predicate is exercised in both directions by
  `TestCosCTExtendsClause15PureChain`. It is not a dead check; it is a check
  whose two answers happen to agree today.
- Next:
  1. **[post-land pass — REQUIRED AT LAND by the arbiter, and the one loose end
     in an otherwise clean accept] File the retiring `xsd` issue for the
     clause-1.5 mixed-chain `GAP(xsd)` marker** at
     `xsd/complexextension.go:353-367`. This is advisory finding #1: the marker
     says the follow-up "should be filed as a follow-up xsd issue" and **no
     such issue exists** (searched, and re-confirmed at this entry's writing) —
     every other GAP marker in the tree names its retiring issue, so this is
     the sole exception and a STYLE P3 hole. The issue should decide the
     collapsed intermediate per the §3.4.6.2 Note's reordering recipe and note
     the `contentTypeRestricts` open-content early return
     (`contentrestricts.go:219-231`) it would inherit. **The next landing that
     touches `xsd/complexextension.go` repoints the `:353` marker at that
     number.** This is deferred to step 7, explicitly not dropped.
  2. **[the natural successor — already filed] #336** ("conformance: narrow
     `complexTypeDecidable` to admit the two extension forms and measure the
     schema lane"), which is `blocked` on this issue and **#265**. This is the
     landing that actually exercises the code shipped here against the ratchet;
     until it lands, `cos-ct-extends` is proved only by structural tests, the
     same way #228's slice was. #265 — the §3.4.2.4/§3.4.2.5 attribute-side
     folds — is the other half of #336's unblocking, and is also what retires
     the clauses 1.3 and 1.7 GAP markers.
  3. **[carried, still unowned — ninth carry] `/retro` must DECIDE on the
     trailer-accuracy fix enumerated in #232's entry** — file it or dismiss it
     on the record, against a now eight-session unbroken run of accurate
     trailers, which is itself evidence the retro could act on.
  4. **[carried, still unowned] The standing leak list, repeated so the
     follow-up audit sees it and not only the entries that created it**:
     #246's pre-merge-CI advisory, #241's unmet "stated rule" half, #238's two
     follow-ups, #309's three non-blocking findings, #277's `STYLE T7`/`D6`
     citation-drift decision, #240's foreign-namespace-host prescan advisory,
     and #276's scan-scoped `closureScan.unresolved` coarseness note. **None
     has a tracking issue.** Item 1 above is NOT on this list — it has a named
     owner (step 7 of this session) and an arbiter "required at land".
- Thread record (do not duplicate) —
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/264>: the unblock + **acceptance
  correction** at
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/264#issuecomment-5142541803> (why
  #228 could not narrow the lane and the rehoming to #336), the oracle's
  GROUNDING at
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/264#issuecomment-5153973022>
  (verbatim cos-ct-extends and cos-particle-extend, the src-ct clause-3

  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/264#issuecomment-5154015864> (the

## 2026-08-02 — #249 — xsd: the substitution-group membership relation stops being two approximations and becomes ONE exact predicate deciding `cos-equiv-derived-ok-rec` clauses 1, 2.1, 2.2 and 2.3; the arbiter rejected round 1 for a ·derivation· walk that ABANDONED at a simple type instead of stepping through it, and the repair turned the file's deleted `GAP(subst)` markers into an honest `GAP(xsd)` naming a rule this repo enforces nowhere
