## 2026-08-02 — #265 — xsd: `derivation-ok-restriction` clause 3's WILDCARD half is charged via `cos-ns-subset`, and §3.4.2.5 clause 2.2's extension base-wildcard `cos-aw-union` becomes a materialised finalize-time fold mirroring #401's; a three-part issue that looked park-shaped turned out to be one fold plus two small charges because two of its three parts were pure spec-reading rulings, and the arbiter rejected round 1 for a REPRODUCIBLE false reject disclosed in honest prose carrying no `GAP(` token

- Shipped on `wip/issue-265` as three commits on top of `a28fcc5` (#253) —
  `f0e68c7` (`xsd: decide cos-ns-subset for derivation-ok-restriction clause 3's
  wildcard half, and fold §3.4.2.5 clause 2.2's extension base wildcard at
  finalize (#265)`), `d2643e4` (the comment-only repair round), and `5c5e778`
  (the arbiter's ratchet bank) — **11 files, +750/−90**:
  `xsd/attributewildcardfold.go` (new, 254 lines),
  `xsd/attributewildcardfold_test.go` (new, 266 lines),
  `xsd/complexderivation.go` (+80/−15), `xsd/complexextension.go`,
  `xsd/complextype.go`, `xsd/resolve.go`, `xsd/contentrestricts.go`,
  `xsd/defaultbinding.go`, `xsd/namespaceconstraint_union.go`,
  `conformance/schema.go`, and `conformance/testdata/expectations/schema.txt`.
  **One arbiter repair round, ACCEPT on round 2.** §2 of the issue is not here:
  it split to **#401** and landed separately at `b5b5598` earlier the same day.
- **Ratchet: `schema` 4257 -> 4263 (+6), `datatypes` 1107 unchanged, `instance`
  0 unchanged, 0 regressed — RUN AND BANKED at `5c5e778`.** The arbiter's
  figures, copied verbatim from the final verdict and not recomputed. The banked
  diff is `6 insertions(+), 6 deletions(-)`, **all `fail` → `pass`, none
  downward**, out of 15432 `schema` cases:
  `MS-ComplexType2006-07-15/ctO005`, `ctO007`, `saxonData/Wild/wild020`,
  `wild021`, `wild022`, `wild057`. Every one is a previously-*passing* invalid
  schema now correctly REJECTED by clause 3's wildcard half — `ctO005` (base has
  no `<anyAttribute>`, restriction declares `##other`), `ctO007` (`##other` →
  `##any`, widening), `wild020/021/022` (each commented *"invalid wildcard
  restriction, restricted type allows more namespaces than base type"*), and
  `wild057` (base `notQName="##defined"`, restriction `notQName="zang zong"` —
  `cos-ns-subset`'s conjunctive tail condition 2, `defined` not carried down).
  Both mason and the arbiter read all six schemas rather than reasoning from the
  direction; the arbiter re-derived the counts independently in two throwaway
  worktrees against the same suite submodule (`7bc3365`) before believing them.
- **The feared 27-case MS-ComplexType collision did not occur, exactly as the
  grounding predicted.** `ctG007`–`ctG069` and `ctO003` produced **no diff lines
  at all** in either round, and the read-only run stayed green — a stronger
  statement than "no diff", since a regression there would have surfaced as a
  `Regressed` failure. This thread had carried a *"ground it with the oracle and
  post the verdict here BEFORE implementing"* warning since 2026-07-26, on the
  strength of #262 having regressed those 27 cases when it charged
  `loc-testSubP` clause 3's strict branch on the letter.
- **The change, exactly.**
  1. **`xsd/complexderivation.go`** — new unexported
     `checkRestrictionAttributeWildcard` (`:434`), called from
     `checkRestrictionAttributes` (`:378`) *between* the `{attribute uses}` loop
     (cvc-complex-type clause 2.1) and `checkRestrictionRequiredAttributes`
     (clause 3), i.e. in cvc-complex-type's own order. It consumes the
     pre-existing `wildcardSubset` (`xsd/namespaceconstraint_subset.go`, #263)
     with **T's constraint as `sub` and B's as `super`**; T with no wildcard
     discharges the clause vacuously; T-with/B-without is charged, as is a
     failing subset. The old `GAP(xsd)` at the seam is **deleted**.
  2. **`xsd/attributewildcardfold.go`** (new) — `foldAttributeWildcards` (`:90`),
     the property-wise sibling of #401's `xsd/attributeusefold.go`, walking
     `s.types` base-before-derived with the same position index (named complex
     types only) and the same acyclicity licence. Clause **2.1** leaves a
     restriction's own wildcard alone — which is what stops the fold collapsing
     every type onto `·xs:anyType·`'s lax `##any`, the vacuity hazard this thread
     warned about; **2.2.2.1/2.2.2.2** hand an operand through unchanged;
     **2.2.2.3** builds a new `Wildcard` with the extension's OWN `{process
     contents}`/`{annotations}` over `UnionNamespaceConstraint` (`cos-aw-union`,
     exported by #230 at exactly the signature this thread pre-decided) of the
     two `{namespace constraint}`s. Absence is the `*Wildcard` nil the
     component's Optional slot already uses — no second "present" flag (D3).
  3. **`xsd/resolve.go:148-149`** — `s.foldAttributeWildcards()` wired in beside
     `s.foldAttributeUses()`, both before `checkComplexDerivations()`; Phase D's
     "one mutation" paragraph updated to two.
  4. **`xsd/complexextension.go`** — new unexported
     `checkExtensionAttributeWildcard` (`:225`), `cos-ct-extends` clause **1.3**,
     mirror direction (`sub: B`, `super: T`). The clause-**1.3 half only** of the
     `GAP(xsd)` block #264 left behind is deleted; the clause-**1.7** paragraph
     survives at `:111` and belongs to **#346**, not here.
  5. **`xsd/defaultbinding.go:73-83`** — the `GAP(xsd)` for this exact gap and
     the *"walking the chain for wildcards would be wrong"* disclosure paragraph
     are **both deleted**. The read site's code did not change: the chain is
     folded upstream now, so reading `c.AttributeWildcard()` alone is simply
     **correct by construction**, not a compromise.
  6. **`xsd/contentrestricts.go:359`** — `coveringWildcardUnion`'s
     sibling-keyword marker rewritten, **comment-only, no code change**, to name
     #265 and record the ruling below. It keeps its `GAP(` token and its
     fail-open direction, and cites #265 as the ruling's **provenance rather than
     as a retirement owner** — which is what P3 permits.
  7. **`conformance/schema.go`** — `complexTypeDecidable` still declines the two
     `<extension>` forms (the lane widening is #336's), but the *reason* narrows:
     clauses 1.2 and 1.3 now read FOLDED components, so only the §3.4.2.1
     `{assertions}` fold (**#346**) is still missing.
- **Grounding — four questions, and two of the four answers were rulings that
  removed code rather than requiring it.**
  (a) `derivation-ok-restriction` clause 3 (`c-ran`) cites cvc-complex-type
  clauses 2 and 3 **by number**, and only clause **2.2** (`c-avaw`) pulls in
  `{attribute wildcard}`. Clause 3 is stated EXTENSIONALLY over every element and
  attribute information item that could ever appear, and a full-text grep shows
  `derivation-ok-restriction` **never names `cos-ns-subset`** anywhere — the
  linkage is an **implementer's inference**, not a spec-stated cross-reference,
  but it is the only named relation available for deciding the question
  statically. (b) `cos-ns-subset`'s full four-case text plus its conjunctive
  `{disallowed names}` tail matches the landed `wildcardSubset` **exactly**, so
  nothing needed re-deriving. (c) Appendix G.1.5 item 1 confirms 1.1's rewrite of
  the restriction rules was a deliberate *"shorter and more correct"* fix, not a
  compatibility carve-out — there is **no spec-sanctioned fallback to 1.0
  semantics**, so the 27-case collision #262 hit is expected divergence from
  1.0-era test authorship under PRINCIPLES 25, and, decisively, **does not apply
  to this issue's new check at all**, which fires only when T declares its own
  `<anyAttribute>` and those 27 cases declare none. (d) `cos-aw-union` has **no
  sibling bullet by design**: §3.10.6.3 is titled *Attribute* Wildcard Union,
  cvc-wildcard clause 3 gates the sibling test on *"W is an element wildcard"*
  explicitly, and `##definedSibling` is not even in `<anyAttribute>`'s `notQName`
  grammar (`xmlschema11-1.md:2843` vs `<any>`'s at `:2835`). Grounding:
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/265#issuecomment-5159526103>.
- **The repair round: ONE finding, narrow and specific, and it was a real bug the
  suite has zero coverage of.** Round 1 rejected with an all-green gate and a
  measured (unbanked) +6, on `[STYLE P3]` at `xsd/complexderivation.go:406-415`.
  Mason had **honestly disclosed** the residual fail-closed over-approximation in
  `checkRestrictionAttributeWildcard` — `cos-ns-subset` decides over `{namespace
  constraint}`s alone and knows nothing of `{attribute uses}`, so a subset
  failure caused SOLELY by a QName in B's wildcard's `{disallowed names}` that B
  (hence T, by §3.4.2.4 clause 3.2) declares a use for is charged though clause 3
  does not require it — but disclosed it **in plain prose with no `GAP(` token**,
  which P3 says *"does not count as tracked debt no matter how honest the prose
  around it is"*. The 2026-08-02 `/backlog` GAP sweep on this very thread would
  have walked straight past it.
  **The arbiter did not stop at citing the STYLE rule.** It built the shape and
  reproduced the false reject from ordinary XSD 1.1 syntax — a base declaring
  `<xs:attribute name="foo"/>` alongside `<anyAttribute namespace="##any"
  notQName="foo"/>`, restricted by a type carrying a plain `<anyAttribute
  namespace="##any"/>` and inheriting the `foo` use — and confirmed `Finalize`
  rejects a schema that `c-ran` makes **valid**. Not hypothetical, not
  suite-only; the suite simply has no case with the shape, which is precisely why
  the marker had to exist.
  The prescribed fix was **comment-only** — *"the code needs no change; I am not
  asking for the precise fix"* — so **#430** was filed by the orchestrating
  session (a mechanical prerequisite: the marker cannot name an issue that does
  not exist) to own the actual narrowing, and `d2643e4` rewrote the prose **in
  place** into a proper `// GAP(xsd):` marker. Every added and removed line in
  that commit begins with `//`; `checkRestrictionAttributeWildcard`'s body is
  byte-identical to `f0e68c7`. The marker satisfies **P3a** by enumerating the
  consumer chain by actual identifier — `checkRestrictionAttributes` →
  `Schema.checkComplexTypeRestriction` → `Schema.checkComplexDerivations` →
  `Schema.resolve` → `SchemaBuilder.Finalize`/`FinalizeWith` — with the explicit
  statement that every link propagates a non-nil error as a rejection, says
  `FAIL-CLOSED` and `FALSE REJECT of a VALID schema` in the file's own capitals,
  and writes the arbiter's reproduction shape into the comment so it never has to
  be rediscovered. Round 2: **accept, no findings.**
- **Exported surface: ZERO delta over the whole branch**, verified by the arbiter
  independently in both rounds and two ways in round 2 — `go doc -all -short` on
  `./xsd`, `./conformance`, `./parser`, `./validate` and root, reduced to
  declaration lines and sorted, **identical** either side (434 declarations in
  `./xsd`); plus a scan of the branch diff for added exported declarations,
  which yields only unexported methods (`foldAttributeWildcards`,
  `foldTypeAttributeWildcard`, `clause2AttributeWildcard`,
  `storeFoldedAttributeWildcards`), the unexported `attributeWildcardFold` type,
  unexported helpers, and `Test*` functions. `UnionNamespaceConstraint` was
  already exported by #230; this branch only updates its doc to name the landed
  consumer.
- **The two clause-1.3 rejections are UNREACHABLE through `Finalize` today, kept
  anyway, and mutation-tested so the pairing is not decorative.** §3.4.2.5 clause
  2.2 makes T's wildcard the union of its own with B's, and a `cos-aw-union`
  result is a `cos-ns-subset` superset of both operands by construction — so
  neither rejection can fire on a faithfully mapped type. They are kept as the
  executable statement of the clause and as the guard on the fold's 2.2 arm,
  which is **exactly what `checkExtensionAttributeUses` already does** in the same
  file over #401's `{attribute uses}` fold, in the same words. The arbiter checked
  that precedent rather than taking it on report, and proved the pairing live by
  mutation: **disabling the fold makes clause 1.3 reject three valid schemas**,
  and disabling the fold AND removing the clause-1.3 call makes the acceptance
  test fail with *exactly* the false reject this issue names.
- Decisions:
  1. **A separate fold file, NOT a shared "fold engine" with
     `attributeusefold.go`.** Two different mapping rules with different case
     lists and different combination operators, sharing only the shape of the
     base-before-derived walk. Merging them would be an abstraction bought with a
     loop; the arbiter ruled the refusal correct under T4.
  2. **The sibling-keyword gap is PERMANENT and closes as a ruling, not as
     code.** The acceptance addendum from the 2026-07-31 GAP sweep offered two
     exits — *"either the fold stops dropping the sibling keyword, or the marker
     is rewritten to name this issue and record the ruling."* The oracle's answer
     (d) forces the second: there is nothing to fix, because `sibling` is an
     element-wildcard-only concept and `cos-aw-union` is correct to have no
     bullet for it. The loss is local to `coveringWildcardUnion`'s one call site,
     where the attribute-only algebra is applied to ELEMENT wildcards, and only
     when more than one base wildcard is live. Still fail-open.
  3. **`foldAttributeWildcards` returns `error` rather than swallowing or
     panicking**, even though the union is unreachable-failing for two validly
     constructed operands, so a future divergence fails closed.
  4. **The `GAP(xsd)` on `ComplexType.AttributeWildcard` (`xsd/complextype.go:507`)
     is BARE, no issue number — deliberately.** P3 requires an owning issue only
     where one owns the retirement, and there is direct precedent one screen up:
     `ComplexType.AttributeUses` (`:477`) carries an identical bare marker for the
     same `InlineTypeDefinition` seam, landed with #401 and accepted. The new
     marker binds itself to it explicitly (*"ONE unfolded shape with two sites,
     not two gaps"*). The arbiter ruled this **legitimate, not a finding**.
- Surprises:
  1. **A three-part issue that looked park-shaped fit one session, because two of
     its three parts were spec-reading rulings rather than code.** This thread
     carried eight bookkeeping/reconciliation comments over seven days and an
     explicit *"this is `ready`, but it is not one session"* warning. What
     actually shrank it was the grounding: the sibling-keyword bullet closed as a
     ruling (decision 2), and ruling out the 27-case regression closed as a
     ruling (grounding (c)). The remaining code surface was **one new finalize
     fold plus two small charge additions**. Worth remembering the next time a
     multi-section issue looks unsplittable: ask which sections are decidable
     from the spec alone before assuming they all need commits.
  2. **The one genuinely new bug in this landing was found by the ARBITER's own
     reproduction discipline** — not by mason (who reasoned the seam correctly
     but mis-classified how to disclose it), and not by the conformance suite
     (which has zero coverage of the shape and would never have caught it). The
     verdict did not stop at *"this paragraph lacks a `GAP(` token"*; it built the
     schema and confirmed `Finalize` false-rejects it. That is the "don't just
     check for the token, try to break it" discipline earning its cost in a
     measurable way, and it is the argument for keeping it when it feels
     expensive.
- Friction:
  1. **`golangci-lint` is STILL not on `PATH`, and the arbiter paid the tax
     twice more this session** (once per round; it lives at
     `$(go env GOPATH)/bin/golangci-lint` and runs `0 issues` from there). This
     is the same tax recorded in the #417 and #253 entries above. It is **#426**,
     filed and **still unowned** — three consecutive sessions now, which is a
     pattern rather than a nuisance and should be visible to the next retro.
  2. **The repair round cost nothing on behaviour and everything on record
     hygiene** — one prose paragraph turned into one marker, plus one issue
     filed. Same shape as #417's and #253's rounds: a diff whose *code* was right
     on the first pass, rejected on what it said about itself. Three in a row is
     worth the retro's attention: the recurring defect is not correctness, it is
     **disclosure mechanism**, and it keeps being caught at the last gate rather
     than by mason.
- Next:
  1. **#430 — filed, `ready`, `kind/gap`/`area/xsd`, open.** Owns the actual
     narrowing of `checkRestrictionAttributeWildcard`'s fail-closed
     over-approximation. The arbiter read its body and confirmed it is not a
     placeholder: it carries the reproduction as an acceptance test and offers
     the same two exits the marker names — a name-set-parameterised subset
     relation, or a pre-filter of B's `{disallowed names}` against T's
     `{attribute uses}` at the call site. Whichever it takes, it must not
     re-derive `cos-ns-subset` in a second encoding (T4, the hazard #262
     declined).
  2. **[arbiter non-blocking, UNFILED — for the post-land pass or the next
     `/backlog`]** The bare `GAP(xsd)` markers on `ComplexType.AttributeWildcard`
     (`xsd/complextype.go:507`, new here) and `ComplexType.AttributeUses`
     (`:477`, pre-existing from #401) are **one shape with two sites and no
     owner**: neither fold reaches an anonymous complex type nested as an
     `InlineTypeDefinition`. Both verdicts said the same thing — file **one**
     issue for the `InlineTypeDefinition` fold seam and repoint **both** markers
     at it, and do not let it hold up this landing. Not filed by this session;
     recorded here so it does not evaporate.
  3. **[carried, unchanged]** #346 (§3.4.2.1 `{assertions}` fold) is now the
     **sole** remaining reason `complexTypeDecidable` declines the two
     `<extension>` forms, and #336 owns the lane widening once it lands — that

## 2026-08-02 — #301 — xsd: `ComplexType.{context}` (§3.4.1 `ctd-context`) lands as a `ComponentID` component-handle — an opaque pointer identity minted by the producer BEFORE either endpoint exists — with the `{name}`/`{context}` XOR enforced by a constructor PARTITION rather than a runtime check; the oracle's decisive finding was that the tableau's Complex-Type-Definition arm is declared but reachable by NO mapping rule in the spec, and the arbiter accepted on round 1 with the ratchet unchanged exactly as predicted

- Shipped on `wip/issue-301` as ONE commit on top of `54fd100` (#265) —
  `7c73577` (`xsd: wire ComplexType.{context} (§3.4.1 ctd-context) as a
  component-handle identity (#301)`) — **10 files, +634/−29**:
  `xsd/componentid.go` (new, 82 lines), `xsd/componentid_test.go` (new, 93),
  `xsd/complextype.go` (the bulk, +274/−…), `xsd/complextype_test.go` (+145),
  `xsd/typedefinition.go`, `xsd/elementdeclaration.go` and `xsd/doc.go` (all
  doc-only), plus three test files whose helpers had to migrate
  (`xsd/schema_test.go`, `xsd/complexderivation_test.go`,
  `xsd/substitutiongroup_test.go`). **Warden pre-flight BEFORE any code**
  (required by the issue's Surface section) returned `revise` with eight
  findings, **all of them local amendments** — the architecture was approved as
  designed and no second pre-flight was taken. **Zero arbiter repair rounds,
  ACCEPT on round 1.** No `parser/` change; all four non-goals held.
- **Ratchet: unchanged in ALL lanes — `schema`, `datatypes`, `instance` and the
  four empty lanes, 0 regressed — RUN by the arbiter, not asserted.** The
  arbiter's figures, copied verbatim from the verdict and not recomputed: all
  seven files under `conformance/testdata/expectations/` are **byte-identical**
  before and after the `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` pass (`shasum` compared: `schema.txt`
  `2d97d85a…`, `datatypes.txt` `9f0d34df…`, `instance.txt` `0615ef31…`, the
  four empty lanes `da39a3ee…`), and the working tree was still clean after the
  ratchet's write pass. This is exactly what the issue predicted and for the
  stated reason — **a leaf shape with no producer**: nothing in `parser/`
  constructs an anonymous `ComplexType` yet, so no suite case can reach the new
  slot. The issue body had flagged that #206 made the same prediction about a
  structural back-pointer and was wrong; here it held, measured.
- **The change, exactly.**
  1. **`xsd/componentid.go` (new)** — `ComponentID struct{ cell *identityCell }`,
     `identityCell struct{ _ byte }`, `NewComponentID()`. A struct wrapping a
     pointer, so it is `==`-comparable, its zero value is the meaningful ABSENT
     identity, and it survives by-value copies: copying a `ComplexType` copies
     the interface value, which copies the struct, which copies the POINTER.
     `cell` is unexported, so minting is the sole source of a present identity
     (T1). `IsPresent` was **dropped** entirely per the pre-flight's [T5] —
     `id == ComponentID{}` IS the contract, and a second spelling of it would be
     a second story.
  2. **`xsd/complextype.go`** — `ComplexTypeContext`, the two-arm sealed sum
     §3.4.1's tableau declares (`ElementDeclarationContext` /
     `ComplexTypeDefinitionContext`), with `ID() ComponentID` **promoted into
     the interface** per the pre-flight's [T2/T3] so a consumer reads the
     identity without a type switch; `checkComplexTypeContext` (exhaustive
     switch, `default: panic`, charged to `xsderr.RuleComponentInvariant`); the
     `context ComplexTypeContext` field; the constructor partition; and
     `Context() (ComplexTypeContext, bool)`, the two-result shape matching
     `Scope.Parent` / `AttributeWildcard`.
  3. **The constructor partition** — `NewComplexType` (signature
     **byte-identical**, confirmed by the exported-surface diff) builds the named
     variety and takes no context; the new `NewAnonymousComplexType` takes a
     context and no name; both run one unexported `newComplexType` core. The
     named-plus-context state and the neither state simply **do not type-check**
     at the entry points, so §3.4.1's Required-iff XOR needs no runtime check —
     the `NewGlobalScope`/`NewLocalScope` payoff, reused. Per the pre-flight's
     [T1] on the core, `newComplexType`'s doc states that the XOR is a
     precondition enforced by its two callers and not by itself, and binds any
     third caller.
  4. **Doc reconciliation in the SAME commit (T6)** — `ComplexType`'s stale
     *"{context} … is entirely UNMODELED, tracked as #301"* paragraph is gone;
     `typedefinition.go`'s `InlineTypeDefinition` paragraph, which was both
     **stale** (#206 landed) and **mis-cited** (§3.16.1 is the *simple* type's
     `std-context`), is rewritten; `elementdeclaration.go`'s `NewLocalScope`
     *"a component handle, not a name"* note finally names its referent; and
     `xsd/doc.go` gained a component-identity paragraph beside "Names are
     expanded QNames", which was only half the package's identity story as of
     this commit.
- **Grounding — the decisive finding was a NEGATIVE one, and it removed code
  rather than requiring it.** §3.4.1's tableau declares `{context}` as "Either
  an Element Declaration or a Complex Type Definition", but §3.4.2.1
  `dcl.ctd.common` has **exactly one case**, producing only the Element
  Declaration arm ("the nearest `<element>` information item among the ancestor
  element information items"). The `<complexType>` content model has no slot for
  a nested unnamed `<complexType>`; anonymous complex types appear only under
  `<element>` or `<alternative>` (§3.12.3 `src-ta`), both of which always have
  an `<element>` ancestor. So **the Complex-Type-Definition arm is textually
  declared and unreachable by any mapping rule in this spec** — a genuine
  spec-text asymmetry, not an implementation gap to be filled with invented
  logic. The contrast case is `std-context` (§3.16.1), whose 4-member sum IS
  fully realized, by §3.16.2.1's 2.1–2.5.2 case split. The oracle also ruled out
  the obvious refactor: §J Summary of Changes makes `{context}` (types) and
  `{scope}` (declarations) deliberately separate, differently-shaped properties
  — `sc_e` is CTD|MGD, `ctd-context` is ED|CTD — so **their Go representations
  must not be unified**. Grounding:
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/301#issuecomment-5159761490>.
- Decisions:
  1. **Settle the ARITY of a sealed sum at birth; add a stored FIELD only when
     something reads it.** One rule, stated up front in the design, resolving
     both of its "model it now or later?" questions consistently rather than ad
     hoc. Adding a variant to a sealed sum later is a **runtime** break — every
     exhaustive consumer switch in `xsd/` ends in `default: panic("non-exhaustive
     … switch")`, so a new arm turns correct code into a panic the compiler
     cannot see. Adding a **parameter to a constructor** later is a
     **compile-time** break: loud, mechanical, caught by the gate on the first
     `go build`. So both arms ship now (including the unreachable one) and **no
     own-identity field lands on `ElementDeclaration`/`ComplexType`** — #340 adds
     field, producer and resolver in one landing. The consequence is a
     deliberately small #301 with **zero churn on the 61 `NewComplexType` and 49
     `NewElementDeclaration` non-test call sites**.
  2. **`identityCell struct{ _ byte }` — the blank byte is LOAD-BEARING, and the
     trap is worse than the design first stated.** Go does not guarantee
     distinct addresses for distinct zero-size allocations, and an **escaping**
     `new(struct{})` comes back from `runtime.zerobase`, so every zero-size cell
     would share one address and two independently minted identities would
     compare **equal**. The warden measured it on go1.26.4 (escaping zero-size:
     `equal=true`; escaping 1-byte: `equal=false`) and added the fact that
     decides the test design: the failure is **escape-dependent**, so a
     `struct{}` cell passes a stack-allocated unit test and then collides the
     moment the IDs escape into components — which is exactly what this package
     does with them. The doc says so imperatively, so a future "unused field"
     cleanup cannot silently delete component identity.
  3. **`ComponentID` is never rendered and never ordered** — its underlying
     value is an ADDRESS, so any textual form (and any address ordering) is
     run-to-run nondeterministic, which D1 forbids outright. No `String()`, no
     `fmt.Stringer`; rejections describe the offending arm with `%T`. The same
     fact is booked FORWARD in `ComponentID`'s doc against #340's resolver: it
     must never range a `map[ComponentID]…` to produce output or ordering (D2).
     The one `ComponentID`-keyed map in this commit is a collision index inside a
     test, never iterated into output — D2's sanctioned internal-lookup use, and
     the arbiter checked that rather than assuming it.
  4. **The unminted-ID rejection is charged to `RuleComponentInvariant`, NOT to
     `ct-props-correct`** — deliberately unlike `NewLocalScope`, which charges
     its empty-name guard to `e-props-correct` clause 1. The split is principled
     and the arbiter called it the subtlest call in the diff: the tableau's own
     Required-ness is a **spec** fact a schema author can be told about (nil
     context → `ct-props-correct` cl. 1), whereas an unminted `ComponentID` is a
     representation **this package invented** → the footing
     `checkTypeDefinitionOrRef` already stands on.
  5. **The unreachable `ComplexTypeDefinitionContext` arm carries PROSE, not a
     `GAP(` marker.** Its unreachability is permanent spec fidelity, not
     incompleteness, so marking it GAP would require an owning issue under P3
     that can never close — manufacturing exactly the dead end P3 forbids. The
     arbiter ruled this correct and put it on the record. The *other* fact — that
     the ED arm has no producer until **#340** — IS marked `GAP(xsd)`, in two
     places, naming an issue verified OPEN.
  6. **The named-path guard tests `name.Local == ""`, not `name == QName{}`**
     (pre-flight [T1]), so `QName{Space:"urn:x", Local:""}` cannot slip through
     as a NAMED type and §3.4.1's "an NCName, or absent" has no third state.
     This is one piece of the folded-in `xsd.QName` question moved INTO this
     commit rather than adding a fresh `== QName{}` site for a successor issue to
     clean up later.
- Surprises:
  1. **`reflect.DeepEqual` is IDENTITY-BLIND for this scheme, and it is pinned by
     a test that asserts the wrong-looking thing on purpose.** Measured by the
     warden: DeepEqual follows pointers and compares pointees, every
     `identityCell` is the same zero byte, so two **distinct** minted identities
     compare `DeepEqual == true`. A component-level DeepEqual assertion will
     therefore silently accept a wrong `{context}`.
     `TestComponentIDDeepEqualIsIdentityBlind` asserts `a != b` **and**
     `reflect.DeepEqual(a, b) == true`, with a comment saying a failure means the
     doc went stale, not that a bug appeared. The same measurement **corrected
     the design proposal in the other direction**: the proposal had rejected an
     alternative on the ground that DeepEqual would infinitely recurse over a
     cyclic component graph — measurably false (it carries a visited set), as was
     the companion claim that `%v` follows a nested pointer. Both false grounds
     were kept OUT of the tree: mason shipped no rejected-alternatives comment at
     all rather than restate a refuted argument.
  2. **The pre-flight's "of the 61 `NewComplexType` call sites, none passes an
     absent name" held for non-test code only.** Five sites across three `xsd/`
     test helpers (`complexTypeNamed`, `dType`, `sgType`) DID build anonymous
     complex types through `NewComplexType(…, QName{}, …)` and had to migrate.
     Each now dispatches on `name.Local == ""` to `NewAnonymousComplexType` with
     a freshly minted `ElementDeclarationContext` — the honest arm in all three,
     since they either wrap the result in an `InlineTypeDefinition` on an element
     declaration or only care that `{name}` is absent. Test intent is unchanged,
     and the side benefit is real: it turns `NewAnonymousComplexType`'s T5
     justification into a **caller** argument rather than only a contract one.
  3. **A field/method name collision forced a rename the design had not
     anticipated.** Go forbids a type having both a field `ID` and a method
     `ID()`, and the pre-flight's [T2/T3] requires `ID()` in the interface — so
     the arms' field is `Component`, not `ID`, following the in-package precedent
     where an exported field and a promoted method differ in name
     (`ElementContent.Mixed` + `Variety()`). The arms stay literal-constructible
     from outside `xsd` (`xsd.ElementDeclarationContext{Component: id}`), so no
     extra constructors had to be exported. Small, but it is the kind of thing a
     design review cannot catch on paper and the compiler catches in one second.
  4. **Both key tests were mutation-checked by mason AND independently re-run by
     the arbiter, which added a third of its own.** `identityCell struct{ _ byte
     }` → `struct{}` fails `TestNewComponentIDDistinctWhenEscaping` and the
     DeepEqual pin while the stack-local pair still PASSES (the escape-dependence
     made visible, and the reason the escaping test exists); `name.Local == ""` →
     `name == (QName{})` fails the absent-name rejection; and the arbiter's own —
     `Context()`'s presence flag `c.context != nil` → `true` — fails
     `TestNewComplexTypeNamedHasAbsentContext`. All three reproduced exactly.
- Friction:
  1. **`golangci-lint` is STILL not on `PATH`**, and the arbiter paid the tax
     again (neither `$PATH` nor `go tool` resolves it; it lives at
     `~/go/bin/golangci-lint` and runs `0 issues` from there). This is the same
     tax recorded in the #417, #253 and #265 entries above. It is **#426**, filed
     and **still unowned** — **four consecutive sessions** now. The last entry
     already called it a pattern rather than a nuisance and asked for the next
     retro's attention; it has not moved since.
  2. **Nothing else.** No repair round, no re-grounding, no scope surprise —
     the pre-flight-before-code ordering absorbed what three of the last five
     sessions paid for at the arbiter instead. Worth recording as the
     counter-example to the "the recurring defect is disclosure mechanism, and it
     keeps being caught at the last gate" pattern the #265 entry flagged: here
     the design review happened before any code existed, so eight findings cost
     zero commits.
- Next:
  1. **#340 is the next consumer of this design and is UNBLOCKED by this
     landing** (`parser: produce inline anonymous <complexType> on local
     elements`, still labelled `blocked`, `kind/feature`/`area/parser`). It
     inherits three concrete obligations recorded on this thread rather than left
     to be rediscovered: (a) [pre-flight T1] `NewElementDeclaration` must reject
     an inline anonymous `ComplexType` whose ED-arm `{context}` ID differs from
     the declaration's own minted ID — that check is what finally makes
     mis-pointing unrepresentable, and it is unbuildable until an own-identity
     field exists; (b) [pre-flight D2] the ID→component resolver must never range
     a `map[ComponentID]…` to produce output or ordering; (c) it must reuse THIS
     scheme for the `ComplexTypeScopeParent` side rather than invent a second,
     which is the "no second scheme" bullet this issue was chartered on. Its
     title still reads "blocked on #301's scope-parent handle" and its label is
     still `blocked` — **relabel and retitle it on the post-land pass**.
  2. **[arbiter non-blocking #1 — for the post-land pass, owned by #340]**
     `parser/produce_complex.go:1124`'s error string reads *"… not yet produced
     (#340, blocked on #301)"* and goes **stale the moment this merges**. Checked
     by the arbiter: it is plain prose inside an `fmt.Errorf`, **not** a `GAP(`
     marker (`grep 'GAP(' parser/ | grep -E '301|340'` returns nothing), so P3's
     "repoint it in the landing that closes the owner" does not formally bite,
     and this issue's non-goals exclude `parser/`. #340 rewrites the line when it
     builds the producer. `produce.go:827` and `produce_complex.go:1104`, which
     cite #301 as the reason `ComplexTypeScopeParent` cannot express an anonymous
     container, remain **TRUE** — that rework is #340's; do not touch them.
     Separately, `parser/produce_complex.go:1162` still cites #206 for a wrapped
     SIMPLE type's `{context}` (§3.16.1) — a different property, out of scope
     here; only the `xsd/typedefinition.go` half of that staleness is fixed.
  3. **[arbiter non-blocking #2 — DISCHARGED by this entry]** The verdict flagged
     that `docs/LOG/2026-08.md` carried no entry for this branch while every
     recent landed commit does. That is this entry, written on the WIP branch
     before the PR opens.
  4. **[UNFILED follow-up, carried from #206 — do not let it evaporate]** The
     folded-in `xsd.QName` empty-NCName question is **NOT subsumed** by
     `ComponentID` — the pre-flight said so explicitly and the arbiter carried it
     forward, which is precisely the answer this issue's acceptance bullet
     demanded ("the design must state whether it subsumes the empty-NCName
     problem or leaves it standing; if independent, split it out **then**, with
     that conclusion recorded"). `ComponentID` answers "which component", never
     "is this name well-formed". The successor issue — a validated constructor,
     or a distinct `NCName` type, making an empty local part unconstructible —
     must be **sequenced after #340**, because the surviving-guard set is only
     knowable once `ComplexTypeScopeParent` has or has not grown an identity arm.
     **It does not exist yet**: the nearest open issues are #343, #305, #295 and
     #296, and none of them is it. File it at the post-land pass or the next
     `/backlog`.
  5. **[carried, unchanged]** #426 (the lint `PATH` seam) is still filed and
     unowned after four sessions; #430, #346, #336, #400, #347, #420 and #270 are
     all still undischarged from earlier sessions.
- Thread record (do not duplicate) —
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/301>: the GROUNDING at
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/301#issuecomment-5159761490> (the
  §3.4.1/§3.4.2.1 citations, the unreachable-CTD-arm finding, the `std-context`
  contrast, and the §J ruling against unifying `{context}` with `{scope}`),
  mason's DESIGN PROPOSAL at
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/301#issuecomment-5159792703> (the
  arity-vs-field rule, the pointer-in-a-value-struct mechanism, the constructor
  partition, and four rejected alternatives), the WARDEN PRE-FLIGHT VERDICT at
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/301#issuecomment-5159818481>
  (`revise`, eight findings, every one a local amendment; the measured
  `runtime.zerobase` aliasing and the measured DeepEqual blindness; the five
  answered design questions; and the two debts explicitly booked onto #340),
  mason's implementation summary at
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/301#issuecomment-5159878309> (the
  file-by-file account, the four disclosed deviations, and its own mutation
  checks), and the round-1 ACCEPT at
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/301#issuecomment-5159905169> (the

  taken in a throwaway worktree against `origin/main`, the three reproduced
  mutations, the rule-by-rule STYLE review, and the two non-blocking post-land
  notes). Prior art consumed and not re-derived: **#229**
  (`TypeDefinitionOrRef` / `InlineTypeDefinition`, the FORWARD edge this
  property is the back-pointer of), **#206** (which split this issue out and
  rejected the QName representation for it), **#210** (the precedent for landing
  a latent slot that an anonymous producer exercises later), and
  `NewGlobalScope`/`NewLocalScope` (the constructor-partition pattern). Exported
  surface: **additive only** — seven declarations added, zero removed, zero
  signatures changed. This entry is WORKFLOW step 6, written on the WIP branch
  after the FINAL verdict and before the PR is opened so it rides in the squash;
  the squash message carries `Spec: ct-props-correct cl. 1 (§3.4.6.1),

## 2026-08-02 — #281 — parser: `substitutionGroup=` stops being an attribute this producer never reads and becomes real `{substitution group affiliations}` data, retiring `contentrestricts.go`'s global/global fail-open outright; two deviations were discovered MID-implementation and both were upheld by the arbiter — `block=` had to be mapped in the SAME slice or affiliations alone would false-reject `elemZ028a`, and a never-exercised eager `src-resolve` loop had to be DELETED because populating the slot would have turned it on and lost `missing002`, a schema the W3C suite says is valid

- Shipped on `wip/issue-281` as THREE commits on top of `034ef49` (#301) —
  `5af4cae` (`parser: map substitutionGroup= into {substitution group
  affiliations}, retiring contentrestricts' global/global fail-open (#281)`,
  8 files, +451/−54), the repair round `15bb0e8` (`parser/xsd: repair round for
  #281 — GAP marker ownership + comment accuracy`, 4 files, +40/−19, **every
  changed line a `//` line**), and the arbiter's ratchet write `a7d3050`
  (`conformance: ratchet schema lane 4263 -> 4283 for #281`). Branch total
  **9 files, +492/−74**: `parser/produce.go` (+127, the two new mappers),
  `parser/produce_test.go` (+193, seven new tests), `parser/produce_complex.go`
  (+67), `xsd/resolve.go` (+45), `xsd/contentrestricts.go` and its test
  (the fail-open's removal), `xsd/substitutiongroup.go`, `parser/doc.go`, and
  the expectations file. **One arbiter repair round; ACCEPT on round 2.**
- **Ratchet: schema lane `4263 → 4283 pass / 15432` (+20), ZERO downward flips
  on any lane, all six lanes audited — RUN and WRITTEN by the arbiter in
  `a7d3050`, not asserted.** The arbiter's figures, copied verbatim from the
  round-2 verdict and not recomputed: 20 upward flips, 0 downward, 0 added,
  0 removed lines; `instance.txt`, `datatypes.txt`, `xpath.txt`, `json.txt`,
  `ber.txt` byte-identical. The 20 are `All/all241`, `All/all242`,
  `MS-Additional/addB180`, `MS-Element/elemZ028b|c|d|f1|f2|f3`,
  `MS-Particles/particlesIg006|007|008|009|010|013|016`,
  `substitution-groups/sg-abstract-edc|upa|upa2`,
  `substitutionGroup/s2_2_2si01` — every one explainable by the diff
  (affiliation edges for `cos-nonambig` and `e-props-correct` clause 5 to walk,
  `block=` mapping, §5.3 alignment), with `particlesIg006.xsd` and
  `all241.n.xsd` spot-checked as actually carrying the attributes. **The
  issue's named regression guards all still pass** — `elemZ027_a/_b/_e/_f`,
  `elemZ028a`, `particlesZ008`, `particlesZ028` — and they passed at baseline
  too, so the zero-downward count covers them. The ratchet was run TWICE (round
  1 verified-but-not-written, round 2 re-run fresh) and reproduced exactly.
- **The change, exactly.**
  1. **`parser/produce.go` — `substitutionGroupAffiliations`.** Reads
     `substitutionGroup` off a top-level `<element>`, splits on
     `strings.Fields`, and resolves each item through the existing
     `p.resolveQName` in-scope-bindings resolver (STYLE T4 — no second
     resolver, and §F.1 task (b)'s chameleon coercion comes along for free).
     Returns a slice in document order, never a set, so nothing is ever ranged
     out of a map (D2). It replaces the literal `nil` `produceElement` had been
     passing to `NewElementDeclaration` since the producer spine landed.
  2. **`parser/produce_complex.go` — `e-props-correct` clause 3 is charged.**
     `produceLocalElement` now rejects a local `<element>` carrying the
     attribute at all, FIRST, before the `src-element` test, on the attribute's
     presence rather than on the built component — because reaching
     `xsd.NewElementDeclaration`'s identical clause-3 check would require
     building the very state the clause forbids. The meta-schema declares
     `use="prohibited"` on `xs:localElement` (§3.3.2) and this producer runs no
     meta-schema validation pass, so nothing else would have seen it.
  3. **`xsd/contentrestricts.go` — the fail-open is GONE, not narrowed.**
     `elementParticleAdmits` was two arms; it is now one line calling
     `s.inSubstitutionGroupOf` directly. The `GAP(xsd)` block above it is
     replaced by prose that opens *"There is NO approximation left here"* and
     records what the arm was for, so the history is not lost with the code.
     `TestContentRestrictsGlobalSubstitutionGap` — which **pinned** the
     fail-open — is renamed `TestContentRestrictsGlobalSubstitution` and now
     asserts the opposite: a global pairing with no affiliation chain is
     correctly REJECTED.
  4. **Deviation 1 — `{disallowed substitutions}` had to be mapped in the same
     slice.** `disallowedSubstitutions` maps `block=` / the ancestor
     `<schema>`'s `blockDefault=` through the ·effective block value· (three
     cases: empty → empty set, `#all` → all three keywords, else the keywords
     the list names, **non-keyword items ignored** per the mapping table's own
     Note). Shared by the global and local paths (T4). Returned in the spec's
     canonical `extension, restriction, substitution` order rather than lexical
     order, because the property is a set and `block="restriction extension"`
     must not build a different component from `block="extension restriction"`
     (D2).
  5. **Deviation 2 — an eager `src-resolve` loop was DELETED from
     `xsd/resolve.go`.** `resolveElementDecl` had a loop over
     `SubstitutionGroupAffiliationNames()` charging `src-resolve` on any
     dangling head. It had **never fired**, because every producer passed
     `nil`. Turning the slot on would have fired it for the first time.
  6. **Markers and tests.** Seven new tests (`…SubstitutionGroupHeads`,
     `…SingleHead`, `…UnknownHeadAccepted`, `…BadPrefixRejected`,
     `…CircularRejected`, `TestProduceElementBlockMapped` (9 subtests),
     `TestProduceElementBlockSubstitutionNarrowsGroup`,
     `TestProduceLocalElementSubstitutionGroupRejected`). No exported-surface
     movement at all — this filled an existing `NewElementDeclaration`
     parameter, exactly as the issue's Surface section predicted; the arbiter's
     `go doc -all -short` diff found the only delta to be prose.
- **Grounding — the decisive facts were grammatical, and they came before any
  code.** The meta-schema splits `<element>` into `xs:topLevelElement` and
  `xs:localElement`, and the local restriction carries
  `<xs:attribute name="substitutionGroup" use="prohibited"/>`
  (xmlschema11-1.md:5124) while the top-level one does not — so the attribute
  is legal on the global form ONLY, and clause 3 is the component-level
  backstop a processor that runs no meta-schema pass needs. The second finding
  is the one that shaped the whole landing: `{substitution group affiliations}`
  resolves **every** item in the list, while `{type definition}` case 3 reads
  the **first** item alone (both from the §3.3.2.1 table at
  xmlschema11-1.md:1144). *Do not conflate* — the two mappings read the same
  attribute two different ways, which is why #342 stays a separate issue rather
  than falling out of this one for free. Grounding:
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/281#issuecomment-5159958263>.
- Decisions:
  1. **Ship half the pair and you ship the WRONG change.** Affiliations without
     `block=` publishes an OVER-BROAD ·substitution group·: every declared head
     admits every member, and `cos-equiv-derived-ok-rec` clause 2.1 — the
     clause that decides whether a head admits substitution at all — has no
     `{disallowed substitutions}` to consult. The arbiter did not take this on
     faith: it reverted `disallowedSubstitutions` to `nil` while leaving
     affiliations mapped and **reproduced the claimed false-reject**
     (`TestProduceElementBlockSubstitutionNarrowsGroup`, a `cos-nonambig`
     ·compete· diagnostic). W3C `MS-Element/elemZ028a` is exactly that shape —
     an affiliation chain a→b→c→d where c and d each carry
     `block="substitution"`, with b, c and d then named side by side in one
     `<xs:all>`. So the scope grew by one property, and growing it was the
     smaller change, not the larger one.
  2. **§5.3 says an unresolvable reference is an ·absent· VALUE, not a schema
     error — so the correct move was to remove a check, not add one.**
     xmlschema11-1.md:4315: *"the ·resolution· of such QNames can fail,
     resulting in one or more values of or containing ·absent· where a
     component is mandated"*, and the consequence is stated **entirely in terms
     of ·assessment·** — the effect is as if clause 1 of Element Locally Valid
     had failed, and *"processors must fall back to ·lax assessment·"*. §4.2.3
     is more explicit still and cuts directly against the deleted loop:
     *"**During schema construction, implementations must retain ·QName· values
     for such references**, in case an appropriately-named component becomes
     available"* (:4065). `e-props-correct` clause 1 itself reads *"modulo the
     impact of Missing Sub-components (§5.3)"*. The suite agrees in its own
     metadata — `saxonMeta/Missing.testSet:47`, `missing002`:
     *"Element declaration with missing substitution group head / Error only if
     the element declaration is needed for validation"*, `expected
     validity="valid"`, over a schema that is literally
     `<xs:element name="bad" substitutionGroup="rotten"/>`. **The arbiter
     verified this against the spec text independently rather than accepting
     mason's summary**, and put on the record that this was *not* a check
     lowered to make the branch green: the loop was a §5.3 deviation that had
     never executed, and lighting it up would have LOST a case the suite
     requires to pass.
  3. **Align the ONE slot the landing touches; book the rest, do not
     opportunistically fix them.** The other by-name slots (`{type
     definition}`, `<element ref>`, `<attribute ref>`, `<group ref>`, keyref)
     still hard-fail `src-resolve`, which is why `Missing/missing001|003|006`
     sit at `fail`. That residue became **#434** rather than scope creep,
     because aligning it needs ·absent· representable in every slot **plus** a
     lax-assessment fallback at validation time, neither of which exists.
  4. **The two readers of the property were checked to already behave as §5.3
     requires** rather than being changed to: `affiliationChainReaches` skips a
     member it cannot look up (its `continue` comment was rewritten from *"was
     already charged src-resolve by Phase A"* to *"an ·absent· member (§5.3):
     there is no component to walk through"* — the comment was a load-bearing
     lie the moment the loop went), and `checkSubstitutionGroupsAcyclic`
     contributes no edge for one.
  5. **`{substitution group exclusions}` (`final=`/`finalDefault`) was
     deliberately NOT mapped**, even though it is the obvious twin of the
     property that had to be mapped. It is read by exactly one rule —
     `e-props-correct` clause 4 (`c-vs-sg`) — which this package does not
     implement and which `xsd/substitutiongroup.go` records as unimplemented.
     Mapping it would add a fact with no reader (STYLE D4). The asymmetry with
     decision 1 is the whole point: `block=` was mapped because a rule
     consults it TODAY and gets a wrong answer without it.
- Surprises:
  1. **Both mid-flight deviations were upheld, and the one that looked like a
     loosening was the more clearly correct of the two.** Deleting a
     `src-resolve` check inside `xsd/` is the shape that normally reads as
     lowering a bar to make a branch green, and the arbiter treated it that way
     — it re-derived §5.3 and §4.2.3 from the spec text and pulled the suite
     metadata itself. The verdict states flatly: *"This deviation is not the
     reason for the reject. It is correct."*
  2. **The reject was three findings and NONE of them touched behavior.** All
     three were comment/tracking defects: a `GAP(xsd)` marker naming **#281**
     (the issue this landing CLOSES — a marker pointing at a closed issue the
     instant it merges, exactly the dead end STYLE P3 forbids) instead of the
     still-open sibling **#342**; a newly-widened §5.3 deviation documented
     with a promise of an issue (*"Aligning the rest is a §5.3 issue of its
     own"*) but no issue filed or cited; and two doc comments asserting *"the
     local path rejects the attribute outright"* when the `ref=` form does not.
     The repair commit is 40 `//` lines and nothing else.
  3. **`<xs:element ref="a" substitutionGroup="b"/>` is silently accepted, and
     the hole is real but invisible.** `produceElementParticle`'s `ref=` branch
     returns BEFORE `produceLocalElement` — the one place clause 3 is charged —
     ever runs. It is still an `xs:localElement`, still `use="prohibited"`.
     Direction-safe (under-reject; nothing reads the attribute on that path,
     `ElementDeclarationRef` has no affiliations slot at all) and **zero W3C
     suite cases have the shape** — the arbiter grepped `testdata/xsdtests` and
     got 0. So it landed as a **bare, intentionally unowned `GAP(` marker** at
     `parser/produce_complex.go:1070-1082`, which the arbiter ruled legal under
     P3 as written (*"A gap whose retirement is owned by an issue names that
     issue"* — conditional, not universal) and well-formed under **P3a**: it
     names its consumer set by identifier and states the direction as an
     under-reject explicitly.
  4. **The blocked sibling did NOT fall out for free, and that was the right
     answer.** #281's own thread had instructed in advance: absorb #342 if
     clause 3 comes along, otherwise repoint the marker at it rather than
     deleting or rewording it. Clause 3 needs the head's own **declared** type,
     which is recursive (the head may itself be a clause-3 case) and lives
     behind a name this single-document producer may not have mapped yet — a
     resolved-component-phase problem. So #342 keeps its scope and finally has
     its marker back.
  5. **Two non-blocking observations were recorded and deliberately not
     charged**, both consistent with existing repo conventions rather than new
     debt: `ebv == "#all"` is a raw compare with no whitespace collapse (so
     `block=" #all "` falls to case 3 and yields the empty set — identical to
     the existing `fd == "qualified"` convention at `produce.go:1730`), and
     ignoring non-keyword items in `block=` (as opposed to `blockDefault=`,
     which the Note exempts) is strictly a meta-schema datatype violation this
     producer under-rejects — consistent with it running no meta-schema pass
     anywhere.
- Friction:
  1. **The disclosure mechanism was again what the last gate caught — third
     time in the recent run.** The #265 entry flagged *"the recurring defect is
     disclosure mechanism, and it keeps being caught at the last gate"*, #253
     was rejected over `GAP(` markers missing from the canonical site, #249's
     repair turned deleted markers into an honest one. Here the code and both
     spec deviations were right on round 1 and **all three findings were marker
     ownership and comment accuracy**. The pattern is now specific enough to
     name: a landing that RETIRES a gap has to re-point every marker that
     mentions it, and a landing that WIDENS a deviation has to file the issue
     before the arbiter asks. Worth the next retro's attention as a possible
     mason-prompt or STYLE P3 checklist item, since the cost is a full
     reject/repair/re-verify cycle for a comment-only diff every time.
  2. **The `GAP(` marker at `produce_complex.go:1200` had to be repaired by
     REPOINTING at an issue named in this issue's own thread three weeks ago.**
     The instruction was written down, on this thread, in advance, by #229's
     post-land pass — and was still missed. Written-in-advance thread
     instructions are only as good as the step that re-reads them; the mason
     read the grounding comment and not the older sibling-boundary comment.
  3. **[carried, unchanged]** #426 (the `golangci-lint` `PATH` seam) is still
     filed and unowned after five sessions; #430, #346, #336, #400, #347, #420
     and #270 remain undischarged from earlier sessions. #270 item 2 is
     directly relevant now: it names `@substitutionGroup` as one of the four
     §F.1 constructs the chameleon rewriter does not handle, and this landing
     is what makes that construct load-bearing.
- Next:
  1. **#434 is filed and `ready`** (`ready`, `kind/gap`, `area/xsd`) — *"the
     remaining by-name reference slots hard-fail at `src-resolve` instead of
     retaining an ·absent· value per §5.3"* — naming `resolveTypeName`,
     `resolveAttributeName`, `resolveModelGroupName` and `resolveKeyref`'s
     existence half. The arbiter verified it does NOT mis-list the already-fixed
     slot: `resolveElementName` appears only for the `resolveTerm`
     `<element ref>` particle site. Two markers already point at it as owner
     (`xsd/resolve.go:453`, `parser/doc.go:127`), so it must not be closed
     without retiring them.
  2. **#342 is UNBLOCKED by this landing** (`blocked`, `kind/gap`,
     `area/parser` — *"dcl.elt.common clause 3 — an element inherits `{type
     definition}` from its substitutionGroup head"*). Its entire blocker was
     that no producer read `substitutionGroup=`; one does now. **Relabel it on
     the post-land pass** — drop `blocked`, add `ready`. Its marker at
     `parser/produce_complex.go:1200` names it, so it is not orphaned a second
     time, but a marker whose owner is mislabelled `blocked` after the blocker
     cleared is the next version of the same problem.
  3. **#249 is now live in substance.** Its exact `cos-equiv-derived-ok-rec`
     clause 2.3 predicate landed in `2421d17` over a universally-empty
     affiliation set; this landing is what gives it data. No action, but the
     next session touching substitution groups should know the predicate is now
     being exercised for the first time.
  4. **The bare `GAP(` at `produce_complex.go:1070-1082` (the `ref=` form) is
     intentionally unowned and should STAY unowned** unless a W3C case with the
     shape appears — filing an issue for a hole with zero suite coverage and a
     safe direction is the dead-end P3 warns about from the other side. Recorded
     here so a later reader does not mistake the absence of an issue number for
     an oversight.
  5. **#270 item 2 (§F.1 chameleon rewriting of `@substitutionGroup`) is now a
     live under-rewrite, not a theoretical one.** A chameleon-included document
     using `substitutionGroup=` will be silently under-rewritten with no test
     failure. `substitutionGroupAffiliations` routes through `p.resolveQName`,
     which does apply §F.1 task (b)'s coercion to an unqualified item, so the
     common case is covered — but #270's own scope is wider and nothing here
     closed it.

## 2026-08-02 — #334 — parser: `allGroupOf` stops equating "is an all group" with "is an inline `<all>`" and resolves a `<group ref>` particle's `{term}` to the referenced definition's real `{compositor}`, extending the established prescan+memo pattern to named `<group>`s; the oracle's decisive finding was that §3.4.2.3.3 clause 4.2.3 is stated purely over the COMPONENT graph with no XML-shape carve-out, and the arbiter accepted on round 1 with the ratchet proved flat by a write-free `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` run

- Shipped on `wip/issue-334` as ONE commit on top of `034ef49` (#301) —
  `f4610cb` (`parser: allGroupOf resolves <group ref> to its definition's
  compositor (#334)`) — **3 files, +308/−38**: `parser/produce.go` (+138/−…,
  the symbol index, the memo and the two new producer methods),
  `parser/produce_complex.go` (+104/−…, `allGroupOf`/`extensionParticle`/
  `extensionContentType` receiver-ified and the `GAP(xsd)` marker retired),
  `parser/produce_extension_test.go` (+104, three new tests). **Zero arbiter
  repair rounds, ACCEPT on round 1**, no re-grounding, no scope change.
- **The defect, exactly.** `allGroupOf` decided §3.4.2.3.3 clause 4.2.3's
  sub-case (4.2.3.1 / 4.2.3.2 / 4.2.3.3 `c-suffix-extension`) by type-asserting
  a particle's `{term}` straight to `xsd.ModelGroup`, so it recognized only an
  **inline `<all>`**. A `<group ref>` particle's `{term}` is an
  `xsd.ModelGroupRef` — unresolved at produce time — so an all-bodied model
  group reached through a reference always fell through to the general
  4.2.3.3 case and synthesized `sequence[all, all]`, the shape `cos-all-limited`
  clause 1 (§3.8.6.2) structurally forbids. Harmless **today only** because
  goxsd8 charges `cos-all-limited` clause 1 on the source XML in
  `produceGroupParticle`, never on the finalized component — the contingency
  the #228 post-land comment on this thread had already spelled out for the
  4.2.3.1 half too ("the benign half" is benign on the acceptance axis alone;
  the shape is equally illegal).
- **Ratchet: unchanged in ALL lanes — RUN by the arbiter, not asserted.** The
  arbiter's figures, copied verbatim from the verdict and not recomputed: the
  `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` pass **wrote no expectations file at all** and the working
  tree was still clean afterwards — zero movement, confirmed rather than
  assumed. The structural reason was verified independently by mason and the
  arbiter: `conformance/schema.go`'s `complexTypeDecidable` (`:744`) declines
  every `<simpleContent>` (`:745`) and every `<complexContent>` without a
  `<restriction>` child (`:748-757`), so **no lane admits an `<extension>` shape
  that reaches clause 4.2.3** and none can observe this fix.
- **The change, exactly.**
  1. **`symbols.modelGroups`** (`parser/produce.go:170`, populated at `:425`) —
     a prescan index from each top-level named `<group>`'s expanded name to its
     source element, beside the existing `simpleTypes`/`complexTypes`/
     `attributeGroups` indexes.
  2. **`symbols.builtGroups`** (`:229`) — the tri-state memo (absent /
     on-stack `nil` sentinel / done), mirroring `built`/`builtComplex`.
  3. **`buildModelGroupDefinition`** (`:708`) — the memoized on-demand producer
     mirroring `buildComplexType`, and now the **single entry point** for
     mapping a named `<group>`, including from `run`'s own document-order pass
     (`:562`, which no longer calls `produceModelGroupDefinition` directly).
  4. **`resolveModelGroup`** (`:746`) — the read `allGroupOf` uses
     (`produce_complex.go:514`) to follow a `ModelGroupRef` to the `{model
     group}` §3.7.2 `xr.mgd3` makes that particle's `{term}`, then test its real
     `{compositor}`.
  5. **The `GAP(xsd)` marker on `extensionParticle` and its cross-reference in
     `allGroupOf` are RETIRED — removed, not reworded** (STYLE P3), which the
     arbiter verified by grep: zero `GAP(` markers anywhere in the tree
     reference #334, and the two surviving markers in `produce_complex.go`
     (`:1200`, `:1265`) belong to #346/#265 and are untouched.
- **Single mapping is a CORRECTNESS requirement, not thrift.** Mapping the same
  `<group>` twice would re-register every named `<key>`/`<unique>`/`<keyref>` in
  its body and fabricate a spurious `sch-props-correct` clause 2 collision.
  That is why `run` had to be routed through the memoized builder rather than
  left with a second, parallel entry point — and the arbiter mutation-proved it
  by neutering only the memo hit (`if mgd := …; false && mgd != nil`), which
  makes `TestProduceExtensionGroupRefMapsDefinitionOnce` fail with exactly the
  predicted `[sch-props-correct] schema {identity-constraint definitions}[1]
  repeats the expanded name {urn:x}k` error.
- **Three new tests in `parser/produce_extension_test.go`, all four mutations
  reproduced by the arbiter.** Stubbing `allGroupOf`'s `xsd.ModelGroupRef`
  branch back to the old behaviour (`return xsd.ModelGroup{}, false, nil`) fails
  all three: `TestProduceExtensionAllPlusAllMergesThroughGroupRef` → *"merged
  {compositor} = sequence, want all"* (**4.2.3.2** through a `<group ref>`
  base); `TestProduceExtensionAllBaseThroughGroupRefWithEmptyExplicitContent` →
  the derived particle is the 4.2.3.3 sequence wrapper instead of the base
  particle (**4.2.3.1**); `TestProduceExtensionGroupRefMapsDefinitionOnce` →
  *"particle term is xsd.ModelGroupRef, want an inline ResolvedTerm"* (forward
  reference + the single-mapping guarantee). The first test additionally asserts
  that B's own `{term}` stays a retained `xsd.ModelGroupRef`, which pins the
  design's load-bearing claim that **this is a read, never a rewrite**.
- **Grounding — the spec gives the reference case no dispensation.** §3.4.2.3.3
  clause 4.2.3 and its three sub-cases are stated entirely over `{term}` /
  `{compositor}` of already-mapped Particle and Model Group components and
  **never mention the XML representation**; §3.7.2 `declare-namedModelGroup`
  makes a `<group ref>` particle's `{term}` literally *"the {model group} of the
  model group definition ·resolved· to by the ·actual value· of the ref
  attribute"* — the resolved component itself, not a name or deferred handle
  (§3.9.1 and the Term definition allow only Element Declaration, Wildcard or
  Model Group there, never a reference-shaped fourth thing). The `cos-nonambig`
  note (§3.8.6.4) is the general statement of the principle: component-level
  rules apply *"to content models whose origins (e.g. via type ·derivation· and
  references to named model groups) are no longer evident."* So testing source
  XML shape for a component constraint is unsound, and the fix is obligatory,
  not stylistic. Grounding:
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/334#issuecomment-5160135491>.
- Decisions:
  1. **Resolve at PRODUCE time by extending the existing pattern, rather than
     deferring the sub-case choice to finalize.** Two shapes were possible: a
     new produce-time symbol index (chosen), or letting finalize — where refs
     are already followed — recompute the sub-case. Produce-time resolution won
     on architectural consistency: it extends an established, proven mechanism
     used for the *identical* forward-reference problem three times over, where
     the alternative would invent finalize-time recomputation of an
     already-synthesized `{content type}`.
  2. **No verdict moves to produce time.** A ref resolving to nothing, or one
     closing a `<group ref>` cycle (the memo's on-stack state), simply reports
     "not an all group" and falls through. `src-resolve` clause 1.5 and
     `mg-props-correct` clause 2 stay **finalize's**, where the whole graph is
     visible — one fact, one encoding.
- Surprises:
  1. **The single-entry-point requirement was discovered from the failure mode,
     not from the design.** The obvious minimal change — add `resolveModelGroup`
     and leave `run` alone — is wrong for a reason nothing in the issue body
     predicted: double registration of identity constraints. The memo therefore
     carries a correctness role, not a performance one, which is also what makes
     it survive STYLE D3 ("no caches without a measured hot path").
  2. **The cycle guard is UNTESTABLE today, and the honest response was to ship
     it untested rather than fake a test.** The natural test — a local
     `<element>` with an inline `<complexType>` inside a `<group>` body — hits an
     unrelated pre-existing block (`parser: a local <element> with an inline
     <complexType> is not yet produced (#340, blocked on #301)`) before any
     re-entry can occur. The arbiter confirmed **both halves** by probe: the
     precondition is genuinely blocked, and an ordinary two-node cross-group
     cycle (`G → H → G`) reached through a clause-4.2.3 base terminates and is
     rejected at finalize with `[mg-props-correct] model group definition
     {urn:x}G participates in a circular <group ref> chain` — no panic, no hang,
     no produce-time verdict fabricated. The `started && mgd != nil` half of the
     tri-state IS exercised (by the memo test); only the `mgd == nil` half is
     not, and it is one branch returning `false`.
  3. **`run`'s own call had to change, which the issue had not anticipated** —
     the arbiter checked repo-wide that `produceModelGroupDefinition` now has
     exactly one other caller, `parser/produce_scope_internal_test.go:44`, which
     builds its own fresh producer and documents doing so deliberately.
- Friction:
  1. **None charged this session** — no repair round, no re-grounding, no scope
     surprise, and no gate re-run. The full gate was green first time (build,
     vet, `go test ./...`, `golangci-lint run` **0 issues**, conformance pass in
     12.7s), and the T5 exported-surface check came back **byte-identical**
     (`go doc -all -short` over every package, base `034ef49` vs HEAD): nothing
     new is exported, every new identifier is unexported `parser` internals.
  2. **Mason posted no implementation summary to the issue thread.** The thread
     carries the #228 post-land note, the grounding and the verdict, but no
     mason comment — so the file-by-file account exists only in this entry and
     the commit message. Not a defect in the change; worth watching whether it
     recurs, since CLAUDE.md makes the issue thread the cross-session channel.
  3. **[carried, unchanged]** #426 (the `golangci-lint` `PATH` seam) is still
     filed and unowned; #430, #346, #336, #400, #347, #420 and #270 remain
     undischarged from earlier sessions, as does the unfiled empty-NCName
     successor booked on #301's entry above.
- Next — **four deferred items for the post-land pass; none is filed yet and
  none was filed by the chronicler** (cartographer's harvest owns them):
  1. **`docs/PLAN.md` staleness.** `:1164` (the GAP ledger row for #334) and
     `:1173-1175` (a back-reference to the old `parser/produce_complex.go:491`
     line) are stale as of this merge. The arbiter ruled this **non-blocking and
     correctly left alone**: STYLE P3 governs `GAP(` markers in code, that
     obligation is discharged, and the ledger is a point-in-time snapshot in the
     cartographer's own file whose line numbers are already stale across many
     rows.
  2. **`parser/produce_xpath.go:124-127`'s doc comment overclaims.** It asserts
     identity-constraint registration happens *"never at a demand-driven build
     site, so the registered order stays document order (STYLE D2)"*. That
     sentence was **already imprecise on `034ef49`** — `buildComplexType`'s
     on-demand base builds do exactly this, verified by probe — and #334 adds a
     **second route to the same pre-existing behaviour**, not a new class. The
     behaviour is spec-immaterial (§3.17.1 makes `{identity-constraint
     definitions}` a set) and fully deterministic. The comment should be
     corrected to stop overclaiming; the line is not in this diff.
  3. **`extensionParticle` resolves the effective content's term
     unconditionally**, even when the base is not an all group and the answer
     cannot affect the outcome. Flagged by mason as symmetric-with-the-spec-text
     and cheap (a memoized build `run` performs anyway), and **accepted as-is by
     the arbiter** — short-circuiting would add nesting for no correctness gain,
     and the only consequence is which of two independent faults wins,
     deterministically either way. Recorded here only in case a future session
     wants it.
  4. **[T4 watch item, arbiter's own]** This is the **third** instance of the
     prescan-index + tri-state-memo build function (`buildSimpleType`,
     `buildComplexType`, now `buildModelGroupDefinition`; `buildAttributeGroup`
     has no memo). The commit message explains why it mirrors rather than
     unifies, which satisfies T4 as written — but three near-identical bodies is
     where **a steward pass should start asking**, and this is not this change's
     debt to pay. Flag it at the next `/retro` Part 2.
- Thread record (do not duplicate) —
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/334>: the #228 post-land

  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/334#issuecomment-5160135491> (the

  write-free ratchet run, the byte-identical `go doc -all -short` surface diff

  rule-by-rule STYLE pass, and the three non-blocking post-land notes). Prior

  `attributeGroups` prescan indexes and the `built`/`builtComplex` memos
  (`parser/produce.go`), which this change extends rather than parallels.

## 2026-08-02 — #340 — parser: an `<xs:element>` carrying an inline anonymous `<xs:complexType>` produces that type and wires it as `{type definition}` (§3.3.2.1 `dcl.elt.common` tier 1) — on the GLOBAL path as well as the local one the issue scoped it to, because both of the issue's own named acceptance cohorts turned out to be top-level; the warden ran TWICE (pre-flight before code, diff review after) and caught 2 design errors plus 2 residues without a single arbiter rejection, and the arbiter accepted with the schema lane at **+4872, zero regressions**

- Shipped on `wip/issue-340` in four commits on top of `034ef49`/`93c8699`:
  **`2bb6641`** (`parser: produce the inline anonymous <complexType> of a local
  or global <element> (#340)` — the whole change), **`217cc88`**
  (`xsd/parser: address warden diff-review findings on #340` — the two required
  one-line fixes), **`9e8bfec`** (merge of `origin/main` after #334 landed under
  the branch), and **`66eca9e`** (`parser: correct builtGroups doc — #340 makes
  the sentinel reachable, not dead` — the arbiter's one T6 finding), with the
  arbiter's ratchet commit **`f91e82e`** between the last two. **19 files,
  +6121/−5142** against `origin/main`, of which
  `conformance/testdata/expectations/schema.txt` is 9744 lines — the code diff
  is ~1500 lines across `xsd/elementdeclaration.go` (+286),

  `conformance/schema.go` (+86), `xsd/complextype.go` (+81), and two new test
  files (`parser/produce_inlinecomplex_test.go` +338,
  `parser/produce_complexidentity_internal_test.go` +82). **Zero arbiter repair
  rounds — ACCEPT on round 1.**
- **Ratchet — the arbiter's figures, copied verbatim from the verdict and not
  recomputed.** `schema` **4283 pass / 11149 fail → 9155 pass / 6277 fail,
  +4872**; `datatypes` 1107/22 unchanged; `instance` 0/26426 unchanged;
