  of them ` fail`; 4872 lines added, every one of them ` pass`; total case count
  unchanged at 15432**. **0 regressed / 0 new-fail / 0 vanished** — a pure
  fail→pass rewrite. No file under `conformance/testdata/expectations/` appeared
  in the branch diff before the arbiter's own run, so nothing was hand-edited.
- **The two banked cohorts, reported SEPARATELY as the issue body demanded.**
  `saxonData/PDecimal/pdecimal0NN` — **17 newly pass** of 21 in the family (was
  zero passes); the four residuals (`pdecimal001a`, `pdecimal006`,
  `pdecimal019`, `pdecimal020`) fail on unrelated mechanisms.
  `MS-Regex2006-07-15/p16`–`p21` — **all 6 newly pass**, both polarities (p16 a
  valid instance, p18 an invalid one). And as #229's arbiter warned — and as
  #229's own unforecast +1190 from `msData/datatypes/Facets` demonstrated — the
  *bulk* came from somewhere entirely ordinary: **1986 flips in
  `MS-Regex2006-07-15`**, 617 in `MS-IdentityConstraint2006-07-15`, 571 in
  `MS-Particles2006-07-15`, 393 in `MS-DataTypes2006-07-15`. The arbiter
  spot-checked flipped fixtures (`AG_attrUseNS00101m1_n.xsd`,
  `AG_attrWCard00101m1.xsd`) and they carry exactly the `<xsd:element
  name="X"><xsd:complexType>` global inline shape.
- **The scope finding, and why it decided the whole landing.** The issue's
  `## Surface` scoped this to LOCAL elements only. Mason's design pre-flight
  checked the two cohorts the issue itself had banked as acceptance evidence and
  found **both are on the GLOBAL path**: `saxonData/PDecimal/pdecimal002.xsd`
  declares `<xs:element name="doc">` and `<xs:element name="e">` at top level,
  and `msData/regex/p16.xsd`'s still-declining shape is the top-level
  `<xsd:element name="doc">`, not the inline `simpleType` on the named type's
  attribute (that half landed with #229). Neither cohort contains a local
  `<element>` with an inline `<complexType>` at all — so a strictly-local
  landing would have banked **zero** of its own acceptance evidence and reported
  "unchanged" on both lines. Mason escalated it rather than deciding it, and the
  **warden ruled the global arm IN SCOPE for this same landing** (ruling 1) on
  three grounds: §3.3.2.1 tier 1 is a **common** rule — §3.3.2.3 supplements
  only `{target namespace}` and `{scope}`, never `{type definition}`, so
  producing it at one call site and not the other repeats the very split this
  issue exists to close (T4); the machinery is one mechanism at two call sites
  (`NewGlobalScope()` vs `NewLocalScope(...)` is the entire delta); and the
  ratchet risk is bounded and monotone, since `conformance/schema.go:386`
  records an undecidable case as `fail`, so widening the gate can only move
  fail→pass. The arbiter's post-hoc verdict is that this ruling **is** the
  +4872: "a local-only landing would have banked near zero."
- **One `ComponentID` per inline construct, three readers, no second scheme.**
  #301's handle is minted by the producer *before* either endpoint exists and
  then threaded to exactly three places: the anonymous `ComplexType`'s
  `{context}` (`ElementDeclarationContext{Component: id}`, §3.4.2.1
  `dcl.ctd.common`), the new `AnonymousComplexTypeScopeParent{Owner: id}` that
  every local element nested in that type's content model reports as
  `{scope}.{parent}` (§3.3.2.3 `dcl.elt.local`), and a construction-time
  identity comparison inside `NewElementDeclarationOwningType`. One mint, one
  fact, three readers — no `Anonymous bool`, and no second identity minted for
  the complex type itself (#301 pointedly declined to add one). Every comparison
  is `==`; `reflect.DeepEqual` is identity-BLIND on `ComponentID` (measured on
  #301: two distinct minted IDs compare `DeepEqual == true`), so no test here
  may use it.
- **The change, exactly.**
  1. **`xsd.AnonymousComplexTypeScopeParent{Owner ComponentID}`** — a THIRD
     sealed arm of `ElementScopeParent`, not a new field on the existing one.
     `ComplexTypeScopeParent{Name QName}` is byte-identical to `main`; zero
     literals broke. `elementScopeParentName` is replaced by a per-arm
     `checkElementScopeParent`, which rejects a zero `Owner` on the anonymous
     arm on the same `e-props-correct` clause 1 footing the named arm's
     zero-`Name` rejection already had.
  2. **`xsd.NewElementDeclarationOwningType(loc, id, name, definition
     ComplexType, …)`** — the ONLY construction path for a declaration owning an
     anonymous complex type. It takes the `ComplexType` directly rather than a
     `TypeDefinitionOrRef` and wraps it internally, which makes both illegal
     states unrepresentable rather than checked (an inline anonymous CT with no
     identity to compare against; an identity with no inline CT). It checks
     `id != ComponentID{}`, checks `ctx.ID() == id`, and rejects a
     `ComplexTypeDefinitionContext` in this slot (§3.4.2.1 has exactly one case
     and it yields an Element Declaration). `NewElementDeclaration` keeps its 13
     parameters and now **rejects** an `InlineTypeDefinition` wrapping a
     `ComplexType`, pointing callers at the new door. `id` is **not stored** —
     under this shape its only role is the construction-time comparison, and a
     field written and never read is dead state (STYLE T5).
  3. **`complexTypeIdentity`** (unexported, `parser`) — a construction-path
     partition (`namedComplexType(name)` / `anonymousComplexType(owner)`)
     replacing the bare `name xsd.QName` threaded through `produceComplexType` /
     `produceImplicitContent` / `produceSimpleContent` / `produceComplexContent`.
     Its `scopeParent()`/`newComplexType()` **assert on the zero value** rather
     than silently emit an empty arm. The named arm's `name.Local == ""`
     rejection stays FIRST and unconditional, so the verdict still never depends
     on whether the content happens to hold a local element; the anonymous arm
     cannot reach it (it carries no name to be missing, and an unminted owner is
     unconstructible because both producers mint before calling). The anonymous
     path calls `produceComplexType` **directly**, bypassing `buildComplexType`'s
     name-keyed memo — an anonymous type has no name to be referenced by, so it
     can be no cycle member (STYLE 5).
  4. **An `<element>` with BOTH inline type children is now rejected
     explicitly**, on both paths, as a plain grammar fault — no `src-*` clause
     states it (clause 3 names "`<simpleType>` OR `<complexType>` child AND a
     `type` attribute", never both children together), so no rule verdict is
     fabricated. This was a warden finding: under the design as first written,
     the complexType arm would have silently WON.
  5. **The residual global inline-`<simpleType>` decline is re-charged** from a
     fabricated `ruleSrcElement` verdict on a perfectly legal schema
     (`produce.go:838`) to a plain `fmt.Errorf` limitation error (STYLE E2).
     Producing that arm is a separate, adjacent widening and was deliberately
     NOT taken here.
- **Decision: the anonymous complex type is NOT registered in
  `Schema.Types()`/`s.types`** — no `SchemaBuilder.AddType` call, positively
  tested. Three grounds, all three verified by the warden:
  1. **The spec's own mapping.** §3.17.2 (`xmlschema11-1.md:3791`) scopes
     `{type definitions}` to the `<simpleType>`/`<complexType>` items "**in the
     [children]**" of `<schema>` — top level only — while widening
     `{identity-constraint definitions}` to "anywhere within the [children]".
     The spec widens for exactly one property, and this is not it.
  2. **It would falsify an already-published contract.** `xsd/schema.go:436-441`
     says `Types()` is "NOT transitive coverage of every type reachable from the
     schema. An inline (nested) type definition owned by a declaration is never
     added to this set" — registering would falsify that in the very landing
     that first makes it testable.
  3. **It would fork one component in two.** `ComplexType` is a VALUE type;
     `InlineTypeDefinition{Definition: ct}` holds its own copy, and
     `storeFoldedAttributeUses` mutates `s.types[i]` in place post-finalize.
     Registering does not make the fold reach the owner's copy — it makes the
     `s.types` copy **diverge** from it, so a consumer reading `schema.Types()`
     would see folded attribute uses and one reading `ed.TypeDefinition()` would
     not. Registration does not solve warden's #229 finding-5 concern; it
     creates a worse and silent one (STYLE 4/D3).
- **The disclosed consequence, and the narrow gate that bounds it.** Finalize
  Phase A **does** reach the anonymous type, through the owning declaration via
  `resolveElementDecl` → `resolveTypeDefinition`'s `ComplexType` arm — the arm
  #229's arbiter flagged as unreachable-and-untested, which this landing both
  activates AND finally tests. What never visits it is everything that
  quantifies over `s.types`: `checkContentModelsUnambiguous` (`cos-nonambig`),
  `checkElementDeclarationsConsistent` (`cos-element-consistent`), and
  `checkComplexDerivations` with the `checkAttributeUseNamesUnique`
  (`ct-props-correct` clause 4) and `checkExtensionAttributeUses`
  (`cos-ct-extends` clause 1.2) it drives — plus both attribute folds. So
  `conformance/schema.go`'s new **`anonymousComplexTypeDecidable` admits the
  IMPLICIT-CONTENT form ONLY**, at every nesting depth: on that shape
  `{base type definition}` is always `xs:anyType` (§3.4.2.3.2), whose
  `{attribute uses}` are empty and which has no wildcard to union, so both unrun
  folds are **provably the identity**. A `GAP(xsd)` marker sits on the PRODUCER
  (`parser/produce_complex.go:185`), not only on the gate, and names every
  reader that produces no verdict, the direction (open / under-rejection today,
  never fail-closed), and the two owning issues.
- **Successor filed BEFORE landing: #438**, per warden ruling 5's explicit
  precondition. It owns the three read-only finalize passes and **depends on
  #414** (which owns the sibling fold-widening question), with the sequencing
  constraint stated in the issue and repeated in the GAP marker: **folds first,
  read-only walks second, never the reverse.** The warden rejected widening the
  read-only walks in this same landing as *unsafe, not merely bigger*:
  `checkExtensionAttributeUses` iterates `b.attributeUses` and rejects when a
  base use is missing from the derived set, assuming both sides are
  materialised — routing it through anonymous types while the folds still cannot
  reach them would make every anonymous `<complexContent><extension>` a **FALSE
  rejection**, fail-closed, in a landing whose entire point is banking wins.
- **Process data point: the warden ran TWICE, and both runs paid for
  themselves.** Unusual for this repo and recorded as a workflow observation,
  not a failure — no arbiter rejection was issued at any point.
  - **Pre-flight (before any code was written), verdict `revise` on 5 escalated
    questions.** It caught **two design errors while they were still free**:
    (a) mason's proposed `ComplexTypeScopeParent{Name, Owner}` checked-XOR was
    rejected as a straight T1 violation — mason's own doc sentence "Exactly one
    of the two fields is present" is T1's literal trigger, and the #301
    precedent mason cited points the *other* way
    (`xsd/complextype.go:372-379` says the XOR "needs no runtime check, because
    it is enforced by a CONSTRUCTION-PATH PARTITION") — replaced by the third
    sealed arm above, at a cost of 2 exported identifiers and zero broken
    literals; (b) a 14th parameter on `NewElementDeclaration` was rejected in
    favour of the new dedicated constructor, which cost **zero churn at ~50
    existing call sites** across 16 files. Rulings 1 (global path in scope), 3
    (`Owner` naming stands) and 5 (narrow gate + successor issue) were ruled as
    designed.
  - **Diff review (after `2bb6641`), verdict `accept`** with two required
    one-line fixes, both landed in `217cc88` with no re-review needed: a **T6
    doc-comment overshoot** — `xsd/componentid.go`'s repoint claimed #438 needs
    an ID→component resolver, but #438's own body reaches anonymous types by
    *traversal from the owning declaration*, so it will never build one — and an
    **undisclosed reordering** in `produceElement`, where the
    `type=`/`resolveQName` block had moved after `identityConstraintsOf` /
    `substitutionGroupAffiliations`. Lane-neutral, but unnecessary
    (`inlineComplex != nil` already implies `hasType == false`) and undisclosed;
    restored to its `origin/main` position rather than justified after the fact.
- **Surprise 1 — `origin/main` moved under the branch mid-implementation, into
  the same two files.** #334 landed (`2f9c0c4..93c8699`), touching
  `parser/produce.go` and `parser/produce_complex.go`. Git auto-merged clean at
  `9e8bfec`, and the arbiter **checked the semantics rather than trusting the
  merge**: the two changes occupy disjoint regions (#334 in `extensionParticle`/
  `allGroupOf`/`produceGroupRefParticle`, #340 in `produceComplexType` at the
  top of the file and `produceLocalElement` at the bottom), and #340's
  `id.scopeParent()` threading flows into #334's rewritten `complexContentType`
  correctly. Mason's pre-merge prediction of +4872 held **exactly** through the
  merge, because #334 was itself ratchet-neutral.
- **Surprise 2 — this landing turns #334's "unreachable" cycle sentinel into the
  LIVE guard, and the arbiter proved it rather than assuming it.** #334 shipped
  `builtGroups`' on-stack `nil` sentinel with a doc comment saying that state
  was "unreachable TODAY", because the only definition-body child that could
  re-enter complex-type construction — a local `<element>` with an inline
  `<complexType>` — was not yet produced (#340). It is now. The arbiter built
  that exact cycle (`<group name="G">` → local element → inline `<complexType>`
  → `<complexContent><extension>` → `<group ref="G">`) and ran it: **the
  sentinel fires, `resolveModelGroup` reports "does not resolve here", the
  caller falls through to clause 4.2.3.3, and produce completes** — no runaway
  recursion, no stack death. #334 wrote that guard anticipating precisely this
  landing, and it works. Which is why the single arbiter finding was a *comment*
  and not a bug: left as written, the doc told a future reader that a
  load-bearing termination guard was dead code — the standing invitation to
  delete it. Corrected in `66eca9e`. **Neither warden review could have caught
  this**: `origin/main` moved after both. It is documentation drift introduced
  by a merge, in the one file both landings touched.
- **Surprise 3 — mason's call-site count was factually wrong, and the warden
  checked it instead of accepting it.** The design pre-flight asserted that
  `xsd/example_test.go:78` was the only in-repo external caller of
  `NewElementDeclaration`; `xsd/elementdeclaration_test.go` alone has 20, and
  the real figure is ~50 across 16 files. That error was load-bearing for the
  rejected 14th-parameter proposal — unchecked, "one small signature change"
  would have been a fifty-site edit.
- Friction:
  1. **Mason posted no implementation summary to the issue thread — the SECOND
     consecutive landing with this gap** (#334's entry, immediately above this
     one, recorded the same thing). The thread carries the grounding, the design
     pre-flight, both warden verdicts and the arbiter verdict, but the
     file-by-file account of what was actually built exists only in `2bb6641`'s
     commit message and this entry. Two in a row is a pattern, not a one-off,
     and CLAUDE.md makes the issue thread the cross-session channel. **Flag at
     the next `/retro`.**
  2. **The issue body's `## Surface` was wrong about the very evidence the issue
     itself banked.** It scoped the work to local elements while both of its
     named acceptance cohorts are global — an error that survived a post-land
     harvest pass and two body rewrites, and that would have produced a
     technically-correct landing with a "ratchet: unchanged" line had mason not
     checked the fixtures instead of the prose. This enters the pipeline at the
     **issue body**, so the fix belongs with the cartographer: where a body
     names specific acceptance fixtures, `## Surface` should be derived from
     them rather than asserted alongside them. Retro material.
  3. **[carried, unchanged]** #426 (the `golangci-lint` `PATH` seam — the
     arbiter again had to run the binary from `/Users/kud360/go/bin`, not
     `PATH`) is still filed and unowned; #430, #346, #336, #400, #347 and #270
     remain undischarged from earlier sessions. #433 (the empty-`NCName`
     successor split out of #301) now has its precondition settled — this
     landing did NOT grow an identity arm on `ComplexTypeScopeParent`, it added
     a new sealed sibling instead, so the surviving-guard set is knowable and
     should be said on #433's thread.
- Next — deferred items, **none filed by the chronicler** (the post-land harvest
  owns them):
  1. **`xsd/attributedeclaration.go:113` still accepts an illegal
     `InlineTypeDefinition{Definition: ComplexType}` on an attribute
     declaration.** Pre-existing, but the asymmetry is now sharper: the element
     side rejects that shape and the attribute side does not, though §3.2.2.2
     makes an attribute's `{type definition}` always a Simple Type. Warden's
     advisory says "worth a follow-up issue, not a change here" — **no issue is
     filed for it yet; this is an open ledger item.**
  2. **`anonymousComplexTypeDecidable` is redundant with `contentDecidable`'s
     `default` arm and is pinned by NO test.** The arbiter mutation-tested it:
     deleting the explicit narrowing fails nothing. It is correct and worth
     keeping (insurance for the day `contentDecidable` widens, and it carries a
     different rationale — the unrun folds — than `contentDecidable`'s "not
     produced"), but its safety argument is currently unpinned.
  3. **The four residual `PDecimal` failures** (`pdecimal001a`, `pdecimal006`,
     `pdecimal019`, `pdecimal020`) are on unrelated mechanisms — worth a look by
     whoever next touches that family, not by this landing.
  4. **An oracle question is owed** on an `<element>` carrying BOTH a
     `<simpleType>` and a `<complexType>` child. This landing rejects it as a
     plain grammar fault because no `src-*` clause states it, which is the
     honest reading, but the schema-for-schema-documents grounding was not
     confirmed by the oracle in this session — warden asked for the question to
     be filed as a follow-up either way.
  5. **Advisories the warden and arbiter explicitly parked**: the gate asymmetry
     on the both-inline-children shape (the local path admits it and then
     correctly fails it as invalid, the global path declines outright — harmless
     in both directions, recorded so it is not later mistaken for a bug);
     `produce_complex.go`'s `explicitContent` doc comment now incomplete
     (cosmetic); and `NewElementDeclarationOwningType` cannot detect two
     declarations passing the same `id` + `ComplexType` (undetectable without an
     own-identity registry, unreachable from the parser today, moot until a
     resolver exists — recorded for that resolver's future issue).
- Thread record (do not duplicate) —
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/340>: the GROUNDING (§3.3.2.1
  `dcl.elt.common`'s verbatim 4-tier tableau and its status as a COMMON rule,
  `src-element` clause 3's unconditional standing, §3.4.1 `ctd-context` and
  §3.4.2.1's "nearest `<element>` ancestor" computation, and the warning not to
  conflate `{context}` with `{scope}.{parent}` — two properties on two different
  components); mason's DESIGN PRE-FLIGHT (the §0 global-path finding with its
  fixture evidence, the `s.types` decision with its three grounds, the per-phase
  finalize accounting, and the five escalations); the WARDEN PRE-FLIGHT VERDICT
  (`revise`, rulings 1-5 plus five additional findings); the #438 filing note;
  the WARDEN DIFF REVIEW (`accept`, the point-by-point confirmation that all
  five rulings were implemented rather than approximated, the two required fixes

  `ComplexTypeContext` handle scheme (`034ef49`) and #229's `TypeDefinitionOrRef`
  / `InlineTypeDefinition` sum (`aa503c9`), both extended rather than
  paralleled. **Exported surface: exactly 2 new identifiers** —
  `xsd.AnonymousComplexTypeScopeParent` and
  `xsd.NewElementDeclarationOwningType` — both documented, both with real
  in-diff consumers (the parser calls both), and no accessor added for the
  identity (no consumer; STYLE T5). This entry is WORKFLOW step 6, written on

  in the squash; the squash message carries `Spec: dcl.elt.common,

## 2026-08-02 — #365 — conformance: the 24 `xs:int`/`xs:integer` plain lexical fixtures #331 left out of its enumerated 48 are claimed by widening ONE regexp alternation and touching no engine code; the arbiter rejected round 1 not for the code — which it verified figure-for-figure and found sound — but for a comment that replaced #331's undercount with a larger OVERCLAIM ("nothing is left unclaimed"), the exact defect class this issue exists to repair, and the enumerated 20-file remainder the repair wrote in its place is now a ready-made follow-on

- Shipped on `wip/issue-365` as two mason commits plus the arbiter's ratchet
  commit on top of `8b9bd3e` (#340) — `9ce10db` (`conformance: claim
  xs:int/xs:integer lexical fixtures via the existing lexical-facets route
  (#365)`), `4fa2861` (`conformance: correct #365's over-claiming prose per
  arbiter round-1 finding (#365)`) and `ad37961` (`conformance: bank ratchet for
  #365`), **3 files, +214/−15, all under `conformance/`**:
  `conformance/datatypes_test.go` (+153/−4, one new test plus two flipped pins),
  `conformance/datatypes.go` (+52/−15, of which the *only* non-comment line is
  the regexp), and `conformance/testdata/expectations/datatypes.txt` (+24, the
  banked lane). No package outside `conformance/` was touched — no `xsd/`, no
  `value/`, no `builtin/`, no `parser/`. **One arbiter repair round, ACCEPT on
  round 2.**
- **Ratchet: `datatypes` 1107/1129 → 1131/1153 (+24); `instance` and `schema`
  unchanged** (0/26426 and 9155/15432). The arbiter's figures, copied verbatim
  from the round-2 FINAL verdict and not recomputed: *"24 insertions, zero
  deletions, zero modified rows — every flip upward, none downward,
  `instance.txt` and `schema.txt` untouched. All 24 are `int001-008` +
  `integer001-016` marked `pass`, and every one is explained by the selector
  widening under judgment. No unexplained flip."* The same figure was measured
  at round 1 in a **detached throwaway worktree that was discarded** (`0
  Regressed, 0 Vanished, 0 Improved`), so it survived the repair round unmoved
  and was only banked once, on accept.
- **The change, exactly: one alternation, two literals.**
  `conformance/datatypes.go:553`'s `datatypesCase` gained `integer|int` at the
  tail of its existing family alternation, which routes the 24 fixtures through
  the **pre-existing** `execLexicalCase` → `decideLexicalByFacets` →
  `value.ValidateLexical` pipeline that #331 built. `TestDatatypesSelectorClaimsOnlyCohort`'s
  `int001.xml` and `integer001.xml` rows were **flipped** from negative to
  positive — #331 planted them negative precisely so this widening would have to
  argue for itself — and a new `int.xsd` negative row was added. **Zero engine
  code.** The arbiter said this out loud as the diff's strongest evidence: the
  route absorbed a family with *no bounds at all* without a line of engine
  change, which is the widest #331's generalization has yet been read.
- **Spec, and the grounding's decisive contribution was an ORDERING ruling.**
  The oracle confirmed every citation in the issue body was correct as filed —
  `cvc-datatype-valid` §4.1.4 clauses 1 (`dv_pattern`), 2.1 (`dv_atomic`) and 3
  (`dv_vfacets`); `cvc-pattern-valid` §4.3.4.4; `cvc-fractionDigits-valid`
  §4.3.12.3; `cvc-minInclusive-valid` §4.3.10.3; `cvc-maxInclusive-valid`
  §4.3.7.3 — and confirmed the asymmetry the whole issue turns on: `xs:integer`
  (§3.4.13.3) carries fixed `fractionDigits = 0`, a fixable `pattern
  [\-+]?[0-9]+` and **no bounds whatsoever**, while `xs:int` (§3.4.17.3) adds
  `maxInclusive = 2147483647` / `minInclusive = -2147483648`. The load-bearing
  addition was the edge case: clause 3 reads *"V, **as determined by** the
  appropriate sub-clause of clause 2 above"*, so it can only be evaluated after
  clause 2.1 has produced V — and clause 2.1 cannot produce V for a literal
  outside the {primitive type definition}'s lexical space, which for the whole
  integer branch is `xs:decimal`'s `(\+|-)?([0-9]+(\.[0-9]*)?|\.[0-9]+)`
  (§3.3.3.1, no exponent, no `INF`/`-INF`/`NaN` productions). So `-1E4`, `INF`,
  `-INF`, `NaN` must reject via the **pattern** clause and can never reach the
  fractionDigits/bounds value-facet checks. The oracle also flagged `+0.0` as
  the sharpest trap in the family — a genuine decimal literal with zero fraction
  digits that a fractionDigits-only check would happily accept, and that *only*
  the pattern rejects. Grounding:
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/365#issuecomment-5160550387>.
- **The test answers that ruling directly, and it is why no #149 dismissal is
  owed.** `TestDatatypesLexicalIntXFamily` (`conformance/datatypes_test.go:889`)
  is three arguments in one: 18 of the 24 fixtures asserted at **both
  polarities** (the executor must also *Fail* against the inverted
  expectation); the routing premises pinned for both types (seeded, **not**
  directly mapped by `strict`, `strictGoverns` true up the base chain to
  `xs:decimal`, `fixesTimezone` false — so the generalized `!mapped` arm is
  demonstrably what routes them); and a nine-row `value.ValidateLexical`
  **rule-ID** table pinning `cvc-pattern-valid` for `-1E4`/`INF`/`-INF`/`NaN`/
  `ABCDEF`/`+0.0`/`""` against `cvc-maxInclusive-valid` and
  `cvc-minInclusive-valid` for the two `xs:int` overflow literals. That table is
  the #149-shaped "right answer, wrong reason" guard the issue asked for, and
  the arbiter mutation-proved it: changing `-1E4`'s expected rule to
  `cvc-fractionDigits-valid` fails with `rejection rule = "cvc-pattern-valid"
  (ok=true)`. A closing block asserts `xs:integer`'s unbounded arm from the
  **accepting** side — `2147483648`, `-2147483649` and integer011's 29-digit
  literal all valid — which is the one shape #331's 48 could not exercise.
- **The repair round: two P2 findings, both prose, both in one file.** Round 1
  (<https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/365#issuecomment-5160620605>)
  rejected with the gate green, the regex verified by a whole-tree match diff
  (`old count 299 | new count 323 | added 24 | removed 0`, and the arbiter
  separately confirmed the bare `int` alternative reaches neither
  `Facets/int/test111092.xml` nor `int.xsd` nor `integer001.xml`), the pins
  mutation-proved, and the lane measured — and rejected anyway on what the
  comments *claimed*:
  1. **`[P2 / acceptance §5]`** `conformance/datatypes.go:464-465` — *"Nothing
     under msData/datatypes is left unclaimed by the integer family now except
     the reader-shape limits named above"* is **false**. Twenty identically
     shaped fixtures remain unclaimed: `negativeInteger001–005`,
     `nonNegativeInteger001–005`, `nonPositiveInteger001–005`,
     `positiveInteger001–005`. The arbiter verified it three ways — the
     whole-tree regex diff reaches none of them; all 20 sit in `instance.txt` as
     `fail` and are absent from `datatypes.txt`, *precisely the status
     `int001`/`integer001` held before this diff*; and `positiveInteger001.xml`
     carries the byte-identical `comp_foo`/`simpleTest` shape. And they are
     integer-family members **by the file's own definitions** —
     `datatypes.go:159-161` enumerates all four, and `:527` says "twelve
     narrowings", which is only twelve if all four are counted.
  2. **`[P2]`** `conformance/datatypes.go:503-505` — the parenthetical *"the
     families the current checkout carries plain lexical cases for"* was true
     before this diff and is **falsified by it**, since `int` and `integer` now
     carry such cases too.
  The reasoning attached to finding 1 is the part worth preserving: *"This is
  not pedantry about a comment. **It is the same defect this issue exists to
  repair, one iteration later.** #365 exists because #331 shipped a wrong count
  in a comment; the fix for that undercount ships a larger, more confident
  overclaim in its place. And this one is worse than the original: an undercount
  leaves a reader curious, whereas 'nothing is left unclaimed' is precisely the
  sentence a future cartographer or post-land harvest pass reads to conclude
  there is no harvest here."* STYLE **P3a**'s own rationale names the failure
  mode verbatim — *"The unenumerated form … is not an acceptable claim, because
  it is exactly the sentence that gets written when only the consumers in view
  were checked."* The repair (`4fa2861`, **16+/4−, comment lines only**, a new
  commit rather than an amend so the round stays visible) replaced the sentence
  with the four sub-families by name, their file counts, their exact present
  status, their identical shape, and the explicit framing that claiming them is
  *"the same selector-only widening #365 performed … a harvest still on the
  table, not a limit of the route"*; finding 2's parenthetical was re-scoped to
  "the families issue #331 claimed".
- **Round 2 checked the repair for EXHAUSTIVENESS, not just accuracy** — which
  is the check that turns a P3a repair from a patch into a guarantee. The
  arbiter swept all thirteen integer-family members for plain `NNN.xml` lexical
  fixtures and cross-referenced `datatypes.txt` at the pre-ratchet baseline:
  `integer` 16 and `int` 8 (claimed by this diff), `long`/`short`/`byte` 9 each
  and the four `unsigned*` 7 each (all claimed by #331), and the four
  enumerated sub-families 5 each. **"Four sub-families, twenty files, and no
  fifth."** It also independently re-read both rewritten blocks whole and
  checked every falsifiable assertion in them, including that the surviving
  "the only cases the route genuinely cannot take are the reader-shape limits"
  sentence does **not** contradict the new 20-file paragraph (it resolves to the
  multi-element `Facets/int/test111092.xml` shape named 30 lines earlier).
  ACCEPT: <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/365#issuecomment-5160665999>.
- Decisions:
  1. **Widen the selector; change nothing else.** The issue was filed as a
     selector-only widening and stayed one. No new executor, no new arm in
     `decideLexicalByFacets`, no engine change — the `!mapped && strictGoverns`
     route #331 generalized already handled a family with no bounds, and
     proving that was more valuable than any code would have been.
  2. **Flip #331's two negative pins rather than delete them.** They exist to
     make this widening argue for itself; the arbiter's mutation check
     (reverting `datatypes.go` to `origin/main` and watching both rows fail) is
     the whole point of having planted them.
  3. **Correct #331's undercount in the code comments and in `docs/PLAN.md`,
     but NOT in `docs/LOG/2026-08.md:305`.** That line ("`int001–008` and
     `integer001–012`") is #331's historical session record in an **append-only**
     log; the arbiter explicitly declined to charge it, ruling that
     retroactively editing it would be worse than leaving it stale. The issue's
     "three prose sites" are satisfied by the two code comments plus PLAN.
  4. **The triple `pattern` entry in `xs:int`'s `EffectiveFacets()` is NOT
     STYLE D3 and was dismissed on the record.** Mason flagged it, the arbiter
     reproduced it and found it real — three `pattern "[\-+]?[0-9]+"` rows
     declared by `integer`, `long` and `int` — but each carries a **distinct
     `declaring` QName**, which is what `st-restrict-facets` §3.16.6.4's overlay
     walk produces and what error reporting attributes a facet to. Collapsing
     them would destroy information, not deduplicate it. Recorded so a later
     pass does not re-open it; same disposition as #331's
     `!mapped && !strictGoverns` note.
- Surprises:
  1. **The fix for an undercount shipped an overclaim, and the overclaim was
     worse.** #331 said 20 where the truth was 24 — a reader who checks finds
     four extra files. #365's first draft said "nothing is left unclaimed"
     where twenty files were — a reader who *trusts* it stops looking. The
     failure directions are opposite and the second one is silent, which is why
     P3a asks for enumerated identifiers rather than a summary sentence. A
     one-iteration recurrence of a defect class inside the very issue filed to
     repair it is the sharpest possible argument for that rule.
  2. **`integer013–016` existing at all was the discovery that set the count.**
     #331's comment named `integer001–012`; the four extra files carry the
     byte-identical `comp_foo`/`simpleTest` shape against `integer.xsd`, and
     they are exactly the `INF`/`-INF`/`NaN`/`ABCDEF` literals whose rejection
     reason the oracle's ordering ruling turned out to govern. The undercount
     and the interesting edge cases were the same four files.
  3. **The 24 newly-claimed cases stay listed in `instance.txt` as well**, and
     that is not a bookkeeping leak — #331's `byte001`/`long001`/`short001`/
     `unsignedByte001` sit in both files too. Established precedent, checked
     before accepting, no finding.
- Friction:
  1. **A new variant of the `golangci-lint` seam (#426), and it is a
     *worktree* variant.** Run from a linked worktree — which is exactly what
     the arbiter's throwaway ratchet measurement creates — the linter emitted
     two spurious `forbidigo` hits on `tools/fetchspecs` and `tools/spec2md`,
     because it printed paths as `../../../..//Users/.../tools/...` and the
     config's `^tools/` exclusion is **anchored**. From the primary checkout: 0
     issues. Neither file is touched by this diff. This is the third
     consecutive session paying a lint-invocation tax; #426 as filed covers the
     `PATH` half, not this anchoring half.
  2. **Both repair-round findings were about prose, in a diff whose code was
     right on the first pass** — the same shape as #417's and #253's rounds.
     Three sessions in a row have now spent a full arbiter round on what a
     correct change *said about itself* rather than on what it did. Left here
     because that is a pattern, not an incident, and the retro should see it.
- Next:
  1. **[the harvest, unfiled — for the orchestrator's post-land pass]** The
     **20** `negativeInteger`/`nonNegativeInteger`/`nonPositiveInteger`/
     `positiveInteger` `001–005` fixtures are a ready-made next issue of exactly
     this issue's shape: selector-only widening, no engine code, arbiter-verified
     as identical in shape and route, with the scope **already written into the
     code** at `conformance/datatypes.go:464-476`. The arbiter's exhaustiveness
     sweep also certifies there is no fifth sub-family, so the harvest is
     complete as scoped. It is not yet filed — this is an open ledger item.
  2. **[carried, unchanged]** #426 (the `golangci-lint` invocation seam, now
     with the worktree-anchoring variant above to add to it) is still filed and
     unowned; #430, #346, #336, #400, #347 and #270 remain undischarged from
     earlier sessions, as do the five deferred items in the #340 entry
     immediately above (the `attributedeclaration.go:113` illegal
     `InlineTypeDefinition` asymmetry and the owed oracle question among them,
     both still unfiled).
  3. **[recorded, not a defect]** The four residual `PDecimal` failures noted in
     the #340 entry are untouched by this landing and remain on unrelated
     mechanisms.

## 2026-08-02 — #346 — parser/xsd: §3.4.2.1 clause 1's `{assertions}` fold lands in the PRODUCER as one alternant-blind helper, which lets both "B.{assertions} is a prefix of T.{assertions}" clauses be charged and both `GAP(xsd)` markers be DELETED in the same commit — the pair had to move together or charging either alone would have false-rejected every valid derivation of a base carrying an assertion; the side effect worth the most is that resolving the restriction alternant's base component unconditionally MOVED a `src-resolve` clause 1.1 verdict from unpositioned finalize to a positioned producer rejection, and the arbiter accepted on round 1 with the ratchet flat in all six lanes

- Shipped on `wip/issue-346` as **one** mason commit — `b17d7e7` (`xsd,parser:
  fold the base type's {assertions} in the producer and charge both prefix
  clauses (#346)`) on top of `origin/main` @ `04e138a` — **12 files,
  +523/−127**: `xsd/assertionprefix.go` (+88, new), `parser/produce_complex.go`
  (+48/−28), `parser/produce_xpath.go` (+29/−4), `xsd/complexextension.go`
  (+30/−20), `xsd/complexderivation.go` (+21/−18), `xsd/complextype.go` (+9/−7),
  `conformance/schema.go` (+14/−15, **comment-only**), and five test files
  (`parser/produce_xpath_test.go` +92, `xsd/complexderivation_test.go` +69,
  `xsd/complexextension_test.go` +52/−14, `parser/produce_inlinecomplex_test.go`
  +38/−21, `xsd/resolve_test.go` +33). **No arbiter repair round — ACCEPT on
  round 1**, three non-blocking findings recorded rather than repaired.
- **Ratchet: UNCHANGED.** The arbiter's figures, copied verbatim from the FINAL
  verdict and not recomputed: *"unchanged — all six lanes (datatypes 1153,
  schema 15432, instance 26426, xpath 0, json 0, ber 0); `GOXSD_RATCHET=1`
  rewrote no expectations file (improved=0 regressed=0 new=0 vanished=0)"* —
  `git status` clean after the ratchet run, and the diff contains no
  `conformance/testdata/expectations/*` change at all. The three fixtures the
  issue body named as the false-reject risk were spot-checked individually with
  `GOXSD_CASE=…` and are unmoved: `Assert/assert_010`,
  `assertion/d4_3_15ii10` and `assertion/d4_3_15v10` all `pass` on the schema
  lane and `fail` on the instance lane (M6/M7, unrelated grounds). The arbiter
  ruled the flat lane **expected, not suspicious**: clause 5 and clause 1.7 hold
  **by construction** for every producer-mapped type precisely because the fold
  landed in the same commit, so this is a closed GAP with no currently-scored
  fixture sensitive to it. Also worth noting for the case-ID confusion this
  issue nearly created: the suite files live at
  `testdata/xsdtests/ibmData/{valid,instance_invalid}/D4_3_15/…` but the driver
  mints case IDs from the **group** name, so `assertion/…` (expectations) and
  `ibmData/D4_3_15/…` (on disk) name the same fixtures — both spellings in the
  issue body were right.
- **The producer half: one fold, one helper, never a branch.**
  `assertionsWithBase(base xsd.TypeDefinition, own []xsd.Assertion)`
  (`parser/produce_xpath.go`) prepends a resolved base's `{assertions}` to
  clause 2's own `<assert>` children, in document order. It is **alternant-blind
  by spec instruction, not by our choice** — §3.4.2.1's own Note says the base's
  members are there *"no matter which alternatives are chosen … `<simpleContent>`
  or `<complexContent>`, `<restriction>` or `<extension>`"* — so there is one
  helper for every call site and no `{derivation method}` switch anywhere.
  `produceSimpleContent` routes through it with the base it had already
  resolved; `produceComplexContent` now resolves the base **component**
  unconditionally on BOTH alternants (previously only extension needed one, for
  content-type merging) and `complexContentType` takes the resolved
  `xsd.TypeDefinition` instead of a `QName`. A simple base contributes nothing
  and the helper says why: a Simple Type Definition has no `{assertions}`
  property at all — its assertions are the §4.3.13 *facet*, which is not this
  property and is not inherited.
- **The unconditional base resolution moved a verdict, and the move is a strict
  improvement.** A dangling `base=` on `<complexContent><restriction>` used to
  be caught at finalize with `xsderr.Loc{}`; it is now caught by
  `resolveBaseType` at the new producer call site, under the **same rule and the
  same clause** (`src-resolve` §3.17.6.2 clause 1.1) but **positioned**, with
  real file:line:column. The arbiter verified nothing that previously validated
  now fails and nothing that previously failed now validates, and checked four
  candidate new failure modes and found none: a closing restriction chain now
  hits `buildComplexType`'s PRESENT-nil sentinel and charges `ct-props-correct`
  clause 3 *positioned* where finalize's `checkComplexBaseAcyclic` charged the
  same rule unpositioned; `producer.run` is fail-fast so no stale on-stack
  sentinel survives to fabricate a "circular complex type definition"; `AddType`
  still happens at each type's own document-order position, so a base built
  early to serve a restriction does not jump the queue (D1/D2 intact); and a
  simple-type base on the restriction alternant still folds nothing and is still
  charged `ct-props-correct` clause 2 at finalize. The one real behaviour delta
  is **error ordering** — for a schema with both a dangling base and a
  restriction-side content-model defect, `src-resolve` now wins. Deterministic,
  which is all D1 asks, and recorded here so a future diagnostic-ordering test
  is not surprised by it. The finalize arm is **retargeted, not deleted**: it
  stays reachable for `SchemaBuilder`-assembled components and
  `TestResolveAnonymousComplexTypeDanglingBase` now pins it, the
  two-entry-points-one-rule shape `buildComplexType`'s `ct-props-correct` clause
  3 rejection already had.
- **The consumer half: one relation, one encoding.** New
  `xsd/assertionprefix.go` decides `assertionsPrefix(base, derived)` once,
  because the two constraints state it in **byte-for-byte identical spec words**
  — the file's header says so and the arbiter confirmed it against the spec.
  `checkRestrictionAssertions` charges `derivation-ok-restriction` (§3.4.6.3)
  clause 5 and `checkExtensionAssertions` charges `cos-ct-extends` (§3.4.6.2)
  case 1 clause 1.7. Assertions compare on their one schema-significant
  property, `{test}`, field for field, with `{namespace bindings}` compared as
  the **unordered SET** §3.13.1 defines — an order-sensitive read would be a
  false reject, the one forbidden direction (PRINCIPLES 9). Both clauses are
  CHARGED rather than assumed even though the fold makes them hold by
  construction, on exactly the footing `checkExtensionAttributeUses` records for
  clause 1.2: they are the executable statement of the constraint for a
  component the `SchemaBuilder` assembled by other means, and the guard that
  turns a future fold regression into a verdict instead of into silence. Both
  `GAP(xsd)` markers are **DELETED, not narrowed** — the arbiter re-read both
  functions directly and confirmed `checkExtensionOfComplexBase` now calls 1.1
  through 1.7 in spec order and `checkDerivationOKRestriction` 1 through 5.
- **Spec, and the grounding's decisive contribution was a CITATION CORRECTION to
  the issue body.** The oracle found the issue's section numbers off by one
  against the local spec: `derivation-ok-restriction` is **§3.4.6.3**, not the
  §3.4.6.2 the issue said, and there is **no constraint literally named
  `derivation-ok-extension`** — the extension-side twin is `cos-ct-extends`
  §3.4.6.2 case 1 clause 1.7. Both corrections are what the code and commit
  message cite. The grounding also settled the two things the fold turns on:
  the mapping lives in §3.4.2.1 `dcl.ctd.common`, which applies *"Whichever
  alternative for the content of `<complexType>` is chosen"* (hence no
  branching), and the order is **base first, own children after** — the separate
  §3.4.2 note that *"the order among the assertions is not significant during
  assessment"* governs **evaluation** order only, never the property's
  structural order, which is precisely what a prefix test reads. Grounding:
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/346#issuecomment-5160731919>.
- **Tests, and the mutation proofs that make them count.** Five new test
  functions plus two rewritten ones. The arbiter applied and reverted each
  mutation: stubbing `assertionsWithBase` to `return own` fails all five
  sub-cases of `TestProduceComplexTypeAssertionsFoldBase` — and it fails
  *through* `derivation-ok-restriction` clause 5, which is direct executable
  proof that the fold and the check are a matched pair and that landing the
  check alone would have been the predicted false reject; short-circuiting
  `assertionsPrefix` at both sites fails the three rejecting sub-cases of
  `TestDerivationOKRestrictionClause5`, all of
  `TestDerivationOKRestrictionClause5DistinguishesTests` (the guard that catches
  a count-only comparison) and the three rejecting sub-cases of
  `TestCosCTExtendsClause17`; and `TestProduceComplexContentDanglingBase`
  discriminates against `origin/main`, where the named-type cases are answered
  by finalize with `xsderr.Loc{}` and fail the
  `strings.Contains(err, "mem://produce.xsd:")` assertion. **Exported surface:
  unchanged** — zero new exported identifiers, zero changed exported signatures,
  verified by diffing every top-level declaration in the non-test files; the
  eight added functions are all lowercase and the only signature change
  (`producer.complexContentType`) is on an unexported method.
- Decisions:
  1. **One helper, no alternant branch.** Not a style preference — §3.4.2.1's
     Note removes the possibility of a legitimate branch, so a `switch` on
     `{derivation method}` here would have been inventing a distinction the
     spec explicitly denies.
  2. **`produceImplicitContent` stays UNFOLDED, with the reason written down.**
     Its base is unconditionally xs:anyType, whose `{assertions}` is empty
     (§3.4.7), so the fold there is provably the identity. The arbiter did not
     take this on faith: it traced the literal `anyTypeName` through
     `seedAnyType` (`produce_complex.go:57-58`), which constructs xs:anyType
     with a `nil` assertions argument, and confirmed `builtComplex` holds that
     seed before any document is produced, so no lookup can find a different
     xs:anyType.
  3. **`t.Loc()` on the two new rejections stays, over the neighbours'
     `xsderr.Loc{}`.** STYLE **E3** is a rule and "match your neighbours" is
     not. `t.Loc()` is the `<complexType>`'s real position for a
     producer-mapped type and degrades to `xsderr.Loc{}` for a
     `SchemaBuilder`-assembled one, so it is never worse and usually strictly
     better — the mutation run printed `mem://produce.xsd:6:2:
     [derivation-ok-restriction] …`, a diagnostic no other check in either file
     can produce. The local inconsistency is **407 legacy `xsderr.Loc{}` sites
     across package `xsd`** — a package-wide cleanup for the steward, not this
     change's debt. Explicit arbiter instruction: **do not downgrade these
     two.**
  4. **`conformance/schema.go`'s extension declines stay declined.** The
     comments were rewritten because every reason the exclusion was *first*
     written is now discharged — `cos-ct-extends` implemented (#264) and all
     three §3.4.2 base folds its case-1 clauses read now done: 1.2 over
     `{attribute uses}` (#401), 1.3 over `{attribute wildcard}` (#265), 1.7
     over `{assertions}` (this issue). What remains is the **lane-widening
     measurement itself**, which is #336's change to make and to ratchet,
     exactly as the restriction path was admitted only once
     `derivation-ok-restriction` existed (#262/#263). Rewriting the prose
     without touching the predicate is what keeps this landing's lane honestly
     flat.
- Surprises:
  1. **The issue's own spec citations were wrong, and the ledger comments on the
     thread were right.** Two of the four thread comments predating
     implementation existed only to repoint `GAP(xsd)` markers that named
     **#265** for work that was this issue's — one of them observing that a
     marker citing an issue which has *explicitly disclaimed* it is worse than
     an unmarked gap, because STYLE P3's greppable-owner sweep reads it as
     owned. Both mis-pointing markers are now gone entirely, which discharges
     the repoint rather than performing it.
  2. **Widening base resolution to a second alternant turned out to be a
     diagnostic upgrade, not a risk.** The instinct going in was that resolving
     a component you did not previously need is the kind of change that
     manufactures new rejections; four checked candidates later, every verdict
     it touches keeps its rule and gains a position.
- Friction:
  1. **Line drift inside a single day, again.** The `GAP(xsd)` marker this issue
     owns is cited as `xsd/complexderivation.go:189` in one thread comment,
     `:203` in another written the same day, and `docs/PLAN.md` still carries
     the `:189` spelling for a line that no longer exists post-fix. The
     `/backlog` comment had already said it out loud — *"re-grep rather than
     trusting the number"* — which is the right instruction and also an
     admission that line-number citations in issue bodies decay faster than the
     issues do.
  2. **A documentation-only test shipped knowingly.**
     `TestProduceImplicitContentAssertionsUnfolded` passes with or without the
     fold routed through the implicit-content site, since xs:anyType's
     `{assertions}` is `nil` either way. Mason said so in the test's own doc
     comment, the arbiter confirmed it is sound and kept it as a control —
     but it is a test that cannot fail, and it is flagged so nobody later
     mistakes it for a guard.
- Next:
  1. **[unfiled advisory — for the post-land pass or the next `/retro` Part 2]**
     **[T4]** `xsd/assertionprefix.go:54` `xpathExpressionsIdentical` and `:78`
     `namespaceBindingsIdentical` are a **second encoding** of a comparison
     package `xsd` already has: `xsd/elementconsistent.go:378`
     `namespaceBindingsEquivalent` is the same set-membership comparison over
     the same property, and `typeAlternativesEquivalent` (`:332`) already
     compares two `XPathExpression`s on exactly the four fields the new helper
     compares — `key-equiv-ta` clauses 1-4 *are* `xpathExpressionsIdentical`.
     T4 permits parallel encodings when the commit message says why they must
     differ; this one does not mention `elementconsistent.go` at all. The
     natural unification is `typeAlternativesEquivalent` delegating clauses 1-4
     to `xpathExpressionsIdentical`. Non-blocking, on the disposition this
     project has consistently given T4 near-duplicates — **recommend a
     `kind/refactor` issue or a steward item.** Not yet filed; an open ledger
     item.
  2. **[unfiled advisory]** **[P2/prose]** `parser/produce_complex.go:206-209` —
     *"assertionsWithBase runs HERE, on every produced type"* is not literally
     true given the `produceImplicitContent` exemption decided above, and the
     sentence wraps with a dangling *"The"* on `:208`. Two lines of prose, no
     code implication; left unrepaired under an accept rather than spending a
     repair round on it.
  3. **[unfiled, named by the arbiter under Housekeeping]** `docs/PLAN.md` still
     lists **#346 as open** at lines 647, 797, 1166, 1362 and 1440-1443,
     including the stale `xsd/complexderivation.go:189` citation for a line the
     fix removed. Cartographer's to reconcile now that this closes; not a
     blocker for the land.
  4. **[carried, unchanged]** #336 (admitting the two extension forms to the
     conformance lane — now unblocked on the spec side, blocked only on the
     measurement), #413 (the Open Content marker, the third one mis-naming
     #265) and #414/#438 (the anonymous-complex-type reach of the two
     finalize-side folds, #438 depending on #414) all remain undischarged, as
     do #430, #400, #347, #270 and #426 from earlier sessions, plus the 20-file
     `negativeInteger`/`nonNegativeInteger`/`nonPositiveInteger`/
     `positiveInteger` harvest left unfiled by the #365 entry immediately above.
- Thread record (do not duplicate) —
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/346>: the SCOPE ADDITION from #264's
  post-land pass at
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/346#issuecomment-5154179701> (the
  second `GAP(xsd)` marker at `xsd/complexextension.go:111-134`, its mis-pointed
  `#265` citation, and the extension-side unit test it added to Acceptance), the
  `/backlog` marker-ownership note at
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/346#issuecomment-5158471496> (the
  "cites an issue that has explicitly disclaimed it" argument, the #344 → #361
  repoint precedent, and the deliberate non-merge of #413 and #414), the
  GROUNDING at
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/346#issuecomment-5160731919> (the
  §3.4.6.3 / §3.4.6.2-clause-1.7 citation correction, the alternant-blindness
  Note, the structural-vs-assessment order ruling, and the xs:anyType fold base
  case), and the round-1 ACCEPT at
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/346#issuecomment-5160859041> (the
  six-lane flat ratchet with a clean `git status` after the write-enabled run,
  the independent spec verification, the three judgment calls on the moved

## 2026-08-02 — #324 — parser: `xsdBool` becomes `facetFixed`, mapping a facet's `fixed=` through xs:boolean's ACTUAL VALUE — pre-lexical collapse first, then the four `booleanRep` literals, then a positioned `cvc-datatype-valid` for anything else instead of a silent `{fixed} = false`; the arbiter rejected round 1 because the first cut's `strings.TrimSpace` trims a WIDER character class than §4.3.6 collapse and therefore false-ACCEPTS `fixed="&#xA0;true"` — the exact defect class the issue exists to close, merely relocated — and the repair narrowing the cutset to the four XML whitespace characters is the whole of the difference between the two rounds

- Shipped on `wip/issue-324` as **two** mason commits on top of `origin/main` @
  `0d8c834` — `904026c` (`parser: map a facet's {fixed} through xs:boolean's
  actual value, not its raw lexical (#324)`) and the repair `6e11422` (`parser:
  collapse only XML whitespace chars in facetFixed, not full Unicode (#324)`) —
  **2 files, +132/−7**: `parser/produce.go` (+45/−7, the rewrite plus its doc
  comment) and `parser/produce_test.go` (+87, one helper and two new test
  functions). **One arbiter repair round: REJECT on round 1, ACCEPT on round 2**
  (repair round 1 of the 2-round cap; the branch did not come close to parking).
- **Ratchet: UNCHANGED, in BOTH rounds.** The arbiter's figures, copied verbatim
  from the round-2 verdict and not recomputed: *"unchanged (expectations
  byte-identical, md5 `7013f123076b369d7372139bbdf46839` before and after)"* —
  the write-enabled `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` run rewrote all six lane files and left
  `git status` clean, so it **wrote nothing**. Lanes: `datatypes` 1153, `schema`
  15432, `instance` 26426, `xpath`/`json`/`ber` 0. The round-1 run agreed
  independently: *"all six lanes byte-identical; 0 regressed, 0 improved"*. The
  arbiter ruled the flat lane **expected, not suspicious**, and gave the reason:
  this is a pure narrowing of the schema producer's acceptance, and no W3C suite
  fixture carries a padded or malformed `fixed=` on a facet at all. The issue
  body predicted exactly this and bounded the risk the same way — the defect's
  direction is an under-**rejection**, so closing it can only start rejecting
  genuinely invalid documents and can never regress a passing case by
  over-rejecting.
- **The defect, and why #219 is what made it matter.** `xsdBool`
  (`parser/produce.go:792` at `dbcfd16`) was five lines with two defects: it
  compared the **raw** `fixed=` attribute string against `"true"`/`"1"` with no
  pre-lexical whiteSpace stage, and its final `return` conflated *"the value is
  `false`"* with *"the value is not a boolean at all"*. Both are pre-existing and
  shared by all thirteen `{fixed}`-bearing facets, so this is **not** a defect
  #219 introduced — it is a defect #219 made **load-bearing**, because
  `xsd/derivation.go:542` `checkScaleFixed` is the only consumer of
  `Facet.Fixed()` outside tests and it reads the **base's** flag. A base
  `maxScale fixed=" true "` therefore silently disabled `f-ms-fixed`/`f-mns-fixed`
  — the very rules #219 first made reachable from `parser.Parse`.
- **The fix: two stages in the order §4.1.4 fixes, and a rename that stops the
  name lying.** `facetFixed(el *Element) (bool, error)` runs the pre-lexical stage
  (xs:boolean fixes `whiteSpace` to `collapse`, §3.3.2.3 / §4.3.6, and §4.1.4
  applies whiteSpace **before** lexical-space membership is tested) and only then
  matches `booleanRep ::= 'true' | 'false' | '1' | '0'` (§3.3.2.2)
  case-sensitively; anything outside those four is charged `cvc-datatype-valid`
  (§4.1.4) positioned on the facet element via `el.Loc()`, reusing the
  pre-existing `ruleDatatypeValid` constant rather than inventing a rule
  spelling. **Absent stays a distinct branch** — `(false, nil)`, the `{fixed} =
  false` default, never an error — which is the edge case the grounding flagged
  and the one place conflation would have been a false reject. The old name was
  the issue's own naming nit: `xsdBool` promised a generic xs:boolean reader while
  hardcoding one attribute (one fact, one encoding). **Exported surface:
  unchanged** — unexported → unexported, one call site (`produce.go:950`), no
  `go doc` delta.
- **Round 1 REJECT, and the finding is worth restating exactly because it is
  subtle.** Mason's first cut used `strings.TrimSpace`, on the argument — carried
  into the doc comment with clause citations attached — that trimming "decides
  that membership exactly as a full collapse would". It does not.
  `strings.TrimSpace` cuts on `unicode.IsSpace`, whose set includes **U+0085,
  U+00A0, U+1680, U+2000–U+200A, U+2028, U+2029, U+202F, U+205F, U+3000**. Every
  one of those is a legal XML 1.0 `Char` and §4.3.6 `collapse` **preserves all of
  them** — its whitespace set is exactly `#x9 #xA #xD #x20`. This repo already
  says so in its own words, at `value/whitespace.go:45` (*"leaving every other
  character — including other Unicode whitespace — untouched"*) and `:58` (*"It
  collapses ONLY #x20"*). The arbiter proved it **end-to-end through
  `parser.Produce`** rather than in the abstract:
  `<xs:totalDigits value="4" fixed="&#xA0;true"/>` on an `xs:decimal` restriction
  was ACCEPTED with no `cvc-datatype-valid`, as were the `&#x3000;`, `&#x85;` and
  `&#x2028;` variants. That is a false-**accept** — the exact defect class #324
  was filed to close, relocated from `"yes"` to `"&#xA0;true"` — and it failed the
  issue's own acceptance criterion *"and any other out-of-lexical-space value — is
  REJECTED"*. Because the direction is a false-accept it could not regress the
  ratchet, which is precisely why the gate was green through a genuinely wrong
  commit and why this was a one-line repair rather than a redesign.
- **Round 2 ACCEPT, verified independently rather than on the self-report.** The
  repair is `strings.Trim(lexical, "\x09\x0A\x0D\x20")` — the XML `S` set exactly
  — plus the doc comment corrected to carry the two-case proof that makes the
  shortcut honest: let `T` be the trimmed literal and `R` its collapse; if `T`
  holds interior XML whitespace then `R` holds a `#x20` and no `booleanRep`
  literal contains one, so **both reject**; otherwise `R == T` identically. So
  membership in the four-literal set coincides on every input, and the operative
  axis is the **character class**, not run-collapsing — which is where round 1's
  reasoning went wrong. The arbiter mutation-tested it itself: reverting the one
  line back to `strings.TrimSpace` fails **exactly and only** the two new
  `&#xA0;true` subtests (`…OutOfLexicalSpaceRejected/totalDigits/&#xA0;true` and
  `/maxScale/…`), so the new case is real, non-vacuous, and precisely targets the
  defect. It then drove a **wider Unicode probe** end-to-end through the real
  producer: U+0085, U+00A0, U+2028, U+2029, U+3000 and U+2003 all correctly
  **reject** with `cvc-datatype-valid`; U+000B and U+000C reject earlier at
  `[xml-wf]` "illegal character code" because they are not XML 1.0 `Char`s and
  never reach `facetFixed` at all. Notably U+0085/U+2028 are **not** silently
  line-end-normalized away by this reader before `facetFixed` sees them, so they
  genuinely exercise the fix.
- Decisions:
  1. **A four-character trim, not a route through `value` or `builtin/strict`.**
     The issue asked for this tradeoff to be **named rather than defaulted**, and
     the answer that survived two rounds is: a 4-character cutset `Trim` is **not
     a third private copy of the collapse algorithm** (STYLE T4), it stays a
     single comparison, and it adds no export — so STYLE 8's "export nothing
     without a consumer" is not strained either. The arbiter endorsed keeping the
     trim through the rejection: only the cutset was wrong, never the shape. The
     A2 advisory below is what would change that calculus, because once there are
     ≥ 2 consumers a shared `parser` helper — or an export from `value` — becomes
     arguable on its own terms.
  2. **Tests span two facet families, because the reader serves thirteen.** The
     `fixedFacetSchemas` helper renders one single-facet `<simpleType>` per
     family — `totalDigits` on `xs:decimal` (a Part-2 facet) and `maxScale` on
     `xs:precisionDecimal` (a scale facet) — as the acceptance criterion demanded,
     and it **returns a slice, not a map**, because subtest order is output
     (STYLE D2). The rejection test pins `Loc` to the facet element's own line 3,
     not the document's.
  3. **`ruleDatatypeValid`'s and `restrictionFacets`' doc comments stay as-is.**
     Both were considered and both were ruled non-stale by the arbiter: the
     former's lead sentence already covers the new site and its `notQName`
     mention is prefixed *"notably"* (an illustration, not an enumeration); the
     latter documents the error return's shape generically and the new charge does
     not change that shape.
- Surprises:
  1. **The wrong equivalence argument entered at GROUNDING, not at
     implementation.** The oracle's comment closed with an explicitly-labelled
     *"Design note carried into implementation (not spec, architecture)"* arguing
     that `strings.TrimSpace` is "behaviorally equivalent to full collapse+trim
     here", on the reasoning that no `booleanRep` literal contains whitespace.
     That sub-argument is **true**; the conclusion drawn from it is **false**,
     because the axis that matters is which characters get cut, not which runs get
     shrunk. Mason took the note (its final line said *"Final call left to
     mason/warden during implementation"*), the arbiter disproved it. Worth
     recording as a shape: a grounding comment that ventures past spec exegesis
     into architecture is carrying an unrefereed claim, and this one cost a repair
     round.
  2. **Mason cited `produce_complex.go` as existing precedent for `TrimSpace`, and
     the precedent turned out to be the bug.** The round-1 commit message says
     *"this is already the package's keyword-lexical idiom (produce_complex.go)"*
     — and the arbiter's answer was that those eight sites *"are precedent for the
     bug"*, which is how advisory A2 below got its scope.
- Friction:
  1. **`golangci-lint` was UNRUN in both containers, in both rounds.** The binary
     is not on `PATH` and is not a `go tool`, so gate part 2 could not be run by
     mason **or** by the arbiter, in either round; the arbiter refused to record
     it as passed and mitigated by hand (`gofmt -l parser/` clean, `go vet`
     passing — both in `.golangci.yml`'s enabled set) while flagging that the
     judgment linters (`revive`, `staticcheck`, `errorlint`, `nilerr`,
     `exhaustive`) were never exercised. CI is the authority on that leg. This is
     the second consecutive session to hit it; it is a `/retro` input, not a
     one-off.
  2. **A false-accept defect leaves a green gate, which is exactly why the arbiter
     had to reproduce it by hand.** Nothing in build/test/vet/conformance/ratchet
     could have caught round 1 — the whole point of an under-rejection is that no
     lane moves. The finding came out of the arbiter deliberately constructing
     `fixed="&#xA0;true"` and running it through `parser.Produce`. Cheap to say in
     hindsight; not automatable from the gate as it stands.
  3. **A stale claim survived into git history.** The corrected reasoning made it
     into the code comment on the repair, but `904026c`'s **commit message** still
     carries the disproved *"already the package's keyword-lexical idiom"*
     sentence. Since landing is a squash, the arbiter made dropping it an explicit
     land-gate condition (A3) — the squash message states `Ratchet: unchanged`
     plainly, replacing both commits' hedged *"arbiter to confirm"*.
- Next:
  1. **[unfiled advisory — A1, for the post-land pass]** `boolAttr`
     (`parser/produce_complex.go:2144`) has the **same class of defect with a
     wider blast radius and does zero normalization at all**:
     `return v == "true" || v == "1", true`. Eleven call sites
     (`mixed`/`abstract`/`nillable`/`inheritable`/`appliesToEmpty` among them), so
     every other xs:boolean-valued schema attribute still silently maps a padded
     `" true "` to **false** — the exact defect #324 just fixed for facet
     `{fixed}`, and the larger remaining hole. Pre-existing; **both mason and the
     arbiter correctly ruled it out of #324's scope** (the issue body names only
     `xsdBool`; one issue = one focused change = one commit). **NOT YET FILED — an
     open ledger item.**
  2. **[unfiled advisory — A2, for the post-land pass]** Eight
     `strings.TrimSpace`-based keyword-lexical comparison sites in
     `parser/produce_complex.go` — lines **934, 949, 1974, 1997, 2033, 2060, 2122,
     2128** (`mode`, `minOccurs`/`maxOccurs`, `unbounded`, `none`, `xs:allNNI`) —
     share the identical over-broad character class corrected here, in a different
     but related idiom, and are all likewise `whiteSpace = collapse` fixed.
     Pre-existing and out of scope. The arbiter's recommendation is that **A1 and
     A2 are one natural follow-up**, most likely a single tiny collapse-trim
     helper in `parser` that `facetFixed`, `boolAttr` and all eight keyword sites
     share. **NOT YET FILED — an open ledger item.**
  3. **Ledger discipline note.** Both advisories above are recorded here
     explicitly and by line number precisely so the post-land pass picks them up
     rather than letting them evaporate on the issue thread — which is the failure
     mode **#400** exists to fix. If the post-land pass files them, these two
     bullets are discharged; if it does not, they are `/retro` ledger leaks by
     definition.
  4. **[carried, unchanged]** #400, #430, #347, #270, #426, #336, #413, #414/#438
     and #417 all remain undischarged, as do the two unfiled T4/prose advisories
     from #346's entry above and the 20-file `negativeInteger`/`nonNegativeInteger`/
     `nonPositiveInteger`/`positiveInteger` harvest left unfiled by #365's.
- Thread record (do not duplicate) —
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/324>: the GROUNDING at
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/324#issuecomment-5160904137> (the
  `booleanRep` and `whiteSpace = collapse(fixed)` citations, the §4.1.4 ruling
  that there is *no fallback/default clause* so a silent `false` is a spec
  violation, the "actual value" `key-vv` term-of-art reading, the three-step
  collapse order, the case-sensitivity and absent-vs-invalid edge cases — and the
  architectural design note whose `TrimSpace` equivalence claim round 1 acted on
  and round 1's verdict disproved), the round-1 REJECT at
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/324#issuecomment-5160958614> (the
  `unicode.IsSpace` character-class enumeration, the repo's own
  `value/whitespace.go` testimony against the claim, the four-code-point

  handed over as the repair, the STYLE D3 finding on the doc comment carrying a
  wrong spec justification with citations attached, and the ruling that keeping a
  trim is still the right call), and the round-2 ACCEPT at

  byte-identical md5 both sides of the ratchet run, the independent mutation

  parity, advisories A1–A5, and the explicit reasoning for accepting rather than

  `checkScaleFixed` and whose arbiter follow-up (3) filed this issue). This entry
  is WORKFLOW step 6, written on the WIP branch after the FINAL verdict and before
  the PR is opened so it rides in the squash; the squash message carries `Spec:
  cvc-datatype-valid (§4.1.4), booleanRep §3.3.2.2, boolean whiteSpace =

## 2026-08-02 — #377 — conformance: `extraDocsInClosure`'s DECIDE path gains the `<xs:override>` arm it never had, closing the last of the three directives `closureScan.decidable` follows; the arbiter accepted on round 1 but CORRECTED the coverage claim mason's commit message makes — `<override>` was never unguarded outright, it was guarded at the WALK level by six pre-existing arms, and the one level genuinely bare was the executor decide path; and `golangci-lint` was actually RUN this session, breaking a two-session streak of an unverifiable gate part 2

- Shipped on `wip/issue-377` as one mason commit on top of `origin/main` @
  `9a26ac2` — `cba8dab` (`conformance: pin the decide path's <override> arm —
  the third directive compose walks (#377)`), **1 file, +16/−0, test-only**:
  `conformance/schema_closure_test.go` gains a fourth arm to
  `TestSchemaExecutorDecidesReachableExtraDocuments`. No production file
  touched, no behaviour changed, no exported identifier added. **Arbiter ACCEPT
  on round 1, no repair round consumed** — two non-blocking advisory findings,
  neither charged as repair work.
- **Ratchet: unchanged.** The arbiter's figure, copied verbatim from the verdict
  and not recomputed: **`RATCHET: unchanged`**. The mechanism it proved it by,
  also verbatim: it ran `GOXSD_RATCHET=1 go test ./conformance -run
  TestConformance -count=1`, the run *"passed, and `git status --porcelain` was
  **empty** afterward — the ratchet wrote nothing, so there is no lane movement
  in either direction and no unexplained upward flip."* No file under
  `conformance/testdata/expectations/` appears in the diff. The issue body
  predicted exactly this and gave the reason the arbiter then confirmed: this is
  a unit case over a **synthetic `writeSchemaTree` tree** and decides no suite
  fixture at all, so there is no lane it could move.
- **The gap, stated exactly.** `closureScan.decidable`
  (`conformance/schema_closure.go:150`) follows three directives and only three:
  its switch at `:163` sends `"include", "override"` to `compose` and `"import"`
  to `importDirective`. `TestSchemaExecutorDecidesReachableExtraDocuments`
  carried one arm each for `<include>` and `<import>` and **none** for
  `<override>` — so the arm whose omission #238's round-1 arbiter rejection
  called out **in prose** was the arm with no test behind it either. This issue
  was harvested from #238's post-land pass (PR #376, squash `5597192`) and
  recorded there as explicitly unfiled for a later pass; it is that pass paying
  out.
- **Spec: `src-override` §4.2.5 clauses 3.1.2 and 3.2.2**, and the grounding's
  contribution was to establish that `<include>` and `<override>` share ONE
  reachability relation while `<import>` does not. Both clauses read verbatim
  that the `<override>` in *Dnew* pointing at *Dold* *"is replaced by an
  `<include>` element pointing to Dold′ and the inclusion is handled as
  described in [§4.2.3]"* — 3.1.2 for the plain case, 3.2.2 identically for the
  chameleon case, so **both** override code paths terminate in §4.2.3's include
  machinery. That is why one `compose` method legitimately serves both, and it
  is textually distinct from `src-import` (§4.2.6), whose reachability rule is
  self-contained and delegates nowhere. The §4.2.5 `key-targetset` note
  corroborates independently: the target set is the transitive closure of the
  inclusion relation **union the override relation**, and *"does not include
  schema documents which are pointed to by `<import>` or `<redefine>`"*. The
  arbiter re-read all three against `docs/specs/md/xmlschema11-1.md` and found
  the comment's central claim exact. Grounding:
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/377#issuecomment-5161065605>.
- **The arm is load-bearing BY CONSTRUCTION, not merely by assertion.**
  `main.xsd`'s only directive is the `<xs:override schemaLocation="lib.xsd">` —
  there is no `<include>` fallback path to `lib.xsd` — and `main.xsd` declares an
  element whose type `tns:code` exists **only** in `lib.xsd`. So `expectValid:
  true` cannot hold unless the override actually composed `lib.xsd`. Both
  documents carry the same `urn:a` target namespace, which keeps src-override
  clause 2.1 satisfied so the 3.1.2 delegation **fires** rather than diverting
  into a namespace-mismatch path — that was edge case 1 of the grounding, and it
  was honoured. The override's single child names nothing in `lib.xsd` and is
  simply ignored; the point of the arm is the **reach**, not the substitution.
  The shared loop asserts the new arm under **both** polarities, identical
  treatment to the three existing arms, so a vacuous pass is not available to it.
- **Mutation-checked twice over — by mason, then independently by the arbiter in
  its own tree, both mutants applied and reverted.** Mutant 1, `case "include",
  "override"` → `case "include"`: fails `second document reached by the first's
  <override>` and, within this test, that arm alone. Mutant 2, `extraDocsInClosure`
  neutralised to `return false`: **all four** arms fail. Both as mason reported.
- **The arbiter's one CORRECTION to mason's framing — it does not change the
  verdict and it is the most important thing in this entry.** Mutant 1 is not
  *uniquely* killed by the new arm. With the test file reverted to `origin/main`
  and mutant 1 still applied, **six arms of `TestClosureScanWalksIncludedDocuments`
  plus `TestSchemaExecutorDeclinesUnresolvedDirectiveTarget` already fail**. So
  `<override>` was **not** unguarded before this change: it was guarded at the
  **walk** level (`closureScan.decidable`) and at the unresolved-target level.
  What was genuinely bare, and what the new arm now pins, is the **executor
  decide path** — `extraDocsInClosure` → `scan.visited`, i.e. that an extra
  *declared* document reached only through `<override>` is DECIDED rather than
  declined. That is real incremental coverage and it is literally what the issue
  asked for; the acceptance criterion ("the new arm must fail under either
  mutant") is met on its own terms. Recorded here in the arbiter's own words so
  that **nobody later reads the commit message as claiming `<override>` was
  previously untested outright**, which is not true and which the squash message
  should not be allowed to imply.
- Decisions:
  1. **One arm, mirroring the existing `<include>` arm's shape exactly, rather
     than a new test function.** The three existing arms already share a loop
     that asserts under both polarities; a fourth entry inherits that machinery
     for free and inherits its non-vacuity discipline with it. A separate
     function would have had to re-derive both.
  2. **The override child deliberately matches NOTHING in `lib.xsd`.** §F.2
     clause 1 is the only route by which an `<override>` child enters *D2′*, so
     a child whose precondition fails is ignored rather than an error — which
     keeps the arm about reachability and stops it accidentally becoming a
     component-substitution test. That reasoning is written into the arm's own
     comment, not left on the issue thread.
  3. **The two pre-existing prose omissions were NOT fixed inline.** Both mason
     and the arbiter ruled them out of a test-only diff (one issue = one focused
     change = one commit); they are advisory A2 below.
- Surprises:
  1. **The `golangci-lint` streak broke, and the workaround is worth copying.**
     The last two sessions (#324's Friction 1, explicitly flagged there as *"the
     second consecutive session to hit it; it is a `/retro` input, not a
     one-off"*) could not run gate part 2 at all because the binary is neither on
     `PATH` nor a `go tool`. This session ran it for real —
     `go run github.com/golangci/golangci-lint/v2/cmd/golangci-lint@v2.1.6 run`
     against the repo's own `.golangci.yml` — **0 issues**, by mason and again by
     the arbiter, which recorded it as *"genuinely verified, not waived"*. The
     judgment linters (`revive`, `staticcheck`, `errorlint`, `nilerr`,
     `exhaustive`) were therefore actually exercised for the first time in three
     sessions. **This is a process finding, not a #377 finding**: the `go run`
     pin is a one-line answer to a recurring blocker and belongs in WORKFLOW or
     ROUTINES rather than being rediscovered per session.
  2. **A test-only diff with no exported surface still had a real correction in
     it.** The STYLE sweep was empty (no error handling, no `else`, no switch, no
     logging, no map-ranged output — `writeSchemaTree` already sorts its keys, D1
     intact) and the exported-surface diff was empty, yet the round-1 verdict
     still carried a substantive re-framing of what the change proves. Coverage
     claims are assertions about the *absence* of guards elsewhere, and nothing
     in the gate checks those; only someone reverting the test file and re-running
     the mutant can.
- Friction:
  1. **`cba8dab`'s commit message cites `conformance/schema_closure.go:148` for

     forever because nobody re-checked it before the squash. Correct numbers for

- Next:

     cites *"(§F.2 clause 1)"* for what is a **no-match** outcome, and clause 1 is

     clause 2 is the no-match case for a *D2* child, and an *O1* child matching
     nothing at top level is covered by **no clause at all** (4.3 handles that only
     for a nested `<override>`); the comment's *substance* is right — the child is
     ignored, not an error, and clause 1 is the only route by which an `<override>`
     child enters *D2′*, so its precondition failing is exactly why nothing happens
